The request carries the attempt's id, the page call's trace, the bundle active at the call (`readActiveBundle`; K-08 default 1), the code's id, its keyed hash and the secret's name, the email row's id, and the row's code-built plan and final parts (`codeRowPlan`, `codeFinalParts`: below); never a person, a handle, an address, the code alone or a time. In one transaction, by the database's clock:
    1. **The contact's lock:** the attempt's `contact_hash` is read and K-22 rule 9's lock is taken on it, the same lock as item 58's, before the attempt is locked and before the account or the day's codes are read: one send per contact at a time, so two sends at once cannot both pass the cap (DL-546; T-322's security note b).
    2. **The attempt,** locked `FOR UPDATE`: none is `notFound` (table `sign_in_attempts`); one that names a code answers that send again, `repeated` (its code, its email row found by the attempt's id as its job id, K-04 v1.1 `outbox_rows_job_idx`, and its end), writing nothing and making no second code; one with no person answers `notSent`, `noPerson`; one whose `expires_at` is before now `notSent`, `attemptExpired`.
    3. **The address,** read for the attempt's person, never taken from the caller (K-22 rule 4): the person's `accounts.email` with `email_proven_at` set and its `handles` row of kind `email`; none answers `notSent`, `noProvenEmail`.
    4. **The day's codes:** `proof_code_limits(<that handle>, 'sign_in', now() - interval '24 hours', now())`: 5 codes or more to the handle in the window, every person and purpose, answer `notSent`, `dayCap`; a sign-in code of it paused answers `notSent`, `codePaused` (ADR-016 section 4, "after the answer").
    5. **The writes:** the `proof_codes` row (`sign_in`, the attempt's person, no business, that handle, channel `email`, the keyed hash and its secret's name, `sent_at` now, `expires_at` 10 minutes on); the email row with no turn (`job_id` the attempt's id, `turn_id` null, recipient that handle and the person, no business, booking, question or copy, channel `email`, `quick_reply`, `email_purpose` `code`, `not_after` the code's `expires_at`, `versions_read` empty, the request's bundle and trace), inserted `planned` at version 1, then its plan and final parts written and moved to `checked` at version 2, K-07's two moves (README lines 40 to 41 of K-07), so no row ever stands without final parts to stop its recipient's later rows (item 3); the attempt's `proof_code_id`; and the three entries below. The answer names the code, the row, its commit order, its version, the code's end and the three entry ids.

    **The entries** (ADR-016 section 1, "The entries"; K-08 v1, v1.1), written by the call itself from what it wrote, never handed in: each with the attempt's id as its job id, no turn, no bundle and no business, the request's trace, actor Rosie, zone null (UTC), nothing sealed, `why` `{"step": "S11-52"}`:

    | Entry                  | Kind                 | Old → new                                                                                                                                        |
    | ---------------------- | -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
    | `log-<row id>-created` | `outbox_row.created` | none → `state` planned, `channel` email, `send_class` quick_reply                                                                                |
    | `log-<row id>-planned` | `outbox_row.planned` | `state` planned → checked                                                                                                                        |
    | `log-<code id>-sent`   | `proof_code.sent`    | none → `person_id`, `purpose` sign_in, `sent_to_handle_id`, `channel` email, `sent_at`, `expires_at`, under K-22 v1's column names (default 123) |

    None holds the plan, the final parts, the code, its hash or the address (tool-api schema line 4065; K-08 README lines 179, 182). **The K-03 shape** (ADR-016's "Not measured" row): K-03 v1's `rowPlan` and `finalParts` hold a code row as they are, so K-03 needs no minor version: the plan is code-built, as a fixed line's row's is (CQ-177 item 128; `write-plan-alex-safety.json`), with channel email, no copy, formats `text` alone and no lines, question, page type or thread, since K-03 v1 has no slot for a code (K-09 open gap 25); the final parts are one `words` send holding the code's line, "Code NNNNNN: type it on the sign-in page. It works for 10 minutes." (K-12 `send-code.json`'s form; the stand-in for Jay's words, ADR-016 open point 5), with no calendar file, option, link, fixed line or slot (`codeRowPlan`, `codeFinalParts`; T-322's `actions/src/domain/proof-codes/words.ts`; default 116). The email port adds the subject, the automated-assistant line and the sender, as for every email row (K-12 README line 29).

60. **`proveSignIn`** (K-21 v1 `signInCheck`'s server side; ADR-016 section 4, "The check"; K-22 v1.2 rule 9's check). The request carries the page's attempt id, the device's sha256, the check's `requestId`, the typed code's keyed hash (made for every check, with or without an account) and the session a right code would start (its id and its token's sha256, the token 32 bytes from a cryptographically secure generator: card A); never the device cookie, the code, the token or a time. As `rosie_app`, in one transaction, by the database's clock, in K-22 rule 9's order:
    1. **The binding:** `sign_in_attempt(attemptId, deviceSha256, now())` locks the attempt; no row, whether the id is unknown, the device another or an argument null, answers `attemptNotFound` alone, the same for all, telling nothing of any attempt and writing nothing (K-21 README line 117; `refuse-prove-sign-in-other-device-told`).
    2. **The check's key and the look:** the call makes the key, sha256 of the attempt's `id`, the device's sha256 and `requestId`, a line feed between (K-22 rule 9, `last_check_sha256`), from the `id` the binding answered, which the page never learns; `sign_in_attempt_tried(id, key, NULL, now())` writes nothing, and a repeat answers its first result, `repeated`: a `signedIn` one as `alreadySignedIn`, with no token (K-21 README line 115).
    3. **The attempt's own state:** signed in answers `alreadySignedIn`; paused answers `paused` until its `paused_until`; past its `expires_at` answers `expired`; each recorded by `sign_in_attempt_tried` with that label, no try counted, before any code is checked. So a sixth check after five wrong ones answers `paused` from the server, cookie or none (STATE note 74).
    4. **The code:** `check_proof_code(proof_code_id, typedHash, now())`; a null `proof_code_id` (no account, or no code yet) answers `unknown` before any write.
    5. **`right`:** `rosie.person_id` is set to the attempt's person; `device_known(deviceSha256)`; an attempt whose person held a live owner or admin role at its start (`owner_or_admin`), who has had a session, live or ended (rule 5's select policy now shows the person's own), on a device `device_known` does not know, answers `secondProofNeeded`, recorded, and starts no session (K-21 README line 110); else the session is inserted under rule 5's insert policy (its id, the person, the token's sha256, the device's sha256, `proven_via` `code`, the attempt's code, `started_at` and `last_seen_at` now, `expires_at` 30 days on: ADR-016 open point 2), the check recorded `signedIn`, and `sign_in_attempt_signed_in(id, <session id>)`: `signedIn`, with the person, the session, its end and the code. **`used`** (the attempt's code used, no session naming it) answers `secondProofNeeded` again, recorded. **`wrong`, `paused`, `expired` and `unknown`:** one wrong try, `sign_in_attempt_tried(id, key, 'wrong', now())`, and the answer is the attempt's after it: `wrong` with 5 less its `wrong_tries` left, or at the fifth `paused` until its `paused_until`; the call then writes nothing more and commits, so the try counts whatever fails after (K-22 README line 212).

    **The null id's path departs from K-21 README line 116** (DL-526, from T-1250's round 2 security note b). K-21's line maps `check_proof_code`'s `unknown` to `attemptNotFound`; here a null code id's `unknown`, and DL-526's `expired` before a code's `sent_at`, are one wrong try of the attempt, answered from the attempt (`wrong` with its tries left, then `paused`), never `attemptNotFound`, so the five answers of `sign-in-wrong-five.json` are the same with or without an account (ADR-016 section 4; K-22 rule 9's check, step 5). In a sign-in check `attemptNotFound` comes from the binding alone (step 1). B2's builder (T-1285) follows this item, not K-21's line 116, for a sign-in check; that line stays as it is for a K-09 `proveCode` by a code id the server kept.

    The answer is K-21's label with what the server needs and the page never sees: the attempt's id (the job id of the check's entries), the person (for `personStanding`), the session, and the attempt's code (for `signInEntries`), never a hash. A session start with no person is refused (`refuse-prove-sign-in-session-no-person`). A session the database refuses (an id or a token hash taken, 23505) answers `constraint`, and nothing, the code's use included, was written.

61. **`endSession`** (K-21 v1 `signOut`'s server side; ADR-016 section 6). The request carries the session token's sha256 alone (`refuse-end-session-token-not-hash`). As `rosie_app`, in one transaction: `session_for_token(tokenSha256, now())`; no row (unknown, ended or expired) answers `signedOut`, `repeated`, naming nothing and changing nothing; else `rosie.person_id` is set to the person it answers, and `ended_at` = now, `ended_why` `signed_out` and the version raised are written on that session, which rule 5's update policy admits only as that person's own live session (K-22 rule 5), answering the session and the person for the `session.ended` entry. Ending another person's sessions (`left`, `handed_over`, `contact_changed`) is not this call's: the commit writes them on the runtime side (K-22 rule 5).

62. **`personStanding`** (K-21 v1 `signedIn.person`; ADR-016 section 6, "The answer's standing"). The request names the person `proveSignIn` answered. As `rosie_job`, in one read-only transaction that writes nothing: the person's live roles (K-04 `person_roles`, `ended_at` null, as `db/repo/people.ts` reads them), every business, in role id order, in K-21's names (`own_business` as `ownBusiness`), a customer role left out, since customers have no sign-in (FS line 701; `refuse-person-standing-customer-role`); `team`, a live K-36 `team_page_recipients` row (`removed_at` null); `tester`, by the rows v1.12's `readTesterStanding` reads (item 53, default 103), read by person, until T-1295's version widens both (open gap 62). The page call gives `tester` false outside staging (K-21 v1 schema line 853), and on `transient` answers `signedIn` with roles empty and both flags false, failing closed; each console's own call checks standing again (ADR-016 section 6).

63. **`signInEntries`** (ADR-016 section 6; K-08 v1.1 `proof_code.checked`, K-08 v1.2 `session.started` and `session.ended`). The request carries the job id, the page call's trace and what to log, a code or a session by its id, never a value: the call reads every value from the row's plain columns, so no caller can put a code, a hash, a token or an address in an entry (`refuse-sign-in-entries-caller-values`). As `rosie_job`, in one transaction, each entry `insert ... on conflict (id) do nothing`, so a repeat writes nothing and the call can run after the answer, and again after a lost one:

    | Asked                        | Entries the row calls for                                                                   | Id                                              | Old → new (K-22 v1's column names)                                                                        | Actor                                                                                           | `why`                       |
    | ---------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------- |
    | `proof_code.checked`, a code | one per change of the row not yet logged: each wrong try k, 1 to `wrong_tries`, and its use | `log-<code id>-wrong-<k>`; `log-<code id>-used` | `wrong_tries` k − 1 → k, and at k = 5 also `paused_until` null → its time; `used_at` null → its time      | Rosie for a wrong try (who typed it is not known); the code's person for its use, their own act | `{"step": "S11-52"}`        |
    | `session.started`, a session | its start                                                                                   | `log-<session id>-started`                      | none → `person_id`, `proven_via`, `proof_code_id`, `started_at`, `last_seen_at`, `expires_at` (K-08 v1.2) | the session's person                                                                            | `{"step": "S11-52"}`        |
    | `session.ended`, a session   | its end, once it has one                                                                    | `log-<session id>-ended`                        | `ended_at`, `ended_why` null → their values                                                               | the person for `signed_out`; Rosie for any other end                                            | null (K-08 v1.2 default 25) |

    Each entry has the request's job id and trace id, no turn, no bundle and no business (team only: K-08 defaults 16 and 22), zone null (UTC), nothing sealed, and its `at` the time it is written. **The job id** (CQ-258 (a)): the attempt's id for a check's entries, as for its code's (default 117); for a sign-out's `session.ended`, the sign-out's own K-21 `page.requestId`, since no attempt is part of it. **`secondProofNeeded`** (CQ-258 (b)): its code was used, so it writes the code's `proof_code.checked` (`log-<code id>-used`) and no session entry (K-08 v1.2 default 24); a later check answered `secondProofNeeded` again changes nothing and logs nothing new (default 118). Attempts get no entry: their own row is their record (ADR-016 section 6). An asked code or session that does not exist is `notFound`, and nothing is written.

64. **`claimOutbox`** (v1.2 item 30, with K-04 v1.1): each claimed row also carries **`emailPurpose`** and **`notAfter`**, `outbox_rows.email_purpose` and `not_after` as stored, null where the column is null, always written by a v1.13 claim; a `claimedRow` with neither is a v1 to v1.12 answer. As K-04 v1.1's CHECKs hold them, a purpose is only on an email row and a `code` row always has its end (`refuse-claimed-code-row-no-not-after`). Limit 8 withdraws a row at or after its `notAfter`, at every try (ADR-016 section 3; E1, T-1283), and the email port takes the row's K-12 purpose from `emailPurpose` (E2 and F, T-1286 and T-1288). The claimed row still carries no turn id and no job id (ADR-016 section 1's reader table). R0 (T-1279) builds it.

65. **`readOutboxRow`** (v1.3 item 35): **a row with no turn** (`turn_id` null, a job row) **answers `notFound`** (table `outbox_rows`) before any turn is read, as a safety row answers `fixedLineRow` first, so the read never reads its turn and never meets `answersOf`'s `BrokenRecordError` for it (ADR-016 section 1's reader table and open point 12). No v1.3 exchange changes: v1.3 never held such a row. R0 (T-1279) builds it, and D (T-1289), the first card that lets a job row be written outside a test, depends on R0, so no reader as deployed meets a job row unguarded. If the CTO rules K-04 v1.1's change major (CQ-248), this rule moves with it and nothing else does.

66. **`noTurn`** (ADR-016 section 3, "How a code row ends"): a code row is never given up, never `failed` and has no follow-up; it ends `sent`, `delivered`, `droppedStopped` or `withdrawn` at its `not_after`. `dispatcherGivesUp` (item 17: checked once the row is found and locked, before its claim, version or time), `writeAllSet` (item 25: as each listed row is locked, before its state) and `markPart`'s `failed` mark that carries `followUps` (item 15: before any part or row moves) write their rows and entries under the failed row's turn, which a row with no turn has none of, so each answers **`noTurn`** with the row and writes nothing, the failure included (`refuse-give-up-no-turn-wrote`). A `failed` mark with no `followUps` on such a row is marked as for any row, and its retry passes limit 8's `notAfter` first. None of the three is built yet (`db/repo/outbox.ts` names no give-up, "all set" or follow-up), so the refusal goes with the card that builds each.

### What v1.13 does not name

- **Way text and a link's sign-in that sends a code** (CQ-244; ADR-016 section 5): not served. `startSignIn` takes way `email` alone (`refuse-start-sign-in-text-way`), and a link start writes an attempt with no person and sends nothing.
- **The second proof's calls** (a `new_device` code to the owner's email, or the ID re-check: K-21 open gap 2): the attempt keeps the used code and its person; no tester can reach it (ADR-016 section 6).
- **CQ-44's general commit with no turn**: open; any call that carries it writes outbox rows in K-04 v1.1's shape. **A clock firing with no turn that writes an outbox row** stays refused (`refuse-fire-without-turn-writes-outbox`; CQ-28): v1.13 restates no `fireClock` definition.
- **T-1295's widened tester standing**, now v1.15 (items 68 and 69); **T-1155's arrival read, T-1177's outcome per answered label and T-1187's row reader**, and **CQ-211's note on `markPart`, CQ-234, CQ-235's note a and CQ-263** (item 56's role check, which T-1301 built): each at the next free number after T-1391's v1.16 (DL-506; v1.14 is T-1353's, DL-687; DL-754), since v1.13 keeps to its card (DL-526).
- **A delete job for attempts** (CQ-246; ADR-016 open point 10).

### Consumers of v1.13

Each moves to v1.13 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.12 consumers keep working.

| Role                  | Card                   | Components                                    | What it builds on v1.13                                                                                                                                                          |
| --------------------- | ---------------------- | --------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| database engineer     | R0, T-1279             | C-152                                         | `claimOutbox`'s `emailPurpose` and `notAfter`; `readOutboxRow`'s `notFound` for a row with no turn (items 64, 65)                                                                |
| database engineer     | R1, T-1280             | C-152, C-193                                  | `startSignIn` and `sendSignInCode` in `db/repo/sign-in-send.ts` (items 58, 59)                                                                                                   |
| database engineer     | R2, T-1284             | C-152, C-193                                  | `proveSignIn`, `endSession` and `personStanding` in `db/repo/sign-in-check.ts`, with `db/repo/scope.ts`'s person setter (items 60 to 62); `signInEntries` (item 63: open gap 68) |
| action-layer engineer | B1, T-1282             | C-193, C-095                                  | `signInStart`'s server side: the ids and hashes, `startSignIn`, the answer, then `sendSignInCode` with T-322's code, plan and final parts                                        |
| action-layer engineer | B2, T-1285             | C-193, C-095                                  | `signInCheck` and `signOut`'s server side: `proveSignIn`, `personStanding`, the answer and its token, then `signInEntries`; `endSession`; the null id's path of item 60          |
| runtime engineer      | E1, T-1283; E2, T-1286 | C-124, C-129                                  | limit 8's `notAfter` withdrawal; the email branch, which never asks a give-up, an "all set" or a follow-up of a row with no turn                                                 |
| runtime engineer      | F, T-1288; D, T-1289   | the composition root, for C-129, C-193, C-213 | the claim's two fields mapped through; the calls bound on each side's client, the hashing port, section 2's dispatch after `sendSignInCode`                                      |
| QA                    | T-1292                 | the contract tests, for C-152                 | the v1.13 fixtures against v1.13, each refused one mended into its twin, every earlier fixture against v1.13, and each exchange through db/repo's calls                          |

What the new tasks must hold to:

- **db/repo's calls** run items 58 to 66 as written, each in one transaction in its role, setting `rosie.business_id` empty and `rosie.person_id` only where the table above says, passing `now()` as every `at`, and answer each v1.13 fixture's outcome, each answer checked against its v1.13 definition in their tests. No statement returns `token_sha256`, `device_sha256`, `code_hash`, `contact_hash`, `request_sha256` or `last_check_sha256`, and no answer, error, span or log line holds a request's hash, the contact or the code's words; their tests check both. R1's tests hold the card's own cases (ADR-016 card R1) and two parallel sends of one contact at its cap giving one code; R2's hold card R2's, DL-526's race and a sixth check answered `paused` with and without an account. R0 adds `read-outbox-row-sign-in-code`'s noted rows to `db/repo/turn-face-reads.int.test.ts` (its `NOTED_ROWS`), so the read meets the job row itself: until then that test lists the fixture (T-1277, under DL-455's grant) and reads a row it has not written, which answers the same `notFound`.
- **The page calls** never send the device cookie, the code, the token or a time to K-05, make the attempt's two ids from at least 128 random bits each, map item 60's answers to K-21's labels with the token added only to `signedIn`, give the page no `personId`, `id` or code id, and follow item 60 for the null id's `unknown`.
- **The dispatcher and the wiring** map `emailPurpose` and `notAfter` field by field, read a missing field as a v1 to v1.12 claim (no purpose, no end), and never ask `dispatcherGivesUp`, `writeAllSet` or a follow-up of a row with no turn; a `noTurn` answer is a bug to log by the row's id alone.

## v1.14 (T-1353): the safety-only claim, which no other claim holds back

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): T-1352's two rulings, the architect's and the security reviewer's (`ops/cards/T-1352.md`), and the lead's choice between them (DL-687), carded as T-1353. A danger line's outbox row must reach Linq within 5 s of its text's arrival (arch 04 line 211), sent first: "Danger now: the safety message first" (RULES 4.12); "Any danger: the outcome step's checked message goes first, at any hour, past any opt-out" (arch 02 line 111); "its line goes out before anything else" (arch 04 line 201); an outbox row of send class safety "which the dispatcher sends first, within 5 s" (QUESTIONS.md NQ-RT-9). Under v1.13 no claim reaches it there: the walk of items 3 and 13 stops at a row claimed earlier and not finished and at a row with no final parts, and skips a recipient whose lock another claim holds, so a danger row written behind an outage row's live claim (T-1341's row S), a turn's planned rows, a failed row waiting for its retry, or ten sendable rows (the dispatcher's limit) waits for the recipient's next run. **Kind:** additive, a minor version, v1.14, the next free number after T-1277's v1.13 (DL-687); no CTO OK is needed and the 7-day rule does not apply (contract-change skill; both rulings' contract impact). `data-access.v1.14.schema.json` holds every v1.13 definition under its own name, by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.13.schema.json` (which refers on to every earlier version), all fourteen files as they are; it restates four, the rule of section "v1.1"'s **Kind**: `claimOutbox.request`, which takes the optional `sendClass`, with `claimOutbox.exchange`, which refers to it and holds a safety claim's answer to item 67, and `startSignIn.errors`, whose text names migration 0034's unique keys (CQ-292: v1.13's text named `sign_in_attempts_attempt_id_key` and `sign_in_attempts_request_sha256_key`, which no migration makes; no error is added or changed), with `startSignIn.exchange`, which refers to it. So every v1 to v1.13 exchange is a v1.14 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.14). **No schema change:** no state, move, table, column, grant, index or migration. The walk's filter reads K-04 v1's `outbox_rows.send_class` (`outbox_rows_send_class_known`), every move it makes is one of K-07 v1's (checked, held or sending to sending, by `claimOutbox`), and its claims are K-07's `outbox_claims` rows as item 13 writes them. **K-07 does not change:** its schema has no walk or send-order rule, and its README's line 82 describes K-05 v1's walk "as frozen", which stays true of it (open gap 74). **Not here:** db/repo's claim (T-1354); the danger wake, the dispatcher's guard and its safety-first order, and the outbox doubles (T-1352); the real port's bind (T-1356); a failed safety line's retry by this claim (T-1357); the safety rows to other recipients (T-1358); the contract test (T-1355).

### The call v1.14 changes

| Call                            | Item | Role and scope                                   | Repeating it                                                                                         | Time budget                                                                             |
| ------------------------------- | ---- | ------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `claimOutbox` with `sendClass`  | 67   | `rosie_job`, the runtime side, no business scope | Never hands a row twice: one claim per row by compare-and-set; a lost reply's rows lapse, as item 13 | Inside "Send ≤ 1 s" (arch 04 line 227): one claim, after at most one other claim's walk |
| `claimOutbox` without the field | 3    | as v1.13                                         | as v1.13                                                                                             | as v1.13                                                                                |

### How the safety claim works (K-04 v1 and v1.1, K-07 v1 as migrated)

67. **`claimOutbox` with `sendClass` `safety`**, the safety-only claim of a danger line's own wake (T-1352's rulings; DL-687). The request gains one optional field, `sendClass`, whose only value is `safety` (`refuse-claim-safety-other-class`), allowed only with `recipientHandleId` (`refuse-claim-safety-no-recipient`). Without it the call is v1.13's, unchanged (items 3, 13, 30 and 64). With it, in one transaction on the primary, uncached, as `rosie_job` on the runtime side:
    1. **The recipient's lock, waited for:** item 3's transaction-level advisory lock on the recipient's handle id, the same key, taken in its blocking form (`pg_advisory_xact_lock`, where every other claim takes `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock`), so the claim waits for another claim's walk of that recipient, one transaction, and never skips it (default 129).
    2. **The read:** under the lock, the recipient's open rows whose `send_class` is `safety`, in `commit_seq` order, `FOR UPDATE`. No row of another class is read, locked, passed, listed, waited for or counted toward `limit`, whatever its state or claim: an outage row sending under another run's live claim (T-1341's row S), a turn's reply, a planned or stale row, a held row, a failed try waiting for its retry.
    3. **The walk among safety rows**, in commit order, until `limit` rows are taken. **Claimed**, as items 3 and 13 claim a row (its state to `sending` by compare-and-set on its version, its version raised by one, a new part under `<row>/<part>/<try>` with try 1 and `attempts` 1, a part the provider never accepted under its stored key with `attempts` raised, its `outbox_claims` row written with `claimer`, `claimed_at` = `now`, `lapses_at` = `now` + 60 s and `claim_no` 1 or raised by one, `first_try_at` and `next_try_not_before` kept): a `checked` row; a `held` row whose `held_until` is at or before `now`; a `sending` row whose claim has lapsed (`lapses_at` at or before `now`) or that has no claim row (default 20). **Passed**, with nothing written for it, where the walk of items 3 and 13 stops or lists: a `sending` row with a live claim, which its claimer sends, once (default 130); a `waiting_chat` row, as item 13 passes it; a `planned` or `stale` row, with no final parts yet, which a later safety claim takes once they are written; a `held` row not yet due, not listed (default 131); and a row the claim cannot take as it stands (default 132). Finished rows are skipped, as in item 13.
    4. **The answer** is v1.13's `claimOutbox.response`: `claimed`, the safety rows taken, in commit order, at most `limit` of them, each of send class `safety` and the named recipient's, each stating its `claim`, `claimedFrom`, `port`, `emailPurpose` and `notAfter` (default 135); and `passedHeld`, always empty. `claimOutbox.exchange` holds the class, the five fields and the empty `passedHeld` (`refuse-safety-claim-takes-routine-row`, `refuse-safety-claim-lists-held-row`); the recipient and the limit are rules the schema cannot hold, which the database engineer's tests and QA's database side hold. An empty `claimed`, with no safety row to take, is an answer like any other, and `transient` may be sent again (the danger wake repeats it: T-1352).

    **One claim per row** (K-07: a `sending` row is "claimed by one dispatcher"): every claim takes a row only by compare-and-set on its version and its claim's `claim_no`, so no row is handed to two runs, whatever lock each holds. The safety claim never takes a row another claim holds live; an ordinary claim that meets a safety row the safety claim holds stops there, as item 13 says; a part that a run sends again after taking a lapsed claim goes under its stored key, which makes it one message (K-07 retry rule 1; K-11 v1). So a danger row has its own claim beside row S's, and two runs for one recipient may send at once, one of them on safety rows only (CQ-296).

    **Why** (the sources): RULES 4.12, arch 02 line 111, arch 04 lines 201 and 211 and NQ-RT-9 send a safety line first and within 5 s; item 3's commit order, from arch 02 line 125, yields for safety rows alone, and among them the walk passes a row it cannot claim rather than stopping there (DL-687; default 130). Reading safety rows alone keeps RULES 7.2: the danger wake is never gated, since a safety line goes past any opt-out (limit 8 sends it to a stopped phone with the override), so its claim must take nothing a STOP binds, and it takes safety rows alone (the security reviewer's ruling, "THE RUN"; `refuse-safety-claim-takes-routine-row`).

    **The one exception** to items 3 and 13: their walk stops at a row claimed earlier and not finished and at a row with no final parts, and skips a recipient whose lock another claim holds; a claim with `sendClass` does neither, for safety rows alone. Every other claim walks as they say.

### What v1.14 does not name

- **The send order inside a run** (safety rows first, then the rest, each group in commit order; LETS_LATER_PASS along that order) and **the dispatcher's guard** (a safety run never sends a claimed row of another class, so a store or a wrapper that drops the field fails closed): C-124's, T-1352 (T-1352's rulings, rule 3; the lead note DL-687). The claim answers in commit order, as every claim does.
- **The sweep** (no recipient) keeps v1.13's walk of every class: a safety claim names its row's recipient (default 134).
- **A failed safety line's retry** at K-07 rule 3's 10 s, not the claim's 60 s lapse: C-126's, T-1357, with this claim; v1.14 adds no retry rule.
- **K-07 README line 82** and the descriptions of "held" CQ-289 names: not restated here (open gaps 74 and 76).
- **CQ-293** (the sign-in send's locks), the points carded for "K-05's next minor version" (CQ-211, CQ-234, CQ-235's note a, CQ-263) and T-1155's, T-1177's and T-1187's reads: each at the next free number after T-1391's v1.16 (DL-754), since v1.14 keeps to its card (DL-687); since v1.17 (T-1418) and v1.18 (T-1421) are taken (DL-877), the next free number after T-1421's v1.18. T-1295's widened tester standing is v1.15 (items 68 and 69).

### Consumers of v1.14

Each moves to v1.14 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.13 consumers keep working.

| Role              | Card   | Components                      | What it builds on v1.14                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ----------------- | ------ | ------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| database engineer | T-1354 | C-152                           | `claimOutbox` with `sendClass` in `db/repo/outbox.ts` (item 67): the blocking lock, the class read, the walk that passes, the answer in v1.13's form; a claim without the field unchanged |
| runtime engineer  | T-1352 | C-124                           | the danger wake's run with `sendClass` `safety`, `DispatchOptions`' and `ClaimOutboxRequest`'s field, the guard, the safety-first order, and the outbox doubles answering item 67's walk  |
| runtime engineer  | T-1356 | the composition root, for C-124 | the real outbox port carries `sendClass` to db/repo's v1.14 claim and never drops it; a store that cannot carry it refuses the claim                                                      |
| runtime engineer  | T-1357 | C-126                           | a failed safety line's retry takes the safety claim                                                                                                                                       |
| QA                | T-1355 | the contract tests, for C-152   | the v1.14 fixtures against v1.14, each refused one mended into its twin, every earlier fixture against v1.14, and each exchange through db/repo's claim                                   |

What the new tasks must hold to:

- **db/repo's claim** (T-1354) runs item 67 as written and answers each v1.14 fixture's outcome after its given and the rows its note lists, each answer checked against v1.14's `claimOutbox.exchange`. Its tests also show on PostgreSQL what no fixture can: a recipient's lock held by an ordinary claim's open transaction is waited for, not skipped; two safety claims in parallel hand a row to one of them; a safety claim and an ordinary claim in parallel never both hold one row; a `waiting_chat`, a `stale` and a lapsed safety row, and a row it cannot take as it stands, each as item 67 says; and a claim without the field answers every v1.13 claim fixture as before.
- **The dispatcher and its doubles** (T-1352) send `sendClass` only with a recipient, never send a claimed row of another class in a safety run (a store fault, its claim left to lapse), and model item 67's walk in the outbox doubles: the class read, the pass, the wait, `passedHeld` empty.
- **The real port** (T-1356) maps the field across unchanged; a store or wrapper that cannot carry it refuses the claim rather than claim every class (T-1352's rulings, condition 3).

## v1.15 (T-1295): the human testers' standing, beside the team row

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): v1.12's open gap 62, as the lead ruled it (DL-535: reading (d)), with ADR-016 section 5. v1.12's `readTesterStanding` (item 53) and v1.13's `personStanding` (item 62) count as a tester only a live session's person who holds a live K-36 v1 team row (defaults 103 and 122), after K-21 v1 default 2 made staging's roster testers Rosie's team rows. ADR-016 rules that a human tester gets no K-36 row (section 5; build prompt 9.4, line 490: every alert to Rosie's team goes "to test phones or the test console, so no real person is alerted"), so under v1.12 the people who run staging's human tests answer `notTester`, failing closed, and the sign-in the user chose for them (DL-753, R5 (b): a code to their own test inbox) ends at a standing that admits no one. K-22 v1.3 (`../db-schema/people-access/README.md`, section "v1.3"; frozen with this version) lists them apart, in `staging_testers`, and this version reads that list beside the team row, answering with the same fields. **Kind:** additive, a minor version, v1.15, the next free number (DL-754: T-1391 takes the one after). `data-access.v1.15.schema.json` holds every v1.14 definition under its own name, by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.14.schema.json` (which refers on to every earlier version), all fifteen files as they are; it restates five, the rule of section "v1.1"'s **Kind**: `readTesterStanding.request` and `readTesterStanding.response` for their text alone (the rows a standing reads; no field added or changed), `personStanding.response` for `tester`'s text and the rule that a team member is a tester (team true never comes with tester false, as v1.13's `tester` was the team row itself: `refuse-person-standing-team-not-tester`), and `readTesterStanding.exchange` and `personStanding.exchange`, which refer to them. So every v1 to v1.14 exchange is a v1.15 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.15). Neither read gains a request or answer field. A person v1.12 and v1.13 answered keeps that answer, since no listing existed before K-22 v1.3; only a person with a live listing reads differently, `tester` where v1.12 said `notTester`, the opening gap 62 kept closed until ruled. **Schema change:** K-22 v1.3's `staging_testers`, which both reads read as `rosie_job` with its grants (K-22 rule 10); its migration is the database engineer's new task. No other table, column, grant or index. **Not here:** the migration and db/repo's two reads (section "Consumers of v1.15"); the testers' seed (T-1281); QA's contract tests; what "What v1.15 does not name" lists.

### The calls v1.15 changes

| Call                 | Item | Role and scope                                                                                    | Repeating it | Time budget                               |
| -------------------- | ---- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------ | ----------------------------------------- |
| `readTesterStanding` | 68   | as v1.12 (item 53): `rosie_job`, the runtime side, no business scope, read-only, primary uncached | Safe: a read | as v1.12: once per staging test-tool call |
| `personStanding`     | 69   | as v1.13 (item 62): `rosie_job`, the runtime side, read-only                                      | Safe: a read | as v1.13: once per `signedIn`             |

### How the v1.15 calls work (K-22 v1 and v1.3 and K-36 v1, as migrated once K-22 v1.3's migration runs)

68. **`readTesterStanding`** (item 53, widened; staging's test tools: `TesterAccess.standing`). The request is v1.12's: the session token's sha256 and the time, never the token, a person or a contact (`refuse-tester-standing-by-address`). In one read-only transaction:
    1. **The session:** as item 53's step 1, K-22 v1's `session_for_token(token_sha256, at)`; no row answers `noSession` (default 104).
    2. **The tester rows** of that person, in one statement: K-36 v1's `team_page_recipients` with `removed_at` null (item 53's step 2), or K-22 v1.3's `staging_testers` with `removed_at` null (`staging_testers_one_live_per_person` finds it). Either answers `tester`; neither answers `notTester` (default 136). Each list is read by `person_id` and `removed_at` alone.

    It answers the standing alone, as item 53 does: never the session, the person, a hash, which row stood, whether a listing or a team row ever existed, or when a listing ended (`refuse-tester-standing-ended-listing-told`; default 139). A listing that ended ends the standing at once, the person's session live or not (`read-tester-standing-listing-ended`; default 138). **Who is a tester on staging** (default 136): the person of a live session who holds a live team row (the test console's member: default 105) or a live listing (a human tester: K-22 v1.3, written by T-1281 from staging's inbox list; the user and Jay, DL-011). Every v1.12 exchange answers as before, since none holds a listing.

69. **`personStanding`** (item 62, widened). The request is v1.13's. Its roles and `team` are read as item 62 reads them; `tester` is true when the person holds a live K-36 row or a live K-22 v1.3 listing, read by person as item 68 reads them. So `team` stays the team row's alone (default 137): a human tester answers `team` false and `tester` true (`person-standing-human-tester`), a team member `team` true and `tester` true, never `tester` false (`refuse-person-standing-team-not-tester`), and a person whose listing ended both false (`person-standing-listing-ended`). The page call still gives `tester` false outside staging (K-21 v1 schema line 853) and fails closed on `transient` (item 62).

### What v1.15 does not name

- **A human tester and staging's team console** (open gap 77): K-21 v1 admits the team console's calls to a live K-36 row alone, so a listed tester uses the staging-only test calls and not `teamConsole`, `teamAcknowledgePage` or `teamDecideReviewItem`.
- **The check tester** stays the test console's team member (default 105; open gap 64, which a listing could now narrow).
- **Who ends a listing** when an inbox leaves staging's list (K-22 v1.3 open gaps): T-1281's, or a later seed card's.
- **T-1391's `readOutboxRowWritten`** (K-09 v1.10 open gap 147) at v1.16 (DL-754); T-1155's arrival read, T-1177's outcome per answered label, T-1187's row reader, CQ-211's note on `markPart`, CQ-234, CQ-235's note a, CQ-263, CQ-293 and CQ-289's descriptions (open gap 76): each at the next free number after it, since v1.15 keeps to its card (DL-535).

### Consumers of v1.15

Each moves to v1.15 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.14 consumers keep working.

| Role              | Card                       | Components                    | What it builds on v1.15                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ----------------- | -------------------------- | ----------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| database engineer | a new task (the migration) | C-158, C-154                  | K-22 v1.3's `staging_testers` as its rule 10 says, and v1.3's lists, seed and refused counts and cases in `db/repo/people-access.int.test.ts` and its class in `db/repo/rls.int.test.ts`, as T-1278 did for v1.2 |
| database engineer | a new task (the reads)     | C-152                         | items 68 and 69 in `db/repo/test-phone-reads.ts` (T-1300's `readTesterStanding`) and `db/repo/sign-in-check.ts` (T-1284's `personStanding`), with v1.15's exchanges in their tests                               |
| database engineer | T-1281 (ADR-016 card H)    | C-155, C-193                  | the testers' seed writes a live listing per named inbox's person, with no team row and no role (K-22 v1.3 rule 10, default 29)                                                                                   |
| runtime engineer  | T-1305 (P6b), T-1289 (D)   | C-205, C-193                  | nothing new: the standing and `personStanding`'s fields mapped as given                                                                                                                                          |
| QA                | a new task                 | the contract tests, for C-152 | the v1.15 fixtures against v1.15, each refused one mended into its twin, every earlier fixture against v1.15, and each exchange through db/repo's reads; K-22 v1.3's `db-schema.people-access.v1.3` test         |

What the new tasks must hold to:

- **The migration** builds K-22 v1.3 rule 10 exactly: the table, its partial unique index and CHECK, 0002's version trigger, row-level security on with the one runtime policy, `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE (removed_at, version)` to `rosie_job` and nothing to `rosie_app` or `PUBLIC`, and loads K-22 v1.3's seed with each refused row refused for its one reason.
- **db/repo's reads** run items 68 and 69 as written, each in one read-only transaction as `rosie_job` with no business scope, writing nothing, and answer each v1.15 fixture's outcome after the K-22 v1.3 seed its given names, each answer checked against its v1.15 definition. `readTesterStanding` reads `team_page_recipients` and `staging_testers` by `person_id` and `removed_at` alone after `session_for_token`, and is tested beyond the fixtures with a person who holds both rows (`tester`), an ended listing beside a live team row (`tester`), a listing ended a second before `at` (`notTester`), and a person with an ended listing and a later live one (`tester`); its tests keep item 53's checks (no statement names `token_sha256`, `device_sha256` or `code_hash`; no answer, error or log line holds the request's hash). `personStanding`'s `team` reads the team row alone.
- **T-1281** writes no team row and no role for a listed person, and its tests show it (K-22 v1.3 default 29).

**The integration tests on this branch** (DL-396; DL-334): `db/repo/people-access.int.test.ts`, `db/repo/availability.int.test.ts` and `db/repo/documents-licenses.int.test.ts` load every K-22 seed the index lists (and the first pins K-22's seed and refused counts), so they meet K-22 v1.3's seed before `staging_testers` exists; `db/repo/test-phone-reads.int.test.ts` (T-1300) runs every `readTesterStanding` exchange the index lists through v1.12's read and pins their list, so it meets v1.15's two. Each answers once the two database tasks above land, so this version merges with them in one train, as K-22 v1.2 merged with its migration (T-1278's lead note) and K-05 v1.6 and v1.8 with their reads (DL-396, DL-456). No test was changed here: neither failure is only a pinned list (DL-455's grant does not reach them).

## v1.16 (T-1391): a written row's recipient and send class, for a repeated safety record

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): K-09 v1.10's open gap 147 (`../tool-api/README.md`, "Open gaps, v1.10" and "Round 2"), from T-1373's round 2 (DL-751) after the action-layer engineer's round 1 FAIL, carded as T-1391 (DL-752). K-09 v1.10 default 175 has a repeated `recordSafetyOutcome` name the first record's rows, each with the recipient and send class its stored row holds, so the danger wake reaches each person told at once (RULES 4.12; T-1358's finding), since a safety run claims only its named recipient's safety rows (v1.14 item 67, default 134). No frozen K-05 call returns those for a safety row: `readOutboxRowWritten` (v1.4, item 41), the read `record.ts` `repeatAnswer` makes today, answers only `written`; `readOutboxRow` (v1.3, item 35) answers a row of send class safety with `fixedLineRow` alone (default 49); and v1.14 adds only the claim's `sendClass`. **Kind:** additive, a minor version, v1.16, the next free number after T-1295's v1.15 (DL-754); no CTO OK is needed and the 7-day rule does not apply. `data-access.v1.16.schema.json` holds every v1.15 definition under its own name, by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.15.schema.json` (which refers on to every earlier version), all sixteen files as they are; it restates two, the rule of section "v1.1"'s **Kind**: `readOutboxRowWritten.response`, which takes two optional fields, each of v1.4's five by reference to v1.4's own definition of it, and `readOutboxRowWritten.exchange`, which refers to it and to the new `readOutboxRowWritten.v1dot16Response`, the answer a v1.16 call gives with both fields required, as v1.6, v1.8 and v1.9 did. So every v1 to v1.15 exchange is a v1.16 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.16). **No schema change:** the two fields are K-04 v1's `outbox_rows.recipient_handle_id` and `send_class`, both `not null` on every row (0010), and `rosie_app` holds `SELECT` on the whole table (0012: a table grant, not a column list), so no grant, column, index or migration. **Not here:** db/repo's read and its tests (the database engineer, a new task); `SafetyReader` taking the fields and the repeat's answer filling K-09 v1.10's `safetyLine.recipientHandleId` and `safetyRows` (T-1374); the telling port naming its rows on a repeat (T-319); the actor's wake per recipient (T-1375); QA's contract test (a new task).

### The call v1.16 changes

| Call                   | Item | Role and scope                                                                                                                      | Repeating it | Time budget                                                                                        |
| ---------------------- | ---- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `readOutboxRowWritten` | 70   | as v1.4 (item 41): `rosie_app` on the call's own transaction under its business; with none, the runtime side, rows with no business | Safe: a read | as v1.4: inside "Gate and commit ≤ 300 ms" (arch 04 line 223) when a turn's commit runs the record |

### How the v1.16 call works (K-04 v1 as migrated)

70. **`readOutboxRowWritten`** (item 41, widened; `SafetyReader.outboxRowWritten`; K-09 v1.10 open gap 147). The request is v1.4's: the row's id and the call's business, always stated. The read runs as item 41 says: on the call's own transaction as `rosie_app`, under the business's scope, the business found first (`notFound` otherwise: default 56) and named in the read; with `businessId` null on the runtime side, rows with no business only (default 25). One row by its id per call, as v1.4's (default 143).
    - **Columns:** the row's `id`, `recipient_handle_id` and `send_class`, in one statement, and no other: never `plan` or `final_parts`, which hold the message's words (K-03 v1 `rowPlan`, `finalParts`; `refuse-outbox-row-written-returns-words` stays refused), and no `handles` row, so never the recipient's number or email address (default 142).
    - **The answer:** v1.4's five fields and two more, both stated on every v1.16 answer (`readOutboxRowWritten.v1dot16Response`): `recipientHandleId`, the stored `recipient_handle_id`, a K-08 v1 `handleRef` (`refuse-outbox-row-written-recipient-number`), and `sendClass`, the stored `send_class` (`routine`, `quick_reply`, `visit_now` or `safety`). `written` true gives both as the row stores them (`outbox-row-written-alex-safety-recipient`: Alex's emergency line, `handle-alex`, `safety`; `refuse-outbox-row-written-no-recipient`); `written` false gives both null, whether the row was never written (`outbox-row-written-not-yet-no-recipient`) or the scope does not show it (`outbox-row-written-other-business-no-recipient`; `refuse-outbox-row-written-other-business-recipient`), so nothing of a row the scope does not show reaches the caller, not even whom it goes to or its class (defaults 57 and 141).
    - **What a repeat reads with it** (K-09 v1.10 default 175 and its "Round 2"): `repeatAnswer` names each row the first record's commit wrote by its id (the line's from the event id, `ids.rowId("line", eventId)`, its email copy by `emailCopyId`, the alert and the page rows from the telling port's repeat), reads each once, and names a written one with the recipient and class this answer gives, as stored, never rebuilt from the reach card, the owner read or the record (default 140); a row answered false is not named. One read per row, for the handful of rows a record writes (gap 147).
    - **Every row a record names is seen under the call's business** (gap 147 asks this card to check it). The line's rows and its email copy: yes. The record's own commit writes them under its business: `record.ts` `lineRows` builds no line for a record with no business (`safetyLineNotBuilt`), and T-307's `outboxRows` gives each row the port's business (`actions/src/domain/messages-consent/messages-consent.ts` `rowOf`). The hand-off's alert and the team page's rows: not yet known. They are T-319's, and the telling port is not built (`ports.ts` `NOT_TOLD_YET`). K-36 v1 makes `hand_offs` business-scoped and each page to each person "a core outbox row (send class safety)", but no source names the business a page's row carries, and a row written with no business is seen under no business's scope (0012). So such a row answers false here, and a repeat could not name it (open gap 78).

### What v1.16 does not name

- **The telling port's rows** (T-319): which rows a hand-off and a page write, the business each is written under (open gap 78), and the ids a repeat names them by; K-09 v1.10 leaves them to the telling port ("Who reads v1.10").
- **The repeat's answer and the wake** (T-1374, T-1375): K-09 v1.10's `safetyLine.recipientHandleId` and `safetyRows` filled from this answer, and one wake per recipient.
- **T-1155's arrival read, T-1177's outcome per answered label, T-1187's row reader, CQ-211's note on `markPart`, CQ-234, CQ-235's note a, CQ-263, CQ-293 and CQ-289's descriptions** (open gap 76): each at the next free number after v1.16, since v1.16 keeps to its card (DL-752, DL-754); v1.17 is T-1418's, v1.18 T-1427's (DL-903) and the next T-1421's (DL-877, DL-903), so each now waits for the next free number after T-1421's version.

### Consumers of v1.16

Each moves to v1.16 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.15 consumers keep working.

| Role                  | Card                  | Components                    | What it builds on v1.16                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

The runtime engineer builds nothing of its own on v1.16: the danger wake reads K-09 v1.10's answer (T-1375), and staging's gate (`runtime/src/wiring/staging/tool-api-gate.ts`, T-1398) binds the seven safety reads as `pgSafetyReads` binds them, so it takes the widened read with T-1374's binding, the answer passed through as given.
