## v1.17 (T-1418): commitTurn names a phones row by its handle id, when the request asks

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): T-1417's NEED (`ops/cards/T-1417.md`, run 1) and the architect's ruling on it, QUESTIONS.md CQ-342, option (d) (DL-876), carded as T-1418 (DL-877). K-08 v1 names a phone row by its handle id, never its number, "so no raw number enters the log in plain or a trace" (`../event-trace/README.md` line 42 and its default 3), and K-09 v1's STOP answer names it so (`../tool-api/fixtures/stop-recorded.json`: `phones/handle-ben`). A K-05 row reference names a row by "the row's primary key as text" (v1 `rowRef.rowId`), which for a phones row is its number (K-04 v1 `phones.number`, migration 0004), so `commitTurn` answers a phone row it writes or finds changed by the number (v1.1's `commit-adds-dee-booking`, v1.2's `commit-alex-stop`, v1.8's `commit-alex-start`), and the action layer copies that answer into the turn's span and K-09's answer (`actions/src/commit/commit.ts` `spanRowsOf`, `writtenOf`). Once a STOP commits for real (T-1413, T-1412), the number would reach a trace. The ruling rejects renaming in the action layer, which knows a phones row only by its number, so the handle id would need a second read or a guess and every consumer would have to remember to rename (it fails open), and a new meaning for `rowRef.rowId`, which is breaking (a major version and the CTO's OK; 38 tests compare whole answers). It rules this: the request asks, and the commit, which already holds every row it answers, names it.

**Kind:** additive, a minor version, v1.17, the next free number after T-1391's v1.16 (DL-876, DL-877); no CTO OK is needed and the 7-day rule does not apply. `data-access.v1.17.schema.json` holds every v1.16 definition under its own name, by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.16.schema.json` (which refers on to every earlier version), all seventeen files as they are; it restates two, the rule of section "v1.1"'s **Kind**: `commitTurn.request`, which takes the optional `answerPhoneRowsBy`, each of v1.1's three fields by reference to v1.1's own definition of it (v1.2 to v1.16 keep them unchanged), and `commitTurn.exchange`, which refers to it, takes `call`, `given` and `outcome` by reference to v1.1's own, and holds the answer of a request that asks to item 71. It adds two: `phoneRowByHandleId`, one answered row reference narrowed, and `commitTurn.handleIdExchange`, `commitTurn.exchange` narrowed by `allOf` to a request that asks. So every v1 to v1.16 exchange is a v1.17 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.17): none carries the field, and without it the exchange is v1.16's. `rowRef`, `changedRow`, `rowId`'s description, every request field v1.16 holds, `commitTurn.response`, `commitTurn.errors`, `promotion` and every other call are unchanged. **No schema change:** the handle id is K-04 v1's `phones.handle_id` (not null, unique: `phones_handle_key`; tied to the handle whose address is the number by `phones_handle_fk`, 0004), read from the row the commit already locks or writes; no table, column, grant, index or migration. **Not here:** db/repo's answer (T-1417 round 2); the action layer setting the field (T-1419); QA's contract test (T-1420); `promoted`'s handle (T-1426; open gap 79); the flags and messages reads (T-1421, at the next free number after T-1427's v1.18: DL-903).

### The call v1.17 changes

`commitTurn` with `answerPhoneRowsBy` (item 71) is v1's call (item 1) in all but its answer: the same role and scope (`rosie_job`, per write), the same repeat (a repeated turn id answers its first commit, `written` and `promoted` empty) and the same time budget (inside "Gate and commit", 300 ms: arch 04 line 223), with no read added. Without the field it is v1.16's call, answer included.

### How the call answers when asked (K-04 v1 as migrated)

71. **`commitTurn` with `answerPhoneRowsBy` `handleId`** (the ruling on CQ-342, option (d)). The request gains one optional field, `answerPhoneRowsBy`, whose only value is `handleId`. Without it the call is v1.16's and its answer v1.16's, byte for byte (items 1, 18 and 19). With it the call runs exactly as item 1 says, one transaction on the primary, uncached, as `rosie_job` per write, and only what it answers changes:
    1. **Every row reference it answers names a phones row by its handle id**, `phones.handle_id`, and every other table's row by its primary key as text, as `rowRef` says: `committed.written` (a phones row a state write inserted or updated, `phones/<handle id>` with its version after the commit), `committed.waitingChat` (outbox rows, by item 19: nothing changes there) and `versionChanged`'s `changed` (a phones row read or written at a version that moved, `phones/<handle id>` with the version read and the version now). **`wrongState`'s `rowId` never names a phones row:** its table is `outbox_rows`, `outbox_parts` or `effects` (v1.1 `wrongStateError`, unchanged since), and `commitTurn` answers it only for an effect let go that is not in outage (item 18). `reservationLost`, `turnParked`, `constraint` and `transient` name reservations, the turn, a constraint and a SQLSTATE, and `outboxRows` names outbox rows, so none of them names a phones row (default 147). `promoted` keeps v1's `forPhoneNumber` (open gap 79).
    2. **The handle id is the row's own, read inside the commit, never by a second read** (default 145): a state update's from the row it locked `FOR UPDATE` before the compares, or its own `returning handle_id`; an insert's from its own `returning handle_id`; a changed row's from the row as it was locked for the compare (`FOR SHARE` or `FOR UPDATE`). Never a read outside the commit's transaction, never a lookup of `handles` by the number, and never an id built or guessed from the number.
    3. **A phones row with no handle id, or an empty one, throws** (default 146): a request this layer cannot carry out (db/repo's default 1), so nothing is written and nothing is answered, and the number is never answered in its place. K-04 holds `handle_id` not null, so a null one is a failed read; an empty one, which no CHECK refuses, fails the same way.
    4. **Inside the commit nothing changes.** The request names a phones row by its number, as every request does (`versionsRead`, a state update's `key`, an outbox row's and an open question's `versionsRead`), and the locks, the compares, the writes and the rows K-04 stores (`outbox_rows.versions_read`, `open_questions.versions_read`) keep it. **An answered phones rowId is a name, never a key:** no request accepts it. Passed back where a request names a phones row, a handle id names no row, and the call throws, writing nothing (db/repo's default 1), so it fails closed (the ruling's question 4: as built).
    5. **The schema holds the answer:** `commitTurn.exchange` holds a request that asks to `phoneRowByHandleId` in every row reference its outcome names, so an answered phones `rowId` is a K-08 v1 `handleRef` (its `safeId`: letters, digits and `_ . : / # -`, never a `+`, an `@`, a space or a run of seven or more digits and phone punctuation) and never has `phoneNumber`'s shape (schema `data-access.v1.schema.json` lines 71 to 76): no answered phones rowId is a number in any form (`refuse-commit-alex-stop-answers-number`; default 149). That the handle id is the row's own, and that every other table's rowId is its primary key, are the producer's tests' to show (section "Consumers of v1.17").

    **Why the request asks** (the ruling): the commit already holds each phones row it answers, so the handle id costs no read; the caller that asks is the one that copies the answer outward (the turn's span and K-09's answer, through `actions/src/commit/commit.ts`), and a caller that does not ask keeps v1.16's answer, so no frozen fixture, test or consumer moves until it opts in.

    **Why this name and one value** (default 148): `answerPhoneRowsBy` says that only the answer changes, never what the call reads or writes (requests keep the number), that it changes phones rows alone, and by what it names them; `handleId` is K-05's and K-08's name for a handle's id (`recipientHandleId`, `handleRef`) and K-04's column (`phones.handle_id`). It is the ruling's proposed name, kept. One value and no boolean: the field is either left out, v1.16's answer, or says the one thing it can, so each request has one spelling (`false` would be a second spelling of v1.16's).

### Who calls commitTurn, and whether it sets the field

- **Every turn's one commit** (C-087): K-09 `gateAndCommit` (`actions/src/commit/commit.ts`), its one K-05 request built by `actions/src/commit/build.ts` `buildCommitRequest`, the turn's core actions in it (a STOP's and a START's `recordStopOrStart`, `actions/src/commit/core`), sent through the runtime's gate port and its turn-row port (`runtime/src/wiring/root.ts`, `real.ts`; on staging `runtime/src/wiring/staging/k05-turn.ts`) or the action layer's own `pgCommitTurn` (`actions/src/commit/pg/commit-turn.ts`) to db/repo's `commitTurn`. **Sets the field on every request, from T-1419**, which merges after T-1417 round 2 (DL-877's order); until then it answers as v1.16. Each hop between `buildCommitRequest` and db/repo passes the request whole (the turn-row port: `calls.commitTurn(request)`), so the field needs no other change on the way.
- **Clock firings** (C-054): not `commitTurn` but K-05's `fireClock` (item 7; `db/repo/clocks.ts`), whose firing commits as item 1 does and answers `committed.written` the same way. **No field in v1.17:** `fireClock.request` is unchanged, so a firing that writes a phones row answers it by its number, as v1.16 does, until its own task (open gap 80).
- **The outage replay:** `runtime/src/outage/db/outage.ts` `replayStopOrStart` hands each STOP or START the actor took while PostgreSQL was down to K-09's `recordStopOrStart` made alone (`actorOnReturn`), committed through K-05's commit with no turn (CQ-44; open gap 16), which no version holds yet: `actions/src/domain/flags/handlers.ts` `NO_ALONE_COMMIT` answers `notBuilt`, and the `stopOrStart` port is closed on staging and in production (`runtime/src/wiring/picks.ts`). **No call to set it yet:** the version that adds the commit with no turn states its answer's phones rows (open gap 81).
- **The test rig's seeds** (`runtime/src/wiring/staging/send.seed.ts`, `tool-api-rows.seed.ts`): db/repo's `commitTurn` with v1's, v1.1's and v1.3's fixtures' requests (`commit-opens-question`, `commit-alex-safety`, `commit-s1-01-sam`) to seed a test database. **No field:** they write no phones row, and no span reads their answers.
- **The tests:** `db/repo/*.int.test.ts` and `tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` run v1 to v1.16's exchanges, unchanged. The v1.17 exchanges join `db/repo/commit-turn.int.test.ts` with T-1417 round 2 and the contract test with T-1420.

### What v1.17 does not name

- **`promoted`'s `forPhoneNumber`** (open gap 79; T-1426), **a firing's answer** (open gap 80) and **a commit with no turn** (open gap 81).
- **`versions_read` at rest and the reads that return it** (open gap 82; the ruling's question 3: as built).
- **T-1155's arrival read, T-1177's outcome per answered label, T-1187's row reader, CQ-211's note on `markPart`, CQ-234, CQ-235's note a, CQ-263, CQ-293 and CQ-289's descriptions** (open gap 76), and the flags and messages reads (T-1421): v1.18 is T-1427's catalog reads (DL-903), T-1421 takes the next free number after it (DL-877, DL-903), and the rest wait for the one after T-1421's, since v1.17 keeps to its card (DL-876, DL-877).

### Consumers of v1.17

Each moves to v1.17 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.16 consumers keep working.

- **Database engineer**, the producer: C-152, T-1417 round 2 (`db/repo/commit-turn.ts` and `db/repo/commit-turn.int.test.ts`, on b321c77): item 71 when the request carries the field, v1.16's answer when it does not.
- **Action-layer engineer:** C-087, T-1419 (`actions/src/commit/build.ts`, `commit.ts` and their tests): `buildCommitRequest` sets the field on every turn commit, so the span's rows (`spanRowsOf`), the `stillChanged` rows and K-09's `written` name a phones row by its handle id.
- **QA:** T-1420, the contract tests, for C-152: the v1.17 fixtures against v1.17, the refused one mended into its twin, every earlier fixture against v1.17, then each exchange through db/repo's `commitTurn` after round 2.

What the new tasks must hold to:

- **T-1417** (round 2) applies b321c77's answer (`answerColumn`, `answeredRowId`, the `returning` columns) only when the request carries the field, so every earlier commit exchange answers as before and the 38 tests that compare with them pass unchanged; answers each v1.17 fixture's outcome after its given, each answer and exchange valid at v1.17's `commitTurn.handleIdExchange`; moves its five new tests onto the v1.17 twins and adds `commit-alex-phone-changed-handle-id`; keeps the throw for a missing or empty handle id, with nothing written and no number in what it throws; and shows what no fixture can: a stale write of a phones row (an update whose `expectVersion` moved) answers `changed` by its handle id, and a request that passes an answered handle id back as a phones `rowId` throws and writes nothing.
- **T-1419** sets the field on every request `buildCommitRequest` builds, never on some; reads `written` and `changed` as names, never as keys (no request it builds takes a rowId from an answer: `versionsRead` comes from the turn's reads); keeps a STOP's phones row in K-09's `written`, named by its handle id as K-09 v1's `stop-recorded.json` shows (`phones/handle-ben`), now that the answer names that row by its handle id where `rowsNamedBy` names it by its key, so `writtenOf`'s core filter no longer matches it (the ruling: the frozen fixture wins over `commit.ts` default 9); and tests that no number-shaped id reaches the span, K-09's answer or a `CommitRefusedError` message.
- **T-1420** lists the refused fixture's mend (its one fault `outcome.committed.written.0.rowId`; put right, `commit-alex-stop-handle-id`'s outcome) and adds v1.17 to the fixture part's versions and, after round 2, to the database side's.

**The integration tests on this branch:** none meets a v1.17 fixture. Each loop over the commit exchanges selects `commitTurn.exchange` by its `def` (`db/repo/commit-turn.int.test.ts` `COMMIT_ENTRIES`, line 92, which lines 556 and 580 pin and check), and the v1.17 fixtures are listed under `commitTurn.handleIdExchange`; the other files select by `def` or by version (`tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` `DB_SIDE_VERSIONS` lists no v1.17), and no earlier fixture's `after` names a v1.17 one. `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts` checks every fixture of every version against its schema, so `npm test` checks the five.

## v1.18 (T-1427): the catalog's five reads, under the call's business, with row versions

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): T-320's run 1 NEED (`ops/cards/T-320.md`, "Run 1's builder report": the five reads, word for word) and the lead's ruling on it (DL-888: T-320 builds now with its reads as a port that answers `notBuilt` until bound; the reads become K-05 calls in T-1427, db/repo's in T-1428, the binding in T-1429), carded by DL-888 and launched by DL-903, with T-320's open questions CQ-349 (1) to (4) put to this card (DL-897, DL-903). T-320's catalog and pricing service (`actions/src/domain/catalog`, on main since 497256b) reads a business's kinds of work, services, ways, price rules and a terms snapshot's sources through its `CatalogReads` port (`reads.ts`; the row shapes in `types.ts`), and no K-05 call lists a K-23, K-24, K-25, K-26 or K-30 row: v1.2's `readTurnState` reads one column of a row whose id the caller already has (item 26), and db/repo serves its facts from K-04's core tables alone (`db/repo/turn-state.ts` `FACT_HOMES`, lines 185 to 192); v1.6's `businessSensitive` reads `company_kinds`' two switch lists inside `readOutboxRow` alone (item 45). So every call of the service answers `notBuilt`. Limit 2's owner rules have the same gap (`actions/src/gate/limit-2/facts.ts` lines 9 to 12: "db/repo has no read of K-26's rules yet"). **Kind:** additive, a minor version, v1.18, the next free number after T-1418's v1.17 (DL-903; T-1421's flags and messages reads take the next free number after it); no CTO OK is needed and the 7-day rule does not apply. `data-access.v1.18.schema.json` holds every v1.17 definition under its own name, by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.17.schema.json` (which refers on to every earlier version), all eighteen files as they are, and restates none; it adds the five reads' requests, answers, errors and exchanges and the row shapes they answer. So every v1 to v1.17 exchange is a v1.18 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.18). **No schema change:** the reads read K-23 v1's `business_setup`, `company_kinds` and `flow_defaults` (0019), K-24 v1's `kind_flows` (0021), K-25 v1's `services`, `service_money`, `price_rules` and `price_rule_ties` (0022) and K-26 v1's `rules` and `price_ok_limits` (0023), as migrated, with the `SELECT` grants `rosie_app` holds on each (K-24 rule 2: the Tool API roles may read the shared catalog); no table, column, grant, index or migration. **Not here:** db/repo's reads (T-1428); the binding (T-1429); a write of any kind (K-05 v1's `stateWrite` carries the catalog's inserts and updates, and T-667 lets `commitTurn` take the setup tables with item 77's names); QA's contract test (a new task); the cross-row checks CQ-349 (4) names (a new card: section "CQ-349's rulings").

### The calls v1.18 adds

Each is a list read on the call's own transaction under its business, as the Tool API's role, `rosie_app`, with `rosie.business_id` set to the request's business and confirmed by `requireBusinessScope()` before the first read (T-1428's card). Every table it reads is business-scoped and `rosie_app` holds `SELECT` on it, but for K-24's `kind_flows`, the shared catalog every role may read (K-24 rule 2), so no read needs a table only the runtime side holds. Repeating one is safe (a read). No source gives a time budget (journey 1's and journey 11's calls), save that read 5, when limit 2 reads it at the gate, falls inside "Gate and commit ≤ 300 ms" (arch 04 line 223).

- **`readBusinessKinds`** (item 72): the business's kinds of work, K-23 `company_kinds` with each kind's K-24 `kind_flows` rows, and the business's K-23 `business_setup.no_money`; serves `CatalogReads.kindsOf`.
- **`readBusinessServices`** (item 73): the business's K-25 `services`; serves `CatalogReads.servicesOf`.
- **`readServiceWays`** (item 74): one service's K-25 `service_money` rows; serves `CatalogReads.waysOf`.
- **`readServicePriceRules`** (item 75): one service's K-25 `price_rules` in effect at or after a time, with their conditions, and its `price_rule_ties`; serves `CatalogReads.priceRulesOf`.
- **`readTermsSources`** (item 76): the business's K-23 `flow_defaults` and its K-26 `rules` and `price_ok_limits` in force at a time; serves `CatalogReads.termsSourcesOf` and limit 2's `OwnerRules.rulesOf`.

Every call reads PostgreSQL's **primary, uncached** ("Decision reads go to the primary, uncached": arch 02 line 117; arch 10 lines 504, 538), in one read-only transaction, so every row and version it answers is one moment's. **Each request names one business, the call's own, never null** (`refuse-business-kinds-no-business`): a business's catalog is that business's, and with no scope the read would run on the runtime side over every business (default 154). **The business first:** each call finds the business in its scope (`businesses` by id) and answers `notFound` (table `businesses`) when it does not exist or the scope does not show it, so an empty list always means none in this business, never no such business (`read-business-kinds-no-business-row`; v1.4 default 56's rule). Besides the scope, every read of a business-scoped table names the business, as items 26 and 34 to 43 do, so even a role that row-level security does not bind sees no other business's row; a row of another business is never answered, not even counted (`read-business-services-lee-home-services`). **Each row with its id and version** (limit 4), so a write that rests on it names the version it read: a row whose primary key is one column (`services`, `service_money`, `price_rules`, `price_rule_ties`, `rules`, `price_ok_limits`, and `business_setup` by its `business_id`) by that column, and a row whose key is two columns (`company_kinds`, `kind_flows`, `flow_defaults`) by item 77's name, answered as `rowId`. **Every column answered is named** in the items below, never `select *`, and every object v1.18 answers is closed (`additionalProperties` false), so the schema refuses an answer that carries more: no person's words, number or address (`refuse-business-kinds-setup-listing-phone`), no service's extras (`refuse-business-services-with-extras`) and no rule's words (`refuse-terms-sources-rule-with-words`). Ids are compared byte by byte (collate "C"), never parsed (CQ-09), and every call can answer `transient`. **Values** are given as T-320's catalog and limit 2's evaluator read them: a span (an interval) in whole minutes (default 156), a clock time as `HH:MM` (default 166), money in whole cents with its currency, an instant in UTC ending in `Z`, a day as `YYYY-MM-DD`, and arrays as stored.

### How each v1.18 call works (K-23 to K-26 v1 as migrated)

72. **`readBusinessKinds`** (`CatalogReads.kindsOf`: "the business's kinds of work: K-23 company_kinds of the business, each with K-24 kind_flows' combos per flow"). **Columns:** of `company_kinds`, every row of the business (`company_kinds_business_idx`): `company_id`, `kind_id`, `business_id`, `trade_name`, `pf_facts`, `pf_switches_a`, `pf_switches_b` and `version`, answered as `rowId` (item 77), `companyId`, `kindId`, `businessId`, `tradeName`, `facts`, `switchesA`, `switchesB` and `version`; of `kind_flows`, for each kind: `kind_id`, `flow`, `combos` and `version`, never `main_combo`, `switches_on`, `arrive`, `arrive_window_hours` or `cutoff`, answered in `flows` as `rowId`, `flow`, `combos` and `version`, flow A then B: two rows for a trade-file kind, none for a drafted kind not yet reviewed, which then offers no path (default 155); and of `business_setup`, the business's row's `business_id`, `no_money` and `version` alone, answered as `setup`, or null when the business has no setup row yet (CQ-349 (2); default 161). Never any other column of `business_setup`: not the listing's phone or email, the website, About us, the Stripe account, the terms or control (`refuse-business-kinds-setup-listing-phone`). **Order:** kinds by `(company_id, kind_id)`. **The mapping** to T-320's `BusinessKind` (T-1429), field by field: `companyId`, `kindId`, `businessId`, `tradeName`, `facts`, `switchesA`, `switchesB` and `version` as answered, and `paths.A` and `paths.B` each the `combos` of that flow's row in `flows`, or empty when there is none; `rowId` and the flows' names and versions are what a write that rests on them names (item 77). `setup` is the money-path check's (CQ-349 (2)), which T-320's service does not make yet (its default 2). `BusinessKind` has no field for `setup`, `rowId` or a flow's name and version, so T-1429's binding leaves them unread, and the new card ruling (4) names widens the port to take them. **Shown:** `read-business-kinds-lee-home-services` (two kinds, `setup` null), `read-business-kinds-barbershop` (one kind, `setup` with `noMoney` false at version 4), `read-business-kinds-yoga-studio-none` (no kind, `setup` null) and `read-business-kinds-no-business-row` (`notFound`).

73. **`readBusinessServices`** (`CatalogReads.servicesOf`). **Columns:** of `services`, every row of the business, offered or not (`services_business_idx`): `id`, `business_id`, `kind_id`, `name`, `price_kind`, `unit`, `length` (in whole minutes), `position`, `active` and `version`, answered as T-320's `ServiceRow` names them (`lengthMinutes`). Never `extras`, the profession file's words about the line, which no code reads by sub-key (K-25 README "Services"; `refuse-business-services-with-extras`). The name is the business's own word for its line, which the owner reads back and the booking page shows (audience customer, staff and owner), and T-320's `ServiceRow` carries it (default 162). **Order:** `(position, id)`. **Shown:** `read-business-services-barbershop` (the board's ten lines) and `read-business-services-lee-home-services` (its one; the barbershop's are never answered).

74. **`readServiceWays`** (`CatalogReads.waysOf`: "a service's ways (K-25 service_money), every one, offered or not"). **Columns:** of `service_money`, every row of the service under the business: `id`, `service_id`, `business_id`, `flow`, `place`, `location_id`, `path`, `deposit_cents`, `deposit_percent`, `fee_cents`, `currency`, `cutoff_before` (in whole minutes), `cutoff_day_before_at`, `tip_line`, `active` and `version`, every column the table has (none holds a person's words, number or address), answered as T-320's `WayRow` names them (`cutoffBeforeMinutes`, `cutoffDayBeforeAt`, `tipLine`). **A service the scope does not show answers an empty list, never `notFound`** (default 153): another business's service (`read-service-ways-other-business-service`), and one T-320's service built earlier in the same call and has not written yet, whose ways it adds itself (`service.ts` `waysNow`). **Order:** `id`. **Shown:** `read-service-ways-haircut` (two ways), `read-service-ways-skin-fade` (its own 2-hour free-cancel time as 120) and `read-service-ways-other-business-service` (empty).

75. **`readServicePriceRules`** (`CatalogReads.priceRulesOf`: "a service's price rules with their conditions in effect at or after `at` (a UTC instant: effective_to null or after it), and the service's price_rule_ties"). **Rules:** every `price_rules` row of the service under the business whose `effective_to` is null or after the request's `at` (default 157): the rule in effect at `at`, each set for later, and a price withdrawn before it started whose empty range closes after `at`, which a new rule must see so as not to overlap it; a rule that ended at or before `at` is not answered (`read-price-rules-haircut-after-raise`). **Columns:** `id`, `business_id`, `service_id`, `service_money_id`, `worker_resource_id`, `weekdays`, `from_time`, `until_time`, `price_cents`, `from_cents`, `currency`, `set_by_person_id`, `effective_from`, `effective_to` and `version`, answered as T-320's `PriceRuleRow` names them, the five conditions together in `conditions` (`PriceConditions`); `worker_resource_kind`, always `worker`, is not answered. **Ties:** every `price_rule_ties` row of the service, answered or not, with all its columns (`id`, `business_id`, `service_id`, `first_rule_id`, `second_rule_id`, `asked_at`, `winner_rule_id`, `answered_at`, `answered_by_person_id`, `version`), as T-320's `PriceTieRow`: the service asks a new tie once per pair (`tiesFor`), so it reads every pair already asked. A person is named by id alone, never by name. A service the scope does not show answers both lists empty (default 153). **Order:** each by `id`. **Shown:** `read-price-rules-haircut-before-raise` (the $35 closed at Nov 2, the $40 from Nov 2 and the $75 at home), `read-price-rules-haircut-after-raise` (the $35 left out), `read-price-rules-event-hours-andre` (a worker condition) and `read-price-rules-haircut-tie` (an evening price from `17:00` tied with the home price, unanswered; the database side writes the two rows its note lists, since no frozen seed holds a tie).

76. **`readTermsSources`** (`CatalogReads.termsSourcesOf`: "a terms snapshot's sources at `at`: K-23 flow_defaults of the flow, and the K-26 rules and price_ok_limits of the business, at least every row in force at `at`"; and limit 2's `OwnerRules.rulesOf`: "the business's K-26 v1 rules rows, read on the call's scope: at least every row in effect at `at`"). **The request names the business and the time, and no flow** (`refuse-terms-sources-caller-flow`; default 158): the read answers the business's `flow_defaults` in both flows, so one read serves a snapshot, which takes its flow's row (or none, when the business has no row for that flow), and limit 2, which has no flow. **Flow defaults:** of `flow_defaults`, `business_id`, `flow`, `cutoff_before` (in whole minutes), `cutoff_day_before_at` and `version`, flow A then B, answered as `rowId` (item 77), `businessId`, `flow`, `cutoffBeforeMinutes`, `cutoffDayBeforeAt` and `version`; never `arrive`, which the snapshot does not copy. **Rules and limits in force at `at`:** every `rules` and `price_ok_limits` row of the business whose `tstzrange(effective_from, effective_to, '[)')` holds `at`, as K-26's README reads a booking's rules, of every flow and kind of work (default 159): the snapshot keeps those whose scope covers the booking (`terms.ts` `inForce`), and limit 2's evaluator reads them all and applies those that govern the proposal. **Rules' columns** (default 160): `id`, `business_id`, `rule_kind`, `value_type`, `flow`, `kind_id`, `value_on`, `value_cents`, `value_currency`, `value_span` (in whole minutes), `value_count`, `value_dates` (its `from` and `until` days, `[from, until)`), `effective_from`, `effective_to` and `version`: limit 2's `RuleRow` with the version a snapshot names it at; never `words` (`refuse-terms-sources-rule-with-words`) and never `set_by_person_id`. The kind and the value are answered as stored, not checked against the registry, as the evaluator checks them (a kind it does not hold is refused, never ignored). **Limits' columns:** `id`, `business_id`, `flow`, `kind_id`, `effective_from`, `effective_to` and `version`, the scope and dates by which a snapshot names a limit (K-30 default 11); not its amounts (open gap 83). **Order:** each list by `id`. **The mapping** (T-1429): `termsSourcesOf(businessId, flow, at)` takes `flowDefault`, the row of that flow or null, and `rules` and `priceOkLimits`, each row's `id`, `businessId`, `flow`, `kindId`, `effectiveFrom`, `effectiveTo` and `version` (`ScopedRow`); `OwnerRules.rulesOf(businessId, at)` takes `rules`, each row as `RuleRow` without its version. **Shown:** `read-terms-sources-dee-skin-fade` (at a snapshot's `written_at`: exactly the four rows K-30's `seed-terms-snapshot` names, the no-show count not yet in force) and `read-terms-sources-after-no-show-count` (the count in force, the barber kind's flow B limit beside the flow A one).

77. **A row whose primary key is two columns** (QUESTIONS.md CQ-349 (1)). K-05 names a row by one `rowId`, "the row's primary key as text" (v1 `rowRef`), which K-02's facts, K-04's `versions_read` and every commit answer use, and K-05 v1 gave no text form to a key of two columns, so `db/repo/commit-turn.ts` `oneKey` refuses such a table, for a version read and a write alike. **The ruling:** such a row's `rowId` is its key's values as text, in the primary key's column order, joined by a slash (default 163): `company_kinds` as `company id/kind id` (`company-lee-home-services/plumber`), `kind_flows` as `kind id/flow` (`plumber/B`) and `flow_defaults` as `business id/flow` (`biz-lees-barbershop/A`). Every column but the first of each key holds no slash by its own CHECK (`company_kinds_kind_id_form`, `kind_flows_flow_known`, `flow_defaults_flow_known`), so no two rows share a name, whatever the first column holds. **The database builds the name, and nobody parses it:** a read answers it from the row's own columns (`company_id || '/' || kind_id`), never built by the caller, and a commit finds the row a `rowId` names by comparing the same expression with it as a whole, never by splitting the `rowId` (CQ-09: an id is compared, never parsed). The kind's id alone names no row (`refuse-business-kinds-row-by-kind-id`). It is the name a later write's version read gives the row (`versionsRead`) and the name a commit answers it by (`committed.written`, `versionChanged`'s `changed`). **A write names the row by its key, column to value**, as K-05 v1's `stateWrite` already allows ("The row's primary key, column to value"): `placeKind` inserts a `company_kinds` row (`values` with both columns), and `updateKind` updates one with `key` `{"company_id": ..., "kind_id": ...}` and its `expectVersion`; no field and no version is added for them. **Carried by T-667**, which makes `commitTurn` accept the setup tables as write targets: for `company_kinds` (and `flow_defaults` when a turn writes the business's defaults at S1-21, and `kind_flows` if a write ever names one in its version reads), its `oneKey` takes a two-column key, finds a version-read row by item 77's name and answers a written or changed row by it, while every earlier commit exchange answers as before, since no one-column table's name changes. The same holds for K-24's rows of two columns (`picked_kind_texts` as `pick id/pf key`, `new_work_draft_blocks` as `draft id/block`), whose later key column holds no slash, if T-667 carries them; a table whose later key columns could hold a slash needs its own rule in a later version (open gap 85). **A snapshot's flow default** is named the same way: a terms snapshot that copies the flow's default free-cancel time rests on that row, so its version reads name it `business id/flow` at the version read 5 answers, as they name the rules and limits it lists (T-320's `terms.ts` names those today, not the flow default: the new card, ruling (1)).

### CQ-349's rulings (T-320's open questions (1) to (4); DL-897, DL-903)

1. **`company_kinds`' two-column key, its version read and its writes: item 77.** Read 1 names each `company_kinds` row `company id/kind id` with its version; `placeKind` and `updateKind` are K-05 v1 `stateWrite`s naming the row by its key, carried by T-667 with item 77's names in its version reads and answers, so no later K-05 version is needed; read 5 names a `flow_defaults` row `business id/flow` the same way. **Why:** the name is one string, so every frozen place a `rowId` goes (K-02's facts, K-04's `versions_read`, K-05's and K-09's answers, K-08's `rowRef.id`, whose `safeId` takes a slash) holds it with no field or version added, where a key object in `rowRef` would restate `rowRef` and every definition that holds it, and a surrogate id would need a K-23 column and a migration; and the database builds and compares it whole, so no code parses an id. **What changes elsewhere:** T-320's service names `company_kinds` rows and the snapshot's flow default in its version reads once T-667 merges (its header: "no write names the kind's version read"), a change to the catalog's own code: the new card of ruling (4).
2. **K-23 `business_setup.no_money` (M02) in read 1: yes,** as `setup`, with its row's version. **Why:** K-25 rule 9 checks a way's money path against both the business's M02 and its kinds' `nomoney` fact ("with no money through Rosie (K-23 `no_money`; fact `nomoney`) only nothing before and after `counter` (A) or `own` (B)"), and M02 is the business's own answer (S1-13, S1-27), which no kind's fact holds; T-320's default 2 checks the fact alone because no read gave M02. It is one column of one row, the owner's to see (audience owner), read in the same transaction as the kinds it checks, and its version lets a commit refuse a way written after the owner turned no money on (limit 4). **Null** (no setup row yet) is not known: a consumer that checks money paths reads it as the closed side, no money through Rosie, never as money allowed (default 161). **Who:** the new card of ruling (4) widens T-320's port to take it and makes the check; T-1429 binds the read as it stands.
3. **K-26's registry (`trade_facts`, `plugin_switches`) for the names written to `company_kinds`: no read; names only from the compiled bundle, held to the registry in code.** **Why:** the registry is written once by a migration's seed from the master's live lists, the same lists the compiled bundle carries in each flow's record (K-01 v1 `record`: "live switches and facts"), with `nomoney` added as a switch (K-26 default 3), and an entry is added only by a migration's seed recorded as a K-26 minor version before any row uses it (K-26 rule 6), so the names a business may hold change with a deploy, never between two reads. Every name the catalog writes comes from a compiled source: a trade's defaults (K-24's `kinds.facts` and `kind_flows.switches_on`, written from the bundle's trade overlays: K-24 rule 7) or a new-work draft's checklist names (S1-03). So the check is code's, against the registry's 28 facts and 6 switches held in code and held to K-26's `seed-rule-registry.json` by a unit test, as limit 2's evaluator holds K-26's 16 rule kinds (`actions/src/rules/evaluator/registry.ts`: "registry.test holds this list to the seed"): an unknown name is refused, never written (fails closed). A read on every write would re-read a constant and find a drift only in a live call; the test finds it in CI. **Who:** the new card of ruling (4); until it lands, T-320's default 13 stands (form only, and `nomoney` as both or neither).
4. **The checks no read of T-320's gave, each with the read that answers it and the card that makes it.** One new card, the catalog's cross-row checks (the action-layer engineer, `actions/src/domain/catalog`; after T-1429 and T-667, before T-633 or T-634 binds the service), makes (a) to (d), the money-path check against `setup` (ruling (2)), the registry check (ruling (3)), item 77's version reads (ruling (1)) and T-320's security notes N1 and N2 (a space's business; `checkCatalogWrite` refusing `business_id` or `id` in an update's `set`). T-1429 binds the reads and makes none of them. **The lead cards it** (open gap 86).
   - (a) **A deposit never over the price known then** (K-25 rule 9; S1-13): read 4 answers it, the way's price rules in effect at or after the save (its place's rule, else the service's own), against which a set `deposit_cents` is checked, and a visit path's `from_cents` (A4-13: "never more than the starting price"). A percent is under the price by its form (1 to 99); a photos path's deposit is checked against its quote once priced, as K-30 refuses a line's deposit over its price at booking. A price lowered later under a way's deposit is the same check at the price's save, with read 3.
   - (b) **A list-priced way has a price rule in effect:** reads 3 and 4 answer it (a way whose path starts `list`, and no rule of its place or of the service in effect at the save).
   - (c) **A way's location and place** (K-25 rule 9; S1-43: a location of the business, of the place's kind, with a travel area for the customer's place): no v1.18 read. v1.2's `readTurnState` facts read a K-23 `locations` row by its id under the call's business once T-667 adds K-23's fact homes (its Gives: "each setup fact the journey 1 and 2 calls and the frontier need, once from its home with its row id and version"), its `kind` and `travel_miles` with the row's version, and a location of another business answers `notFound` (item 26), which is the check (open gap 84).
   - (d) **A rule's worker belongs to the business:** no v1.18 read. v1.2's `readTurnState` facts read a K-04 `resources` row by its id under the call's business today (`kind`, `active`: `db/repo/turn-state.ts` `FACT_HOMES`), and another business's worker answers `notFound` (open gap 84).
   - (e) **K-30's `ticked_terms` consent of the booking** (K-30 rule 10: "the terms consent is of kind ticked_terms for the same booking"): not the catalog's. The booking writer that writes the booking and calls `takeTermsSnapshot` holds the consent: the `ticked_terms` row it writes for that booking in the same commit, or one a page wrote before it, which v1.2's `readTurnState` answers among the person's consent rows at the business (`consentRead`: its id, kind and booking). No read is added; the check is the booking writer's card's, which binds the snapshot to the booking it writes (C-099, journey 3's lane): a new card if no card holds it yet (open gap 87; the lead cards it).

Questions (5) and (6) are the lead's with Jay; T-320 built their stated defaults (DL-903).

### Who reads them

- **T-320's catalog and pricing service** (C-096): through T-1429's binding of `CatalogReads` (`actions/src/domain/catalog/pg/`), field by field as items 72 to 76 map them. A `notFound` or `transient` answer, or a row of another business, throws and fails closed, never an empty list or a guessed row, and the service's own `otherBusiness` and `badRead` checks stay.
- **Limit 2's owner rules** (C-082): T-1429 binds `OwnerRules.rulesOf` to read 5's `rules` where they answer its facts (`RuleRow` field by field), or writes a note naming what it lacks.
- **The journey 1 calls** (T-633, T-634) bind the service only after T-1429 and T-667 merge (DL-888).

### What v1.18 does not name

- **Writes** of any kind: the catalog's are K-05 v1 `stateWrite`s, and T-667 carries the setup tables and item 77's names, with no K-05 version for them.
- **Price OK limits' amounts** (open gap 83), **a location's and a worker's facts** (open gap 84) and **K-26's registry** (ruling (3)).
- **Event-log entries for the catalog's writes** (T-320 default 15; CQ-56) and **the business's one currency** (T-320 default 3: the caller's, since no column holds it).
- **T-1421's flags and messages reads**, at the next free number after v1.18 (DL-903), then T-1155's arrival read, T-1177's outcome per answered label, T-1187's row reader, CQ-211's note on `markPart`, CQ-234, CQ-235's note a, CQ-263, CQ-293 and CQ-289's descriptions (open gap 76), each at the next free number after T-1421's, since v1.18 keeps to its card (DL-888, DL-903).

### Consumers of v1.18

Each moves to v1.18 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.17 consumers keep working.

- **Database engineer**, the producer: C-152, T-1428 (one new db/repo file and its integration test): items 72 to 76 as written.
- **Database engineer:** C-152, T-667 (`db/repo/commit-turn.ts`): item 77's names for the two-column setup tables in `commitTurn`'s version reads and answers.
- **Action-layer engineer:** C-096 catalog and pricing, T-1429 (`actions/src/domain/catalog/pg/`): T-320's `CatalogReads` over T-1428's reads; and the new catalog-checks card (rulings (1) to (4)).
- **Action-layer engineer:** C-082 limit 2's owner rules, T-1429 (`actions/src/gate/limit-2/` only if `OwnerRules` binds there; else a note).
- **QA:** a new task, the contract tests, for C-152: the v1.18 fixtures against v1.18, each refused one mended into its twin, every earlier fixture against v1.18, then each exchange through db/repo's reads after T-1428.

What the new tasks must hold to:

- **T-1428** reads each item's columns and no other (its tests check that no statement names `extras`, `words`, `listing_phone`, `listing_email` or `set_by_person_id` of `rules`), as `rosie_app` under the request's business after `requireBusinessScope()`, every list in its order; answers each v1.18 fixture's outcome from its seeds, `read-price-rules-haircut-tie`'s two rows written first; builds item 77's names in SQL from the key columns; answers a span in whole minutes and throws on one that is not (default 156); and shows what no fixture can: under another business's scope each read answers nothing of the first business's, and a price withdrawn before it started (K-25's `seed-price-withdrawn-before-start`) is answered while its empty range closes after `at`.
- **T-1429** maps each answer field by field as items 72 to 76 say, never fills a missing field with a default, and throws on `notFound`, `transient` or a row of another business (the service's checks stay); reads `setup`, item 77's names and the flows' versions into no field, since T-320's port has none for them (the new card widens it); and binds `OwnerRules.rulesOf` to read 5, or notes what it lacks.
- **T-667** follows item 77: a two-column key found by its built name compared whole, written and changed rows answered by it, a `stateWrite` update's `key` naming both columns; every earlier commit exchange unchanged.
- **The new catalog-checks card** makes the checks of rulings (1) to (4), each with its tests.
- **QA's task** lists each refused fixture's mend (its one fault and its twin) and adds v1.18 to the fixture part's versions and, after T-1428, to the database side's.

**The integration tests on this branch:** none meets a v1.18 fixture. No db/repo loop selects these five definitions; `tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` selects its fixtures by version (`DB_SIDE_VERSIONS` lists no v1.18); and `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts` checks every fixture of every version against its schema, every accepted one's required fields and closed objects, its given and its cast's numbers (its parts 1, 3 and 4), so `npm test` checks the twenty-four, while its part 2 mends and side list are QA's v1.18 task.

## v1.19 (T-1455): the kind matcher's catalog and the owner's kinds so far, the reads journey 1's first calls lack

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): T-632's handoff (`ops/cards/T-632.md`: the matcher takes the K-24 kinds rows handed in, and "the production read of those rows is T-633's binding") and the lead's card (`ops/cards/T-1455.md`; DL-949), on the sign-up chain (DL-867), the step after S1-02's question reaches the owner. No K-05 call lists a K-24 `kinds` row (v1.2's `readTurnState` reads one column of a row whose id the caller already holds, item 26 with T-667's setup facts; v1.18's `readBusinessKinds` reads a business's `company_kinds` with their `kind_flows`, item 72), no db/repo code reads the kinds table outside a test, and T-633's May touch (`actions/src/tool-api/journey-1/kinds/**`) can add neither a contract nor a db/repo read. Going through T-633's and T-634's Gives and Proves step by step (section "What journey 1's first calls read" below) finds one more read no K-05 call answers: the owner's kinds so far, the picks with their text blocks and drafts and the companies, which sign-up writes with no business, so that no business scope sees them (K-23 README rule 5; K-24 README "Picks, text blocks and drafts"), and which K-09's setup variants name by id (`SetupS1_03`'s `pickedKindId`; `SetupS1_11`'s and `SetupS1_04`'s `pickedKindIds`). Every other read those steps need is answered by a frozen call, named in that section. **Kind:** additive, a minor version, v1.19, the next free number after T-1427's v1.18 (frozen.json listed v1 to v1.18 when this card started, and main held no contract change since; the card: "v1.19 if this card comes before T-1421, which then takes the next number"), so T-1421's flags and messages reads take the next free number after v1.19; no CTO OK is needed and the 7-day rule does not apply. `data-access.v1.19.schema.json` holds every v1.18 definition under its own name, by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.18.schema.json` (which refers on to every earlier version), all nineteen files as they are, and restates none; it adds the two reads' requests, answers, errors and exchanges and the row shapes they answer. So every v1 to v1.18 exchange is a v1.19 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.19). **No schema change:** the reads read K-24 v1's `kinds`, `picked_kinds`, `picked_kind_texts`, `new_work_drafts` and `new_work_draft_blocks` (0021) and K-23 v1's `companies` (0019), with K-04 v1's `persons` and `businesses` for the checks (0002), as migrated, with the `SELECT` grants each role already holds; no table, column, grant, index or migration. Every column the two reads and the section below name exists in K-23 v1 and K-24 v1 as migrated, so no K-24 change is asked for (the card's "Not here"): the one thing a step needs that no K-24 column holds, a trade's eleven plug-in text blocks for S1-11, is K-01's bundle by design (K-24 README "Who owns what"; default 182). **Not here:** db/repo's two reads (the database engineer's card after this one); binding them (T-633, T-634); the matcher itself (T-632, merged as f84a0e6); a write of any kind (K-05 v1's `stateWrite`, carried by T-667's setup write targets with item 77's names); QA's contract test (a new task).

### The calls v1.19 adds

- **`readKindCatalog`** (item 78): the kind matcher's catalog, every K-24 `kinds` row whose `source` is `trade_file`; serves T-632's `CatalogKind` list through T-633's `matchKinds`.
- **`readPickedKinds`** (item 79): the owner's kinds of work as sign-up holds them, K-24 `picked_kinds` with each pick's `picked_kind_texts` by key and version, its `new_work_drafts` row and that draft's `new_work_draft_blocks`, and K-23 `companies`, of the call's business and of the call's person with no business; serves T-633's S1-02 and S1-03 and T-634's S1-11, S1-04 and S1-08.

Both read PostgreSQL's **primary, uncached** (a decision read: the owner's pick and the steps that rest on it; arch 02 line 117; arch 10 lines 504, 538), each in one read-only transaction, so every row and version it answers is one moment's, and both write nothing. **Each row with its id and version** (limit 4): a row whose primary key is one column (`kinds`, `picked_kinds`, `new_work_drafts`, `companies`) by that column, and a row whose key is two columns (`picked_kind_texts`, `new_work_draft_blocks`) by item 77's name, answered as `rowId`, which the database builds from the row's own columns (T-667's `db/repo/setup/row-names.ts`) and nobody parses. **Every column answered is named** in the items below, never `select *`, and every object v1.19 answers is closed (`additionalProperties` false), so the schema refuses an answer that carries more: no person's address or number (`refuse-picked-kinds-owner-mobile`), no catalog column beyond the matcher's (`refuse-kind-catalog-with-facts`) and no draft block's answer (`refuse-picked-kinds-draft-block-answer`). Ids are compared byte by byte (collate "C"), never parsed (CQ-09), and both calls can answer `transient`. No source gives either a time budget (journey 1's calls; K-09 proposes at most 200 ms for `matchKinds`: its README default 3).

### How each v1.19 call works (K-23 and K-24 v1 as migrated)

78. **`readKindCatalog`** (T-632's `CatalogKind`, `actions/src/domain/kind-matcher/matcher.ts` lines 55 to 63: "One K-24 v1 kinds row as the matcher reads it (README line 41: name, style and pattern; source picks the rows)"; the card's Gives 1). **Columns:** of `kinds`, every row whose `source` is `trade_file`: `id`, `name`, `style`, `pattern`, `source` and `version`, answered under those names, the matcher's five fields with the row's version beside them (default 168), never `facts` (the trade's default facts, which S1-04 reads by id through `readTurnState`: section below; `refuse-kind-catalog-with-facts`) and no column of `kind_flows`. **Never a drafted kind:** the statement takes `source = 'trade_file'`, so a kind an owner drafted at S1-03 (source `new_work`, id `new-...`) is never answered, as K-24 README line 96 keeps it: "v1 keeps a drafted kind to its owner; the matcher offers only `trade_file` kinds" (`read-kind-catalog-sample`, where `new-pack-hikes` stands in the table beside the eight; `refuse-kind-catalog-drafted-kind`). The schema holds each answered row's `source` to `trade_file` and its `style` to a word (`kinds_trade_file_has_style`). **Order:** `id`, compared byte by byte, the matcher's own order (`matcher.ts` `offered()`: "in code-point order of id"; default 169). **Scope, role and grant** (the card: "Rule its scope"): the catalog is shared reference data with no business column. K-24 README rule 2 scopes `kinds` none ("the Tool API roles may select them and never insert, update or delete"), and migration 0021 leaves row-level security off on it ("kinds, kind_flows: the shared catalog every business picks from (scope none), so row-level security stays off") and grants the Tool API's client its `SELECT` (`grant select on kinds, kind_flows to rosie_app;`). So the read runs as `rosie_app` in the call's own transaction under whatever business scope that transaction holds, which does not narrow it, and answers the same rows under any business and under none; `rosie_job` holds the same `SELECT` (`grant select, insert, update on kinds, kind_flows to rosie_job;`), so a runtime-side caller reads it the same way. **The request names nothing** (default 167): a business named would read as a filter the read never applies (`refuse-kind-catalog-names-business`), and K-09 `matchKinds.request`'s `flow` filters no kind (T-632 default 5: all 243 kinds run in both flows), so neither is taken. **An empty list** means the catalog seed has not run (K-24 README rule 7: one `kinds` row per trade file from the compiled bundle); the read answers it as it stands, and the matcher refuses a list with no trade-file kind (`KindCatalogError`, T-632 default 4; QUESTIONS.md CQ-348 (4): K-09's `indexUnavailable`), so S1-02 asks again later and nothing is picked (default 170; `read-kind-catalog-not-seeded`). **The mapping** (T-633): each row to `CatalogKind` field by field, `id`, `name`, `style`, `pattern` and `source` as answered; `version` is what a write that rests on a kind names (`kinds/<id>`), and the matcher leaves it unread; T-633 builds the matcher once per catalog read (T-632's handoff). **Shown:** `read-kind-catalog-sample` (the eight trade-file kinds of K-24's `seed-kinds-sample`, the drafted `new-pack-hikes` left out) and `read-kind-catalog-not-seeded` (empty).

79. **`readPickedKinds`** (the owner's kinds so far; the card's Gives 2, from the section below). **The request** names the call's business (K-09 `caller.businessId`), or null for a sign-up before its business, always stated (`refuse-picked-kinds-business-left-out`), and the call's own person (K-09 `caller.personId`: the turn party's person, K-05 v1.11 item 52, or a page's signed-in person), never null (`refuse-picked-kinds-no-person`) and never a person named in words (default 172). **Which rows:** every `picked_kinds` and `companies` row whose `business_id` is the request's business, and every one with no business whose `owner_person_id` is the request's person; with `businessId` null, only the latter. Never another business's row, not even counted (`read-picked-kinds-barbershop`), and never another person's row with no business (`read-picked-kinds-other-person`). With a business, the person's rows with no business are answered beside the business's, in one moment (default 173), since a sign-up's picks carry no business until S1-04 or S1-09 sets one while its turns may already run under a business (`read-picked-kinds-sign-up-under-business`). Each answered pick carries every one of its `picked_kind_texts` rows, its `new_work_drafts` row and every one of that draft's `new_work_draft_blocks` that the pick's own rule reaches: their business is the pick's or none, by the composite foreign keys `picked_kind_texts_pick_business_fk`, `new_work_drafts_pick_business_fk` and `new_work_draft_blocks_draft_business_fk`, and one with none under a pick with a business is answered nowhere (default 180). **Columns:** of `picked_kinds`, every column: `id`, `owner_person_id`, `position`, `words`, `outcome`, `kind_id`, `company_id`, `business_id`, `picked_at` and `version`, answered as `id`, `ownerPersonId`, `position`, `words`, `outcome`, `kindId`, `companyId`, `businessId`, `pickedAt` and `version` (none holds an address or a number; `words` is the owner's own words for the kind: default 177); of `picked_kind_texts`, `picked_kind_id`, `pf_key`, `business_id` and `version`, never `block` (default 175), answered in `texts` as `rowId` (`<pick id>/<pf key>`, item 77; `refuse-picked-kinds-text-by-key-alone`), `pfKey`, `businessId` and `version`; of `new_work_drafts`, `id`, `business_id`, `state`, `drafted_at` and `version` (`picked_kind_id` is the pick's), answered in `draft` as `id`, `businessId`, `state`, `draftedAt`, `version` and `blocks`, or `draft` null; of `new_work_draft_blocks`, `block`, `business_id`, `outcome`, `facts_on`, `switches_on`, `answered_at` and `version` (`draft_id` is the draft's), never `answer` (default 176), answered in `blocks` as `rowId` (`<draft id>/<block>`, item 77), `block`, `businessId`, `outcome`, `factsOn`, `switchesOn`, `answeredAt` and `version`; and of `companies`, every column: `id`, `owner_person_id`, `name`, `business_id` and `version`, answered as `id`, `ownerPersonId`, `name`, `businessId` and `version` (default 178). Never `company_kinds` (v1.18's `readBusinessKinds` lists a business's once the business exists, and a sign-up's row is read by its item 77 name, `<company id>/<kind id>`, through `readTurnState`), never a `handles` or `phones` row (`refuse-picked-kinds-owner-mobile`), and never a `kinds` row (the catalog is item 78's, and one kind by id `readTurnState`'s). **Order** (default 179): picks by `(picked_at, position, id)`, the order the owner named them (K-24's `picked_kinds_owner_idx`, "in the order named"); each pick's texts by `pf_key`; a draft's blocks by `block`; companies by `id`; ids and keys compared byte by byte. **Scope, role and grants** (defaults 171 and 173; the database engineer's review of round 2 and the lead's ruling, DL-958): `rosie_job`, in one read-only transaction at repeatable read, so every statement reads one snapshot, one moment, whatever scope it runs under, in this order. (1) `persons` by the request's person: it spans businesses and has no row-level security (0012 lines 98 to 101), so no scope narrows it. (2) **With a business**, the transaction's scope set to it (db/repo's `setBusinessScope`) and confirmed by `requireBusinessScope()` before the first read of `businesses` (default 174) and of the business's picks, texts, drafts, blocks and companies, each statement still carrying `business_id = <the request's business>`. (3) Then the scope cleared and confirmed empty, as db/repo's `assertRuntimeSide` confirms it, before the first read of the person's rows with no business, each statement carrying `business_id is null and owner_person_id = <the request's person>`. **With `businessId` null**, (1) and (3) alone, the scope confirmed empty before the first read: the runtime side. **Each child joins its pick under the same rule** (default 180): a statement that reads `picked_kind_texts` or `new_work_drafts` joins the pick, and one that reads `new_work_draft_blocks` its draft and that draft's pick, the rule on the pick (`business_id = <the request's business>`, or `business_id is null and owner_person_id = <the request's person>`) and the same `business_id` condition on each row joined to it, never a child's `business_id is null` alone: these three tables hold no `owner_person_id`, and their composite foreign keys are MATCH SIMPLE, so a child whose `business_id` is null stands on its one-column key under any pick (0021 lines 30 to 35). **Why the business's scope:** a Tool API call never runs with no scope (db/repo `scope.ts` lines 8 to 12, T-1094; QUESTIONS.md CQ-70's default: "a rosie_job path refuses an empty scope"; defaults 25 and 55), since with none `rosie_job` sees every business's rows (0012 lines 21 to 24, and each table's `<table>_runtime` policy in 0019 and 0021), so a business's rows read with the scope empty would have only the statements' own rule between them and every other business's; under the business's scope its policies keep every other business's row out as well. One transaction that switches its scope is what `commitTurn` already does for its per-write scope (db/repo `commit-turn.ts` lines 415 to 432: `Scope.to` sets `rosie.business_id` to each write's business, or to '' for the runtime side). **Why the runtime side for the rest:** a row with no business is seen by no business scope (K-23 README rule 5: "A company, or a company's kind, whose business is null is seen by no business scope; sign-up's turns read and write it on the runtime side"; K-24 README "Picks, text blocks and drafts", the same for picks, blocks and drafts), and migrations 0019 and 0021 give each of these tables `<table>_tool_api`, which shows `rosie_app` only its scope's rows and none with no scope, and `<table>_runtime`, which shows `rosie_job` every row with no scope, else only its scope's. So neither the Tool API's client nor a scoped `rosie_job` can read a sign-up's picks, and with the scope empty the statement's own rule is what keeps every other business and person out. `rosie_job` holds `SELECT` on `picked_kinds`, `picked_kind_texts`, `new_work_drafts` and `new_work_draft_blocks` (0021), on `companies` (0019) and on `persons` and `businesses` (0012); no definer function, no grant and no table owner's read. **The person first, then the business** (default 174): `persons` by the request's person (`notFound`, table `persons`), then with a business `businesses` by it, under its scope (`notFound`, table `businesses`), so empty lists always mean none (`read-picked-kinds-no-business-row`). **Shown:** `read-picked-kinds-sign-up` (Sam's sign-up before S1-04: three picks with no business, the barbershop's eleven blocks by key), `read-picked-kinds-sign-up-under-business`, `read-picked-kinds-other-person`, `read-picked-kinds-barbershop` (a placed pick and its company), `read-picked-kinds-pack-hikes-draft` (a new-work pick, its draft in review and six blocks) and `read-picked-kinds-no-business-row` (`notFound`).

"A write target" is K-05 v1's `commitTurn` `stateWrite`, which T-667 lets carry every K-23 and K-24 table, a two-column key by item 77's name, each write under its own business or, with none, on the runtime side.
