Each read the table names is under the call's business where its rows carry one, and answers each row with its id or item 77's name and its version. T-633's Proves (`match-kinds.json` gives its response; the kinds are one state write in one commit; an unknown kind opens one review item and the business stays offline; S1-74 writes no kind; the write lands in K-24's business kinds) and T-634's (insurance's four facts; the 12 trades' window and empty free-cancel time; three kinds in two companies give two company rows; the plan lists only the kinds kept) each rest on the rows above.

### What v1.19 does not name

- **Writes** of any kind: T-633's and T-634's are K-05 v1 `stateWrite`s through `commitTurn`, carried by T-667 with item 77's names; no field or version is added for them.
- **A drafted kind's text blocks** (`picked_kind_texts`, "for a drafted kind, the draft's": K-24): K-09's `SetupS1_11` loads listed kinds only, and no step of T-633's or T-634's writes them (open gap 89); item 79 answers whatever blocks a pick holds.
- **The review queue's own reads** (K-37 `review_items`, the team's list, a row under review): T-319's `PagingReader`, whose binding is its own card's (open gap 92).
- **K-09 `readSetup`** (where the setup stands, each key given or missing): not T-633's or T-634's; its card reads items 78 and 79 and the frozen calls as the table above does.
- **T-1421's flags and messages reads**, at the next free number after v1.19 (the card; DL-949), then T-1155's arrival read, T-1177's outcome per answered label, T-1187's row reader, CQ-211's note on `markPart`, CQ-234, CQ-235's note a, CQ-263, CQ-293 and CQ-289's descriptions (open gap 76), each at the next free number after T-1421's, since v1.19 keeps to its card.

### Consumers of v1.19

Each moves to v1.19 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.18 consumers keep working.

- **Database engineer**, the producer: C-152, the db/repo card after this one (a new task the lead cards): items 78 and 79 as written, with their integration tests.
- **Action-layer engineer:** C-074 journey 1's calls, T-633 (`actions/src/tool-api/journey-1/kinds/**`): `matchKinds` over item 78 through T-632's matcher (C-130), and S1-02, S1-03 and S1-74 over item 79 and the write targets.
- **Action-layer engineer:** C-074 journey 1's calls, T-634 (`actions/src/tool-api/journey-1/plug-ins/**`, `companies/**` and `plan/**`): S1-11, S1-04 and S1-08 over item 79, `readTurnState`'s facts by id and the pinned bundle.
- **QA:** a new task, the contract tests, for C-152: the v1.19 fixtures against v1.19, each refused one mended into its twin, every earlier fixture against v1.19, then each exchange through db/repo's two reads after the db/repo card.

What the new tasks must hold to:

- **The db/repo card** reads each item's columns and no other: its tests check that no statement names `kinds.facts`, `picked_kind_texts.block`, `new_work_draft_blocks.answer`, `handles` or `phones`, that each of item 79's statements carries its rule, and that each statement that reads texts, drafts or blocks joins its pick (a block its draft and that draft's pick) under it. Item 78 runs as `rosie_app`, in a call's own transaction under any scope and in a read-only transaction of its own; item 79 as `rosie_job` in one read-only transaction at repeatable read, in the order item 79's scope gives: `persons` first; with a business, `setBusinessScope` and `requireBusinessScope()` before `businesses` and the business's rows, then the scope cleared and confirmed empty (`assertRuntimeSide`) before the person's rows with no business; with `businessId` null, the scope confirmed empty before the first read. Every list comes in its order, the business's rows and the person's merged into one as the database orders them (`picked_at` to the microsecond, ids byte by byte), and item 77's names are built in SQL from the key columns (T-667's `rowNameSql`). It throws on an empty `kinds.style` or `companies.name`, a row it cannot carry (the tables hold them only not null: 0021 line 100, 0019 line 123; the schema asks at least one character), as default 156 throws on a span it cannot carry, and never answers it. It answers each v1.19 fixture's outcome after its given, the rows the sign-up fixtures' notes list written first, since no frozen seed holds a pick with no business. And it shows what no fixture can: under business A, B's picks, texts, drafts, blocks and companies never answered, and a person's rows with no business answered to that person alone; under business A, a pick of A whose text, draft or block has no business answered without that row, which is answered nowhere, neither under A nor with the person's rows (default 180); a pick placed in a company with no business yet (S1-04 before S1-09) answered with its company to its person; the catalog the same under every business and under none; and a drafted kind never in item 78's answer, whatever its draft's state, approved included (K-24 README line 96).
- **T-633** builds the matcher from item 78's answer once per read (T-632's handoff), each row mapped to `CatalogKind` field by field, and fails closed on `transient` and on a list the matcher refuses (nothing picked; S1-02 asked again later). It numbers new picks after item 79's, and names a pick, a draft and a block, to `readTurnState` or in a write, only by an id or name item 79 answered for the same person (open gap 90).
- **T-634** loads a listed kind's blocks from the pinned bundle's trade overlay, never from another pick's blocks; reads C5's facts and settings and a trade's default switches by id through `readTurnState` (the catalog rule) or from the same overlay, one source in a call; places only picks item 79 answered, each into a company item 79 answered or one it inserts; and reads a pf.rules block by the name item 79's `texts` gives, with `businessId` null for a block with no business.
- **QA**: `tests/contract/data-access.v1.18.test.ts` selects the accepted fixtures of the versions before v1.18 alone since round 2 of this card mended its line 70 on this branch (DL-954; the paragraph below), so nothing is left to do before this version merges. **QA's v1.19 task** lists each refused fixture's mend (its one fault and its twin, as section "Fixtures", v1.19, names them), adds v1.19 to the fixture part's versions with its sides (C-074, C-130 and C-152), and, after the db/repo card, to a database side.

**The tests on this branch:** one met v1.19's fixtures and failed at its load, and round 2 mended its one line here. `tests/contract/data-access.v1.18.test.ts` (T-1434) compiled v1.18's definition of every accepted fixture whose version was not v1.18 (line 70, `earlierAccepted`, `entry.version !== VERSION`, used at line 164 and in its test "has every accepted fixture of v1 to v1.17 pass the same definition of v1.18"), which its own comment and test title mean for v1 to v1.17 alone; v1.19's fixtures of its two new calls have no v1.18 definition, so the file threw "missing the schema https://rosie.invalid/contracts/data-access/data-access.v1.18.schema.json#/$defs/readKindCatalog.exchange" before any of its tests ran (seen in run 1 with `npm run test:contract -- tests/contract/data-access.v1.18.test.ts`, which reads files alone). Round 2 changed that line alone, under the lead's ruling that added it to this card's May touch (DL-954): `+entry.version.slice(3) < 18 && entry.expect === "accept"`, which selects the versions before v1.18 by their number ("v1" reads as 0, "v1.1" as 1), so it selects the same 255 accepted fixtures as main (dd9eb08) and none of v1.19's, and the file passes with every other contract test (`npm run test:contract`: 18 files, 2,976 tests). No other line of any test changed. Every other file meets nothing it cannot answer: no db/repo loop selects these two definitions; `tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` and `tests/contract/data-access.v1.18.db-side.test.ts` select their fixtures by version (`DB_SIDE_VERSIONS` lists no v1.19); and `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts` checks every fixture of every version against its schema, every accepted one's required fields and closed objects, its given and its cast's numbers (its parts 1, 3 and 4), so it checks the sixteen (784 of 784 on this branch, the file alone), while its part 2 mends and side list are QA's v1.19 task.

## v1.20 (T-1187): the sign-up reading only at a business with no live owner

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): the security review of v1.5, note a (`ops/cards/T-1184.md`), README open gap 46, carded as T-1187 (DL-370) and made card 2 of the S1-04 design ruling (DL-994 ruling 6; DL-999). Item 44 reads a section 1 row to a person with no role as its line's audience, and has no business condition: `rowReader.request` holds nothing about the row's business, so a person with no role at someone else's business would be read as its owner, and limit 7 would release the owner's fields on the row to them (item 35's `slotSource`). Nothing was exposed: bundle v0's and v1's section 1 lines carry no slot in a line Rosie sends, and the test business has no owner. The bundle that pins S1-08 and S1-09 pins section 1 lines that carry slots, so this version merges before that cut (the card's last line). **The form:** the card allows a K-05 minor version, or a design record ruling that a sign-up turn runs only under the business the signer creates, or none. The K-05 form is taken, since the frozen text and the code do not prove the second ("Why v1.11's party rule is not enough", below). **Kind:** additive, a minor version, v1.20, the number free at its start (`frozen.json` held v1 to v1.19 on Nov 20; DL-344, DL-999); no CTO OK is needed and the 7-day rule does not apply. `data-access.v1.20.schema.json` holds every v1.19 definition under its own name, by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.19.schema.json` (which refers on to every earlier version), all twenty files as they are; it restates four, the rule of section "v1.1"'s **Kind**: `rowReader.request`, which takes the optional `businessOwner`, each of v1.5's seven fields by reference to v1.5's own definition of it (v1.6 to v1.19 keep them unchanged); `noReaderError`, whose `why` gains `businessHasOwner`; `rowReader.errors`, which refers to it; and `rowReader.exchange`, which refers to both and holds a request that states `businessOwner` `live` to item 80. It adds two: `businessOwnerState` and `rowReader.ownerExchange`, `rowReader.exchange` narrowed by `allOf` to a request that states the field wherever the outcome reads it. So every v1 to v1.19 exchange is a v1.20 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.20): none carries the field, and without it the exchange is v1.19's. **No database change:** no call is added or changed; the owner state comes from v1.4's `readBusinessOwner` as built (T-1161), and `readOutboxRow` reads as before; no column, grant, index or migration. **Not here:** the port's code (T-1497, card 28 of the S1-04 design ruling: `actions/src/tool-api/turn-face/rows.ts` and `pg/calls.ts`); QA's contract test; the outbox row's own step and line (open gap 29: T-1474, at the next free number, v1.22 since T-1508 took v1.21: DL-1030).

### The rule v1.20 adds

80. **The sign-up reading only at a business with no live owner** (item 44's condition; T-1184's security note a; closes open gap 46). `rowReader.request` gains one optional field, `businessOwner`: whether the row's business has a live owner role (K-04 v1 `person_roles`: `role` owner, `ended_at` null), `none` or `live`. Without it the mapping is item 44 as v1.5 to v1.19 state it. With it, a recipient who holds no role is mapped in this order, the first case that applies (default 187):
    1. **No person, or a step outside section 1:** `noReader`, `noRole`, as item 44 refuses it, whatever the owner state (`row-reader-a5-05-tomas-live-owner`).
    2. **The business has a live owner** (`businessOwner` `live`): `noReader`, `businessHasOwner`, whatever lane the line goes to. A business that has an owner is never read as its owner, its staff or its customer by someone who holds no role at it (`row-reader-s1-02-sam-after-hand-over`; `refuse-row-reader-sign-up-line-at-owned-business`).
    3. **The business has none** (`businessOwner` `none`): item 44 as it stands, the line's audience for a line to owner, staff or cust, else `noReader`, `lineHasNoReader` (`row-reader-s1-02-no-role-no-owner`). `businessHasOwner` is never answered here (`refuse-row-reader-has-owner-at-unowned-business`).

    **A recipient who holds a role is read by it, as before, whatever the owner state** (`row-reader-s1-02-owner-with-owner-state`): the rule narrows the sign-up reading alone, so the business's own owner, its admins, staff and customers are mapped exactly as v1.3 and v1.5 map them, and no owner is refused by their own role.

    **Where the state comes from** (the card: "as v1.4's readBusinessOwner answers it"): the consumer calls `readBusinessOwner` (item 42) for the row's business, which is the business `readOutboxRow` read the row under, the call's own (`readOutboxRow.request` `businessId`; a row of another business is never seen: default 57), in the role and scope item 42 names, on the primary, uncached. An owner answered is `live`; `moreThanOneOwner` is `live` (default 184); `owner` null is `none` (default 185; open gap 94); a row with no business is `none`, with no read (default 186). `notFound` and `transient` give no state: no request is built, and the row is refused before a reader is looked for, as a failed `readOutboxRow` refuses it (default 188).

    **When it is stated, and when it is read** (default 189): wherever the outcome reads it, which is a recipient who is a person, holds no role and is on a section 1 step. `rowReader.ownerExchange` requires the field there and refuses a null (`refuse-row-reader-owner-state-left-out`, `refuse-row-reader-owner-state-null`), so in a v1.20 mapping a reader by the sign-up line always stands on a stated `none`. For every other request the field may be left out and the consumer need not make the read, so a row to someone who holds a role, the common case, costs no extra read and gains no new way to fail. The read is made at each mapping, by compose input and by finalize each, never taken from the turn's party read (item 52), from a cache, or from the Tool API's caller: a row finalized after an owner appeared is refused at finalize though it composed.

    **What the request never holds** (default 190): the owner's person, role or handle. The mapping needs only whether an owner exists, and `ownerContact` stays with the safety service's line.

    **The schema holds the rule:** `rowReader.exchange`'s rules by number are 0 to 2, v1.5's own by reference; 3 to 6, v1.5's rules 3 to 6 applying only while `businessOwner` is not `live`, each with v1.5's own `then` by reference; and 7, new: a person with no role on a section 1 step with `businessOwner` `live` is `noReader`, `businessHasOwner`. Together they still decide every recipient with no role. `rowReader.ownerExchange` adds its one rule (the field stated where the outcome reads it). As in v1.5, the generated types leave the rules out, so a consumer proves its mapping on the fixtures (section "v1.5", "Keywords beyond ADR-005's list").

### Why v1.11's party rule is not enough

The S1-04 design ruling asked whether item 52 already makes the reading safe with no new field (`ops/reviews/s1-04-design/check-merge.json`, its open question on T-1187's form): since v1.11 a turn's business comes only from the sender's own roles and companies, and a company at a business whose live owner role another person holds answers `anotherOwner`. It does not, for four reasons, each from the frozen text or the code on main:

1. **Item 52 decides a turn's business from its sender; item 44 reads a row's recipient.** They need not be the same person: a section 1 line can go to a third party (S1-77's line to the owner a helper names, T-1184's security note d), and `readOutboxRow` gives "the row's own recipient, never another person on the handle" (v1.5 `rowReader.request`). And a row need not come from a turn at all: a clock firing commits as item 1 does with no party read (section "v1.17", "Who calls commitTurn"), and K-04 v1.1 holds a row with no turn.
2. **`anotherOwner` is answered only where a company of the sender's names the business** (item 52's first case; `db/repo/turn-party.ts` `decide`, lines 184 to 188). A sender who holds a live staff, admin or customer role at a business takes it by that role (item 52: "A person with one live role: its business"; `decide`, lines 202 to 216), and that business has a live owner. A row such a turn writes on a section 1 step to a person with no role there would be read as the owner's.
3. **The party is read once, before the turn's texts are opened; the row is read later**, at compose input and again at finalize, each in its own transaction. Between them the owner state can change: journey 1 is to write the owner's role at S1-09 or S1-35 (open gap 58), and a hand-over starts the new owner's role (S11-100; open gap 59). `row-reader-s1-02-sam-after-hand-over` is that case: v1.11 stops Sam's next turn (`read-turn-party-handed-over`), never a row already written.
4. **The row binding reads nothing of the party.** `readerOfRow` and `rowReaderOf` (`actions/src/tool-api/turn-face/rows.ts`, lines 262 to 321) map from `readOutboxRow`'s answer and the row's bundle alone, under the business the compose or finalize call names, so nothing ties that business to a party read.

So a design record could not prove the reading safe line by line, and the condition is checked where the reading is made: at the row's read, on the row's business. Item 52 and item 80 guard different moments, and neither stands in for the other (open gap 95).

### What v1.20 does not name

- **Whether a live owner role exists apart from the owner's handle** (open gap 94): `readBusinessOwner` answers null for an owner who writes from no handle, and v1.20 adds no read.
- **Who writes the owner's role** (open gaps 58 and 95): until journey 1's lane writes it, a business a sign-up made has no live owner role, and item 80 refuses nothing there.
- **Whose company names the row's business:** the mapping reads no company. A person with no role is read by a sign-up line at a business with no live owner whether or not a company of theirs names it, as item 44 reads S1-77's third-party owner (default 191).
- **The outbox row's own step and line** (open gap 29): T-1474's version, v1.22 (DL-1030), restates `rowLine` and with it `rowReader.request`, from v1.20's (below), and the commit's definitions from v1.21's (section "v1.21").
- **T-1421's flags and messages reads, T-1155's arrival read, T-1177's outcome per answered label, the party of a handle with a second company (the S1-04 design ruling's card 17), `promoted` by handle id (T-1426), CQ-211's note on `markPart`, CQ-234, CQ-235's note a, CQ-263, CQ-293 and CQ-289's descriptions** (open gap 76): each at the next free number at its start, since v1.20 keeps to its card (DL-999).

### Consumers of v1.20

Each moves to v1.20 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.19 consumers keep working, and until T-1497 merges the turn face maps by v1.5, with no owner condition (open gap 96).

- **Action-layer engineer:** C-074 the Tool API turn face, T-1497 (card 28 of the S1-04 design ruling: `actions/src/tool-api/turn-face/rows.ts` and `pg/calls.ts`, with their tests), whose compose-input and finalize row ports (C-090, C-093) map the reader by item 80.
- **Database engineer:** nothing to build: C-152's `readBusinessOwner` (T-1161) and `readOutboxRow` (T-1138) answer as they do, and v1.20 needs no migration.
- **QA:** the contract test `data-access.v1.20`, a new task (the S1-04 design ruling's card 26), for C-074: its fixture part joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts`. v1.20 has no database side.

What the new tasks must hold to:

- **T-1497** maps exactly as `rowReader.ownerExchange` does. `readerOfRow` makes the owner read only for a recipient who is a person, holds no role and is on a section 1 step, under the row's business, through `readBusinessOwner` as item 42 runs it, and hands the state to `rowReaderOf`; an owner or `moreThanOneOwner` is `live`, null is `none`, and `notFound`, `transient` or a thrown read refuse the row, never `none`. It makes the read at compose input and at finalize each, keeps nothing between calls, and never takes the state from its caller. A row with no business states `none` with no read (the turn face answers `notBuilt` for such a row today). It logs no owner's person or handle. It tests each v1.20 fixture's request and outcome, the four refused twins put right, and what no fixture can show: a failed owner read refusing the row at compose input and at finalize, and a row that composed with no owner refused at finalize once an owner role exists.
- **QA's task** lists each refused fixture's mend (its one fault and its twin, as section "Fixtures", v1.20, names them), adds v1.20 to the fixture part's versions with its side (C-074), and shows the rules no fixture shows, as it did for v1.5's (T-1184's security note b): with `businessOwner` `live`, a section 1 line to staff or to cust, and one whose `lineTo` is null, each `businessHasOwner` (rule 7 comes before the line: default 187); a row with no person, `noRole`; and each broken by one rule alone, by its number.
- **T-1474** (v1.22, open gap 29; DL-1030) restates `rowReader.request`, `rowReader.exchange` and `rowReader.ownerExchange` from v1.20's definitions, `businessOwner`, `businessHasOwner` and rules 3 to 7 kept, never from v1.5's or v1.19's: `rowLine` changes there, and a restatement from the older definition would drop item 80.

**The tests on this branch:** none meets a v1.20 fixture it cannot answer. The eight are listed under `rowReader.ownerExchange`, which no loop selects (the turn face's own tests name v1.5's three accepted and two refused files and compile v1.5's `rowReader.exchange`: `actions/src/tool-api/turn-face/calls-test-support.ts`); `rowReader` is no database call, so no db/repo test runs one; and `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts` checks every fixture of every version against its schema (parts 1, 3 and 4), so it checks the eight, while its part 2 mends and side list are QA's v1.20 task.

## v1.21 (T-1508): a commit asks one open question again, its ask count raised by one

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): on staging an owner's typed answer to S1-02 ended `asked` and Rosie sent nothing (DL-1014). K-09 v1.12 (T-1504, frozen on its branch: `../tool-api/README.md` section "v1.12", defaults 192 to 198) has the commit send the turn's one open question again, once, in one row, with the question's `ask_count` raised in the same commit, so a second unclear answer finds 2 and gets nothing (its default 194: the cap DL-1018 section C's checker asked for). No frozen K-05 version lets a commit raise `ask_count`: v1's `stateWrite` refuses `open_questions` (its `stateTable`), `openQuestionClose` only closes, and v1.1 to v1.20 keep both (K-09 v1.12 open gap 173; T-1504's NEED, carded as T-1508 and T-1509: DL-1030). **Kind:** additive, a minor version, v1.21, the next free number after T-1187's v1.20 (DL-1030: T-1474 takes v1.22); no CTO OK is needed and the 7-day rule does not apply. `data-access.v1.21.schema.json` holds every v1.20 definition under its own name, by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.20.schema.json` (which refers on to every earlier version), all twenty-one files as they are; it restates five, the rule of section "v1.1"'s **Kind**: `writes` (`openQuestions` takes the optional `askAgain`; every other list v1.1's own, by reference), `commitTurn.request` (to refer to v1.21's `writes`; `turn` and `versionsRead` v1.1's and `answerPhoneRowsBy` v1.17's, by reference), `commitTurn.errors` (v1.20's by reference, and `wrongState` for an open question), `commitTurn.exchange` (to refer to both; v1.17's handle-id rule, its `if` and `then`, by reference) and `commitTurn.handleIdExchange` (to narrow v1.21's exchange; v1.17's second part by reference). It adds three: `openQuestionAskAgain`, `openQuestionWrongStateError` and `commitTurn.askAgainExchange`, `commitTurn.exchange` narrowed by `allOf` to a request that raises. So every v1 to v1.20 exchange is a v1.21 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.21): none carries `askAgain`, and without it the exchange is v1.20's. `fireClock` and every other call are unchanged. **No schema change:** `ask_count` is K-04 v1's (`open_questions.ask_count`, integer, not null, default 1, at least 1: migration 0009), and the raise is an update of a table `rosie_job` already updates (0012); no table, column, grant, index or migration. **Widened before launch** (the card's Lead note 1; DL-1033): this section also states, as README defaults with no new shape, K-05's half of the repeated-lines fix (DL-1032; `ops/reviews/live-reply-faults/rule-repeat.json` and its check, `check-repeat.json`, whose corrected ruling wins): `writePlan` writes K-04's opener mark, which closes open gap 37, and `readFixedLineFacts` reads past a mark whose row never went; `markPart` writes the opt-out line's delivery time; and default 50 counts either opener id (defaults 200 to 205). **Not here:** db/repo's raise (T-1509: `db/repo/commit-turn.ts`, and a firing's refusal of `askAgain` in `db/repo/clocks.ts` `checkFire`: default 199); db/repo's opener mark, its fallback and the opt-out time (T-1513: `db/repo/outbox.ts`, `db/repo/turn-face-reads.ts`); the commit's row and raise (T-1505: `actions/src/commit`); K-09 v1.12 (T-1504); QA's contract test; the outbox row's own step and line (open gap 29: T-1474, v1.22).

### The call v1.21 changes

`commitTurn` with `writes.openQuestions.askAgain` (item 81) is v1's call (item 1) with one more write: the same role and scope (`rosie_job`, per write: the raise under the question's own business, from the row as locked), the same repeat (a repeated turn id answers its first commit and raises nothing: `written` empty) and the same time budget (inside "Gate and commit", 300 ms: arch 04 line 223), with no read beyond the row it locks. Without the list it is v1.20's call, answer included. A firing raises nothing (default 199).

### How the raise works (K-04 v1 as migrated)

81. **`commitTurn` asks one open question again** (K-09 v1.12 defaults 192 and 194 and its open gap 173; T-1504's NEED; DL-1030). `writes.openQuestions` gains one optional list, `askAgain`, of at most one raise (`openQuestionAskAgain`): the question's `id` and `expectVersion`, the version the turn read it at. Left out, the call is v1.20's, byte for byte. Given, the call runs exactly as item 1 says, one transaction on the primary, uncached, as `rosie_job` per write, with the raise among its writes:
    1. **Locked and compared with the commit's other rows.** The raise names an `open_questions` row the commit writes, so it is locked `FOR UPDATE` with every row the commit compares, once each, in (table, row id) order (item 1), and its `expectVersion` is compared as an update's: a question whose version moved since the turn read it refuses the commit with `versionChanged`, listed in `changed` with the version read and the version now, as limit 4 lists every row that changed (arch 06 line 305; `commit-s1-02-sam-asks-again-stale`). A question the request names that does not exist, or one the same commit opens, which is not there when the locks are taken, is a request this layer cannot carry out: the call throws and writes nothing (db/repo's default 1).
    2. **Live, or refused `wrongState`.** At the version read the question must be live, `waiting` or `open` (K-04 v1 `open_questions_status_known`; arch 09 line 490). An answered, defaulted or withdrawn question refuses the commit with `wrongState`, its `table` `open_questions`, its `rowId` the question and its `state` its status now (`openQuestionWrongStateError`; `commit-a7-03-alex-asks-again-defaulted`). The order is item 1's with one step more: `reservationLost` (limit 5), then `versionChanged` (limit 4), then `wrongState`, an effect let go's (v1.1 item 18) before the raise's (default 193). Nothing is written on any refusal.
    3. **The write.** After the questions it opens and closes, the commit raises the question's `ask_count` by one and its `version` by one, where its version is the one read and its status live (`update open_questions set ask_count = ask_count + 1, version = <read> + 1 where id = <id> and version = <read> and status in ('waiting', 'open')`, which 0009's `enforce_version_raise` trigger takes). Nothing else of the question moves (default 195). The count written is the stored count plus one, which the compare makes the count the turn read plus one, so the raise names the version and never the count (default 192). The request's event-log entries are written as item 1 writes them, among them the one `open_question.asked_again` entry the caller lists for the raise (default 196).
    4. **The answer.** `committed.written` names the question, `open_questions`, at its version after the raise, after the questions the commit opened and closed (default 198); nothing else in the answer changes. A repeat of the turn id answers its first commit with `written` empty, as item 1 does, so a rerun never raises twice.
    5. **One raise a commit, and never beside a close** (default 194). `askAgain` holds exactly one raise when given (`minItems` 1, `maxItems` 1), and a commit that raises closes no question (`openQuestions.close` empty when `askAgain` is given: the `if` and `then` on `writes.openQuestions`). So the same question raised twice (`refuse-commit-s1-02-asks-again-twice`) and a question raised and closed together (`refuse-commit-s1-02-asks-again-and-closes`) are requests the schema refuses: db/repo throws on each before any statement reaches the database (its default 1), writing nothing, never `versionChanged` or `wrongState`, which name the record's state and send the caller to reload, while these contradict themselves and a reload changes nothing.

    **Why this name** (the card: "name it in the schema's style, e.g. `ask`"): each of `writes`' lists is a move on the rows it names (`open` and `close`; `make` and `cancel`; `confirm` and `release`; `write` and `letGo`), and the move here is asking a question again: K-08 v1's kind for it is `open_question.asked_again`, and K-09 v1.12's unit names "asked again". `ask` alone would read as K-02's verb `ask`, which writes nothing (K-09 v1.12 default 195). The item is `openQuestionAskAgain`, as `openQuestionNew` and `openQuestionClose` name theirs.

    **The schema holds the rule:** `openQuestionAskAgain` is closed (`additionalProperties` false) and names no count; `askAgain` holds one raise, and with it `close` is empty; `commitTurn.errors` takes the open question's `wrongState`, whose `state` is never `waiting` or `open`. That the raised question exists, is live at its version and is not one the same commit opens are the producer's to check against the database (T-1509), as item 1's compares are. As in v1.17, the generated types leave the `if` and `then` out, so a consumer proves its requests on the fixtures.

### What a raise moves, and what it never moves (the card's Gives 2)

- **Moved by the raise:** `ask_count`, by one, and `version`, by one (K-04 v1 rule 2), both set in one statement at the version read.
- **Moved later, by the row it rides with, never by the raise:** the question's send time and poll. K-04 keeps neither on the question: each is read from the parts of the outbox rows that ask it, to its own handle, of its business, the latest part that went `sent` or `delivered` (v1.2 default 41; `db/migrations/0037_core_question_went_out.sql` lines 55 to 62, `open_questions_went_out`; `db/repo/targets.ts`). So the send time becomes the re-sent row's once a part of that row is sent (or, with no acceptance recorded, delivered), never at the commit, and stays the first row's until then, or for good if the re-sent row never goes (withdrawn, failed, dropped for a stop). K-09 v1.12's default 197 rules that move safe for the questions it asks again, which carry no read-back (its default 193 (2)), and default 41 foresaw it ("a question asked again (`ask_count` 2 or more) gives its newest send and poll"). A poll's id would move the same way; no question K-09 v1.12 asks again holds options (its default 193).
- **Never moved:** the question's `status` (it stays `waiting` or `open`, so it stays live on the handle, `open_questions_handle_live_idx`, and answerable), `options`, `readback`, `versions_read`, `clock` (its Clock line), `bundle_hash` and `bundle_build`, `turn_id` (the turn that first asked it), `person_id`, `business_id`, `booking_id`, `step`, `answered_option_id` and `closed_at`; and its clock row (`clock-<question id>`), which the commit neither makes, cancels, arms nor moves. The re-sent row's first delivery receipt arms that clock row only if no receipt came before; otherwise `armClock` answers `repeated` and moves nothing (item 5: "a later receipt never moves it"; K-09 v1.12 default 196), so the question's due time and safe default stay as they were.

### The event-log entry (the card's Gives 3)

**Ruled: yes, one entry per raise; no K-08 change, so no NEED.** K-08 v1 already holds the kind: its closed list's `open_question` subject has the moves "opened, delivered, asked_again, answered, defaulted, withdrawn", sourced to arch 09 line 490's ask count (`../event-trace/README.md`, "Event kinds (the closed list)", line 66; `../event-trace/event-trace.v1.schema.json`, `open_question.asked_again` in its `kind` enum). The card's default (no entry) rested on K-08 having no kind for the raise, which it has had since v1, so CQ-56's rule (a kind comes with the version that first writes it) asks nothing more: v1.21 is the first version that writes it, and K-08 v1 already lists it. And K-08's first rule asks for the entry: "Every write appends a row; none is ever edited" (`../event-trace/README.md` line 39), and the raise writes a versioned row (`ask_count` and `version`), which the outbox row's own entry (`outbox_row.created`) does not record. **The entry** (default 196): kind `open_question.asked_again`; `tableName` `open_questions` and `rowId` the question; `oldValues` `{"ask_count": <read>}` and `newValues` `{"ask_count": <read + 1>}`; actor `rosie`, no person and no role (what the commit writes because of a person's text is Rosie's: K-08 "Who"); `why` `{"step": <the question's step>}`; `approvedBy`, `sealed` and `proofs` null; `businessId` the question's and its zone the business's; its id `log-<question id>-asked_again`, by K-09 v1.5 default 131's `log-<row id>-<move>`. Like every entry of a commit it comes in the request's `writes.events`, listed by the caller beside its raise (as `actions/src/commit/build.ts` lists `open_question.opened` beside each question it opens), and item 1 writes it with the turn's id, trace id and bundle; K-05 checks no entry against its write, as it checks none today. Under K-09 v1.12's cap a question is asked again once, so its one entry's id meets no other; a later rule that asks a question a third time (K-09 v1.12 open gap 175) names its entries anew.

### The repeated lines: writePlan, markPart and readFixedLineFacts (the card's Lead note 1, Gives 5 to 7)

The user's live reply carried the opener, the STOP line and the privacy link again (DL-1032), because nothing outside tests writes the two facts that switch them off: K-04's opener mark (`openers_sent`; `db/repo/threads.ts` `markOpenerSent`, unused) and `phones.optout_line_delivered_at` (`db/repo/phones.ts` `recordOptoutLineDelivered`, unused). So `readFixedLineFacts` (item 36) answers "never texted" and "never delivered" for every row, and the inserter adds all three (`actions/src/finalize/fixed-lines/insert.ts` `firstTextLines`, lines 62 to 69). The ruling and its check (`ops/reviews/live-reply-faults/rule-repeat.json`, `check-repeat.json`; DL-1033 ruling 4) give each fact a writer inside a call that already holds the row. No request, answer or error changes shape, so these are README defaults, as T-1499's default 191 is for K-09, and v1.21's schema restates none of these calls (defaults 200 to 205; T-1513 builds them: "Consumers of v1.21"):

- **`writePlan` writes the opener mark** (defaults 200 and 201): in its own transaction, after its update, only for a row with a business and a turn whose final parts carry an opener (`opener` or `signUpOpener`: default 204), `on conflict do nothing`, so the first row planned to carry it is named, once, with its turn's commit time. **Closes open gap 37.**
- **`readFixedLineFacts` counts no earlier row that never went, in both branches** (default 202): with a business, a mark naming a row that ended withdrawn, failed, dropped_stopped or superseded falls back to the earliest earlier row to the same handle and business that carries an opener and did not end so, the shape of the no-business branch; with no business, that branch's earlier row is the earliest that carries an opener and did not end so. Never a state filter without that fallback.
- **`markPart` writes the opt-out line's delivery time** (default 203): on a newly delivered words part of a row whose final parts carry `optOutLine`, the recipient phone's `optout_line_delivered_at`, once, with its version raised, the phone locked last.
- **No backfill** (default 205): rows already sent keep no mark and no time, so the next text to a handle after T-1513 deploys may carry the three lines one last time (DL-1033 ruling 6).

No fixture shows them: their exchanges' requests and answers are as before, and a new fixture under `writePlan.exchange`, `markPart.exchange` or `readFixedLineFacts.exchange` would be met by the db/repo loops that select those definitions (`db/repo/outbox.int.test.ts` lines 145 and 1512; `db/repo/turn-face-reads.int.test.ts` line 113) before T-1513 builds them. T-1513 proves each on local PostgreSQL ("Consumers of v1.21").

- **Which questions may be asked again** (default 197): K-09 v1.12's (its default 193: no options as sent, no read-back, no booking; its open gap 175), never K-05's.

- **The opener when finalizes race or come out of order** (open gap 99).
- **A held or sending row counted before it goes** (open gap 100).
- **K-29's README note on the opt-out line's delivery** (`../db-schema/conversations-consent/README.md` line 72 names `phones.optout_line_delivered_at` and no writer): a README change outside this card's folder, a new task.

- **The points open gap 76 lists** (T-1421's flags and messages reads, T-1155's arrival read, T-1177's outcome per answered label, `promoted` by handle id, CQ-211, CQ-234, CQ-235's note a, CQ-263, CQ-293 and CQ-289's descriptions): each at the next free number at its start, since v1.21 keeps to its card (DL-1030, DL-1033).

## v1.22 (T-1474): an outbox row carries its own step and line

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): S1-08 asks no question, and `readOutboxRow` takes a row's line only from the question it asks (item 35, default 48; `db/repo/turn-face-reads.ts` lines 772 to 776), so S1-08's row answers `line` null; the turn face refuses a null line at compose input and at finalize (`actions/src/tool-api/turn-face/rows.ts` lines 303 and 378), so the row keeps no plan; and a row with no final parts stops the claim's walk for its recipient (item 3), with S1-09's question the very next row (the S1-04 design ruling's decision (e), `ops/reviews/s1-04-design/check-merge.json`, its card 5; DL-999). Open gap 29 named the fix: K-04 stores both (K-04 core v1.2, T-1472: `outbox_rows.step` and `line_no`, both or neither; migration 0039, T-1473, merged), and a K-05 minor version in which `commitTurn`'s `outboxRowNew` takes both and `readOutboxRow` reads them first. **Kind:** additive, a minor version, v1.22, the number free at its start after T-1508's v1.21 (the card's Lead note 1; DL-1030); no CTO OK is needed and the 7-day rule does not apply. `data-access.v1.22.schema.json` holds every v1.21 definition under its own name, by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.21.schema.json` (which refers on to every earlier version), all twenty-two files as they are, and restates thirteen (six for the commit, seven for the read), the rule of section "v1.1"'s **Kind**: for the commit, `outboxRowNew` (two optional fields; v1's ten by reference, and v1's rule that a text row is no copy), `writes` (its `outboxRows`; `openQuestions` v1.21's own by reference, so `askAgain` with its `if` and `then` is kept, and every other list v1.1's), `commitTurn.request`, `commitTurn.exchange` (v1.21's errors, `openQuestionWrongStateError` among them, and v1.17's handle-id rule, by reference), `commitTurn.handleIdExchange` and `commitTurn.askAgainExchange` (each narrowing v1.22's exchange, its second part v1.17's or v1.21's own), all from v1.21's definitions as section "v1.21" asks, never from v1.1's or v1.17's; for the read, `rowLine` (`from` gains `row`), `readOutboxRow.response`, `readOutboxRow.v1dot6Response` and `readOutboxRow.exchange` (to refer to v1.22's `rowLine`; every other field v1.6's by reference), `rowReader.request` (its `line`; the other six v1.5's and `businessOwner` v1.20's, by reference), `rowReader.exchange` and `rowReader.ownerExchange` (v1.20's rules 0 to 7 and narrowing, by reference), all from v1.20's definitions as section "v1.20" asks, never from v1.5's or v1.19's. The frozen schema's own `description` names the same thirteen but counts them "the twelve it restates", one short; it stays as it is, frozen, and the count is the thirteen named here and in v1.22's change-log row (T-1523). It adds one: `commitTurn.rowLineExchange`, `commitTurn.exchange` narrowed by `allOf` to a request in which an outbox row names its step, the v1.22 commit fixtures' definition. So every v1 to v1.21 exchange is a v1.22 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.22). **No schema change here:** the columns are K-04 core v1.2's, migrated by 0039; no table, column, grant, index or migration. **Not here:** db/repo's write and read (T-1476: `db/repo/commit-turn.ts`, `db/repo/turn-face-reads.ts`); the turn face's row types (T-1475: `actions/src/tool-api/turn-face/rows.ts`); the walk that writes the pair on each row (T-1479); open gap 30's `fact_slots` and `about` and CQ-204's `first_delivered_key` (DL-999 decision (e)); QA's contract test.

### The calls v1.22 changes

`commitTurn` with a row's `step` and `lineNo` (item 82) is v1's call (item 1) with one more pair of columns on each outbox row it inserts: the same role and scope (`rosie_job`, per write), the same repeat (a repeated turn id answers its first commit and writes nothing), the same time budget (inside "Gate and commit", 300 ms: arch 04 line 223) and the same answer. `readOutboxRow` (item 83) is v1.3's read (item 35) with two more columns of the row it already reads: the same role, scope, transaction and answer shape. Without the fields each is v1.21's call. `fireClock` and every other call are unchanged (default 211).

### How v1.22 works (K-04 core v1.2 as migrated)

82. **`commitTurn` writes a row's step and line** (open gap 29; DL-999 decision (e); the card's Gives 1). Each `writes.outboxRows` item (`outboxRowNew`) may name `step`, a K-01 step id, the step whose line the row carries to its reader, and `lineNo`, that line's index among the step's lines from Rosie to that reader, in the step's say order, 0 the first (default 207), both or neither (default 206). Left out, the call is v1.21's, byte for byte, and the row holds both null. Given, the commit inserts them as `outbox_rows.step` and `line_no` in the insert that writes the row's other columns, under the row's own business (item 1), and nothing else changes: no read, no lock, no compare, no entry and no field of the answer; `committed.outboxRows` names the row by its id and commit order as before (`commit-s1-04-sam-lines`). A request with one and not the other is one the schema refuses (`refuse-commit-s1-04-sam-step-without-line`), and db/repo throws on it before any statement reaches the database (db/repo's default 1), writing nothing: never written half, and never answered `constraint`, which names the record, while this request contradicts itself. Were it to reach the database, K-04's `outbox_rows_step_with_line` would refuse it (23514) and the commit would answer `constraint`, all or nothing. A step not in K-01's form (the schema's `stepId`; K-04's `outbox_rows_step_form`) and a negative line (`minimum` 0; `outbox_rows_line_no_not_negative`) are refused the same way. The commit does not check the step against the turn's bundle, the line against the step's lines, or a row's step against the step of the question it asks (defaults 208 and 209): those are the walk's (T-1479).
83. **`readOutboxRow` answers the row's own line first** (open gap 29, closed; the card's Gives 3 and 4). In item 35's one read of `outbox_rows`, the read also selects `step` and `line_no`, and answers `line`:
    1. **from the row**, `{"step": <step>, "index": <line_no>, "from": "row"}`, when the row holds them (both or neither, by K-04's CHECK), whether or not it asks a question (default 208; `read-outbox-row-s1-08-sam`);
    2. else **from its question**, as v1.3 does: `{"step": <open_questions.step>, "index": 0, "from": "openQuestion"}`, when it asks one (`read-outbox-row-s1-02-older-row`);
    3. else **null**: a row that holds neither and asks no question, which compose input and finalize still refuse, never guess (default 48; `read-outbox-row-no-question`, frozen and unchanged).

    Nothing else in the answer changes. A row with no turn is still `notFound` before anything of it is read (item 65), so its columns are never selected; a safety row is still `fixedLineRow`; `slotSource.question` is still null for a row that asks no question, so finalize's question is null for it, with no ask-by (item 35's table, row `FinalizeRow.question`), and default 54 still holds for a row that asks one. The read selects no words, address or number (item 35's reason 1): the two columns are a step id and an index (audience `never`: K-04 core v1.2 default 29).

**The schema holds the form:** `outboxRowNew` is closed, `dependentRequired` ties `step` to `lineNo` and back, and `rowLine.from` is `openQuestion` or `row`. That `row` is answered whenever the row stores a step, and `openQuestion` only when it stores none, is the producer's to keep (T-1476), as each of item 35's fields is: a schema cannot compare an answer with the database. As in v1.17 and v1.21, the generated types do not hold every rule of the schema (they leave `outboxRowNew`'s `if` and `then` out, as v1's), so a consumer proves its requests against the schema and the fixtures.

### What each consumer reads

- **The turn face** (T-1475): a line `from` `row` is taken as one `from` `openQuestion` is, field by field into `ComposeRow.step` and `line` and `FinalizeRow.step` (item 35's table, rows `step` and `line`), and a null line is still refused. So S1-08's row reaches compose input, and finalize with question null and no ask-by.
- **The reader mapping** (`rowReader`, items 44 and 80): its request's `line` is v1.22's `rowLine`, so a line from the row is mapped as any line is. Its rules read `section` and `lineTo`, never `from` (default 214), so S1-08's row, a section 1 line to the owner, is read by the sign-up line for a person with no role at a business with no live owner and refused `businessHasOwner` at one with (item 80), exactly as S1-02's row is. T-1475's security review covers it (its card: "the sign-up reader now reads a row with no question").
- **The walk** (T-1479): writes the pair on every row it writes (its Gives 1 and 2), checks its exchanges by `commitTurn.rowLineExchange`, and moves `actions/src/commit/types.ts`'s `OutboxRowNew` off K-05 v1.1 (the design ruling's decision (e), item 6).

### What v1.22 does not name

- **A firing's rows** (open gap 101): `fireClock.request` keeps v1.1's `writes` (default 211).
- **A row's step against its question's step** (open gap 102): not compared (default 208).

## v1.23 (T-1531): a final refusal is parked at once, with the passedOn line and Rosie's team's record

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): T-1529's ruling (`ops/reviews/refusal-reply/rule-refusal.json`, its card 2; DL-1142, DL-1144): a refused walk answer gets a reply, not three retries and silence. A final refusal (the ruling's ruling 1) is parked at once, never resent or rerun, and the owner gets one line. v1's `parkTurn` cannot do that: it takes only tries 3 (`data-access.v1.schema.json` line 2277), its one row is written with no plan and nothing words it (`runtime/src/turn/park.ts`, its "Not here", line 24), and the claim passes such a row for good (default 183; `db/repo/outbox.ts` lines 1346 to 1348), so the person hears nothing, after a third crash too. The ruling asks for a K-05 minor version: `parkTurn` takes tries 1 to 3 and a reason naming the refusal, and the park writes K-37's `parked_turns` row, with the passedOn fixed line, word for word, as the standing default for the words, sent only together with that row so the promise has a team record behind it; a park after a third crash sends the same line (its card 7). **Kind:** additive, a minor version, v1.23, the next free number after T-1474's v1.22 (`frozen.json`; the card); no CTO OK is needed and the 7-day rule does not apply. `data-access.v1.23.schema.json` holds every v1.22 definition under its own name, by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.22.schema.json` (which refers on to every earlier version), all twenty-three files as they are, and restates two: `parkTurn.request` (v1's form, by reference to v1.22's, which is v1's, or v1.23's `parkTurn.lineRequest`) and `parkTurn.exchange` (to refer to v1.23's request; `call` and `given` v1's own and the response and errors v1.22's, each by reference). It adds six: `parkReason`, `parkedLinePlan`, `parkedLineParts`, `parkedRowNew`, `parkTurn.lineRequest` and `parkTurn.lineExchange` (`parkTurn.exchange` narrowed by `allOf` to v1.23's request, the v1.23 fixtures' definition). So every v1 to v1.22 exchange is a v1.23 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.23), `park-turn` among them. **No schema change here:** K-04's `turns_tries_range` (0009) already allows tries 1 to 3, K-37 v1's `parked_turns` is migrated (0014), and `rosie_job` holds its SELECT, INSERT and UPDATE; no table, column, grant, index or migration. **Not here:** K-09's `turnRefused` and notBuilt as final (the ruling's card 1, K-09 v1.13); db/repo's park at v1.23 (card 4, `db/repo/park.ts`); the Tool API base answering `turnRefused` (card 5); the runtime parking a final answer at once and the crash park's line (cards 6 and 7: `runtime/src/turn/park.ts`, `runtime/src/actor/lease.ts`); QA's contract tests (card 3) and the end-to-end test (card 8); Jay's words for the park (open gap 103).

### The call v1.23 changes

`parkTurn` in v1.23's form (item 84) is v1's call (item 12) with a reason, any try, the line in its one row and the team's record stated: the same role and scope (runtime, `rosie_job` with no business scope), the same repeat (a parked turn answers its first park, `repeated`; a committed one `alreadyCommitted`), the same time budget (none in the sources) and the same answer. In v1's form it is item 12's call, byte for byte. Every other call is unchanged.

### How v1.23 works (K-04 v1 and K-37 v1 as migrated)

84. **`parkTurn` parks a turn at once on a final refusal, or after a third crash, with the passedOn line and Rosie's team's record** (T-1529's ruling 2; arch 11 line 576's park, applied sooner; the card's Gives). The request is v1's form (item 12) or v1.23's (`parkTurn.lineRequest`): the turn with its tries, 1 to 3; `parkedAt`; `reason`, the refusal's labels (`parkReason`: `from`, `fault` and `ids`, each a K-08 `safeId`), required before the third try and allowed on it (default 218); exactly one row (`parkedRowNew`) with its plan and final parts; and the entries. In one transaction on the runtime side, all or nothing, in this order:
    1. **The turn:** inserted as `parked` with its tries and `parked_at`, as item 12 does. A turn id met again answers its first park, `repeated`, with its outbox rows by turn id in commit order, whatever the repeat's body or form, and writes nothing (default 223); a committed turn answers `alreadyCommitted` and is never parked.
    2. **Rosie's team's record:** K-37 v1's `parked_turns` row for the turn, `open`, with `error_summary` built from the reason's labels and the try in default 220's fixed form (db/repo's fixed crash summary when there is no reason), `reply_due_at` 1 business day after `parked_at` and `person_told_at` null, as db/repo writes v1's park's row today (`db/repo/park.ts` defaults 2 and 3; open gap 104). K-37's rule 5 (the row's turn is `parked`) holds by step 1.
    3. **The one row:** inserted `planned` at version 1 under the turn's id, bundle and trace id (`commit_seq` from K-04's identity column), then its plan and final parts written and moved to `checked` at version 2, K-07's two moves (`(new) → planned`, `planned → checked`), as item 59 writes a sign-in code's email row, so it never stands without final parts (default 221). Its words are K-01's passedOn line, word for word (`parkedLineParts`; default 216); it asks no question, so no clock row and no open question is written or moved. A row that holds a plan is no parked turn's unplanned row, so default 183 does not pass it: the claim walks and claims it like any `checked` row, in commit order (items 3 and 13), and limit 8 decides at send (a stopped number's row is dropped: RULES 7.2; a quick reply is never held for text hours: RULES 4.3).
    4. **The entries:** the request's, each under the turn's id, trace id and bundle, at `parkedAt`, as item 12 writes them: one `turn.parked` (default 222).

    The answer is v1's (`parked`, `turnId`, `parkedAt`, the row with its commit order, `repeated`). What the call checks before any statement (db/repo throws, writing nothing, as `writePlan`'s default 3 does): the plan's and the final parts' `row` and `channel` are the row's, and the row's recipient is the turn's handle (default 219). What it does not check: whether the refusal is final (the runtime's, by the ruling's ruling 1: default 218), the reason against the turn, or the words against the turn's pinned bundle (the schema holds them: default 216).

**Nothing of the refused part is written** (the ruling's ruling 3): the request carries the refusal as labels alone (`parkReason`, each a K-08 `safeId`, so no words, number or address: `refuse-park-turn-sam-refused-reason-words`); the row's words are the fixed line, a constant of the schema, never the model's or a slot's (`refuse-park-turn-sam-refused-line-reworded`); the team's summary is built from those labels alone; the entry's `why` holds the same labels. No open question, booking or state row moves: the turn committed nothing. Logs and spans carry labels only, as the park's do today (`runtime/src/turn/park.ts`; K-08).

**The schema holds the form:** `parkTurn.lineRequest` is closed and its `if` and `then` require the reason before the third try; `parkedRowNew` pins the row's class, booking, question, copy and versions read; `parkedLineParts` pins the words. That the plan's row is the row, that the recipient is the turn's handle, that `error_summary` follows default 220 and that the row is planned then checked are the producer's to keep (card 4), as each of item 12's writes is. As in v1.17, v1.21 and v1.22, the generated types do not hold every rule of the schema (they leave the `if` and `then` out), so a consumer proves its requests against the schema and the fixtures.

### What v1.23 does not name

- **Jay's words for the park** (open gap 103): passedOn stands in, word for word (default 216).
- **When the person counts as told** (open gap 104): `parked_turns.person_told_at` stays null at the park, and no call sets it when the row goes.
- **The first-text lines** (open gap 105): a park's row that is the number's first text from Rosie carries passedOn alone, without the opener, the opt-out line or the privacy-notice link (default 224).
- **K-07's and K-08's names for the park's writes** (open gap 106): K-07's move table names `commitTurn` and `writePlan` as the two moves' callers, and K-08's `why` has no key for a refusal or a crash (v1's `outage` among them).
- **Whether an owner's malformed answer is asked again instead of parked** (the ruling's second need, for Jay): the runtime's and K-09's to rule; K-05 takes the reason it is given.
- **The points open gap 76 lists** and the versions "Producers and consumers" names next: each at the next free number at its start.

Each moves to v1.23 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.22 consumers keep working. Until card 4 merges, db/repo's `parkTurn` takes only v1's form (`db/repo/park.ts` `checkPark`, lines 72 to 91: `checkTurn` with tries 3, and `checkOutboxRowNew`, which refuses a row with a plan or final parts), so a request in v1.23's form throws a `TypeError` before the database is reached, writing nothing, and no consumer sends one before card 4 merges (cards 6 and 7 depend on it).

## Fixtures (`fixtures/`, listed in `fixtures/index.json`)

Each accepted fixture is one exchange (`<call>.exchange`): `call`, `given`, `request` and `outcome` (the response or one of the errors). `given` says what the exchange assumes, so QA's contract test can build it: `k04Seeds` (K-04's seed fixtures, each loaded with its own `after` chain), `after` (exchanges of this folder that run first, in order, each with its own `given`), `now` (the fake clock) and a `note`. `commitSeq` values assume a fresh database, so the test compares their order, not their numbers. Ids the call makes are fixed by rule: a reservation's saved-time clock row is `<reservation id>/saved-time`.

Accepted (38): `commit-first`, `commit-repeat-same-turn`, `commit-version-changed`, `commit-reservation-lost`, `commit-opens-question` (the tour's A7-03), `write-plan`, `write-plan-repeat`, `write-plan-owner`, `unplanned-after-crash`, `claim-in-order`, `mark-sent`, `mark-delivered`, `reserve-10-min`, `reserve-ai-door-15-min`, `reserve-overlap-refused`, `reserve-next-in-line`, `reserve-third-refused`, `release-expired-promotes`, `extend-once`, `extend-ai-door-refused`, `arm-clock`, `cancel-clock`, `due-for-handle`, `due-for-sweep`, `fire-once`, `fire-repeat`, the evening chain behind `claim-held-lets-later-pass` (`commit-evening-notice`, `write-plan-evening-notice`, `claim-evening-notice`, `mark-held-for-text-hours`, `commit-sam-quick-reply`, `write-plan-sam-quick-reply`, `claim-held-lets-later-pass`), `publish-bundle-copy`, `set-active-bundle`, `active-bundle`, `bundle-value-on-miss`, `park-turn`. That is 02-contracts K-05's 22 v1 fixtures, with `due-for-handle` and `due-for-sweep` split by caller as listed, plus the repeats, the bundle writes NQ-PB-5 adds and the chains they need. Refused (7, in `fixtures/invalid/`, each against its `<call>.request` and refused for one reason): an outbox row with a plan at the commit (arch 01 lines 39 to 41), a state write to `reservations` (only confirm, release and reserve change them), a fourth try (arch 11 line 576), a document's saved time over 24 hours (A4-21), the AI door asking for more than its 15 minutes (MC C3), a turnless firing that writes an outbox row (K-04 `outbox_rows.turn_id` is not null), a row held with no time (arch 06 line 313).

**v1.1** (listed with `"version": "v1.1"`; every v1 fixture also passes v1.1). Accepted (38): the effect chain on Alex's calendar write, `commit-writes-effect` (commit-first's turn in its v1.1 form, run instead of it), `claim-effects`, `effect-result`, and instead of it `effect-result-network-error`, `claim-effects-lapsed`, `effect-try-2`, `effect-try-3`, `effect-try-4`, `effect-fifth-try-outage`, then `service-back` or instead `commit-lets-go-outage-effect`; `provider-event-recorded`, `provider-event-duplicate`; `reconciliation-refund`; the claim chain on Alex's confirmation after v1's `claim-in-order`, `renew-claim-first-send`, `mark-failed-retry-after`, `claim-lapsed-reclaimed`, then `renew-claim-not-yet`, `renew-claim-lost`, `dispatcher-gives-up-too-soon`, `dispatcher-gives-up` (the next way, an email row), then `write-all-set` (texts work again), and `write-all-set-not-failed` after `claim-lapsed-reclaimed`; the waiting chain on Dee W.'s booking notice (K-07's `outbox-waiting-chat-*` rows), `commit-adds-dee-booking`, `write-plan-dee`, `claim-dee`, `mark-waiting-chat`, then `mark-row-wrong-state`, `commit-supersedes-waiting` or instead `commit-withdraws-waiting`, and after `commit-supersedes-waiting` the answer that never goes, `write-plan-dee-answer`, `claim-dee-answer`, `renew-claim-dee-answer`, `dispatcher-gives-up-owner-number` (Dee gave no email, so Sam gets her number); the safety chain on Alex's emergency line (K-07's `outbox-safety-retry`), `commit-alex-safety`, `write-plan-alex-safety`, `claim-alex-safety`, `mark-safety-failed-other-way`. Each effect result (`effect-result*`, `effect-try-2` to `4`, `effect-fifth-try-outage`) carries its `effect.result` entry. 02-contracts K-05's three v1.1 names are `claim-effects`, `effect-result` and `provider-event-duplicate`; the rest are added so every v1.1 call, outcome and K-07 move appears. Refused (10, in `fixtures/invalid/`, each for one reason, each accepted once that one fault is fixed: checked by a throwaway probe, 2026-11-07, with 26 more wrong cases refused, and for round 2 with 13 more around the follow-ups, the "all set" and the result's entries): a waiting row with a held time, the reconciliation writing a charge, a calendar effect with no visit, an ok try with an error code, a Retry-After on a failure with no next try, effects of an SMS port (DL-012), a Linq event as a provider event, an effect result with no event-log entry, a safety line's other-way row with no entry, an "all set" by email. Dee W. is the master's sample cast (S12-26), as K-07 names her; her mobile is +15555550107 and her rows are written by the chain, not seeded.

Refused (8, in `fixtures/invalid/`, each for one reason: put right, each is its accepted twin's): a catalog read naming a business (`refuse-kind-catalog-names-business`, by `readKindCatalog.exchange`; twin `read-kind-catalog-sample`, its request), the catalog answered with the drafted kind in its id place (`refuse-kind-catalog-drafted-kind`, by `readKindCatalog.response`; twin `read-kind-catalog-sample`, its outcome), the barber's row answered with its facts (`refuse-kind-catalog-with-facts`, by `readKindCatalog.response`; twin `read-kind-catalog-sample`, its outcome), a picks read with no person (`refuse-picked-kinds-no-person`, by `readPickedKinds.exchange`; twin `read-picked-kinds-sign-up`, its request), a picks read leaving its business out (`refuse-picked-kinds-business-left-out`, by `readPickedKinds.exchange`; twin `read-picked-kinds-sign-up`, its request), block 4 of the pack hikes draft answered with Sam's words (`refuse-picked-kinds-draft-block-answer`, by `readPickedKinds.response`; twin `read-picked-kinds-pack-hikes-draft`, its outcome), the barbershop's pf.rules block named by its key alone (`refuse-picked-kinds-text-by-key-alone`, by `readPickedKinds.response`; twin `read-picked-kinds-sign-up`, its outcome) and Sam's first pick answered with his mobile (`refuse-picked-kinds-owner-mobile`, by `readPickedKinds.response`; twin `read-picked-kinds-sign-up`, its outcome). The drafted kind, the business named and the mobile are the card's own rules ("A drafted kind ... is never answered"; "Rule its scope"; "no person's address or number"); the rest show the column lists and the request's two fields. Sam's number is K-04's seed's, +15555550100, in the 555-01xx range; the times are the seeds' (Sam's S1-02 at 2:05pm Mon Oct 19 in America/New_York, his NEW on Mon Oct 26), and the reads' own times are examples.

**v1.20** (listed with `"version": "v1.20"` under `rowReader.ownerExchange`; every v1 to v1.19 fixture also passes v1.20, v1.5's three accepted `rowReader` fixtures under v1.20's `rowReader.exchange`). `rowReader` is item 44's and item 80's mapping, not a database call, so no database test runs these exchanges; each `given` says what the row's read and the owner read assumed. Refused (4, in `fixtures/invalid/`, each for one reason: put right, each is its accepted twin): Sam read as the owner by the sign-up line after the hand-over (`refuse-row-reader-sign-up-line-at-owned-business`, by `rowReader.exchange`'s rule 7; twin `row-reader-s1-02-sam-after-hand-over`, its outcome), turn 1's row read by the line with the owner state left out (`refuse-row-reader-owner-state-left-out`, by `rowReader.ownerExchange`'s own rule; twin `row-reader-s1-02-no-role-no-owner`, its request) or stated null (`refuse-row-reader-owner-state-null`, by `rowReader.request`; twin `row-reader-s1-02-no-role-no-owner`, its request), and turn 1's row refused as `businessHasOwner` at a business with no owner (`refuse-row-reader-has-owner-at-unowned-business`, by `rowReader.exchange`'s rule 3; twin `row-reader-s1-02-no-role-no-owner`, its outcome). `refuse-row-reader-owner-state-left-out` is v1.5's `row-reader-s1-02-no-role` in all but its note: v1.20's `rowReader.exchange` accepts it, as it must, and only the narrowed definition refuses it. Hugo P. and the handover time are S11-100's own sample; `person-hugo` and `role-hugo-barber` are v1.11's examples, and `handle-hugo` with the number +15555550199 is an example in the 555-01xx range.

**v1.21** (listed with `"version": "v1.21"` under `commitTurn.askAgainExchange`; every v1 to v1.20 fixture also passes v1.21, each earlier commit exchange under v1.21's `commitTurn.exchange` or `commitTurn.handleIdExchange`). Each is a commit whose request asks an open question again, as K-09 v1.12's asked part commits it: one raise beside one outbox row that asks the question, the two entries `open_question.asked_again` and `outbox_row.created`, no question opened or closed, no clock row, and `answerPhoneRowsBy` set, as every turn's commit sets it (T-1419), naming no phones row. Listed under their own definition, they are met by none of the loops that select `commitTurn.exchange` or `commitTurn.handleIdExchange` (section "v1.21", "The tests on this branch"). Accepted (3): `commit-s1-02-sam-asks-again` (K-09 v1.12's `commit-s1-02-asked.json` as K-05 commits it, its turn, text and row ids K-09's: after v1.3's `commit-s1-01-sam`, Sam's "A bit of everything" asks S1-02's question, `oq-s1-02-sam-1`, again: `outbox-s1-02-sam-asked-1` to `handle-sam` and `person-sam` under `biz-lees-barbershop`, on `imessage`, `quick_reply`, the question named, no versions read; the raise at version 1, the question still `waiting` since no K-05 exchange of the chain runs its first delivery receipt's `armClock`, which K-09's chain counts as version 2, the raise being the same at either; `written` names the question at version 2 and the row at commitSeq 2, after `outbox-s1-02-sam-1`'s 1; in the table its `ask_count` is 2 and every other column and its clock row as `commit-s1-01-sam` wrote them), `commit-s1-02-sam-asks-again-stale` (the card's stale version: after that commit, a turn that read the question at version 1 asks it again at version 1; limit 4 refuses it, `changed` naming `oq-s1-02-sam-1`, read 1, now 2, and nothing is written, so no second raise slips past K-09 v1.12's cap) and `commit-a7-03-alex-asks-again-defaulted` (the card's closed question: after v1's `fire-once`, which defaulted A7-03's question, `oq-a7-03-alex`, at version 3, a turn of Alex's asks it again at that version; the version holds, and the raise is refused `wrongState`, `defaulted`, nothing written; A7-03's options and booking would keep K-09 v1.12 from asking it, and K-05 holds no such rule: default 197). Refused (2, in `fixtures/invalid/`, by `commitTurn.askAgainExchange`, each for one reason: put right, it is `commit-s1-02-sam-asks-again`): `refuse-commit-s1-02-asks-again-twice` (the same raise listed twice: one raise a commit; its one fault `request.writes.openQuestions.askAgain.1`) and `refuse-commit-s1-02-asks-again-and-closes` (the question it asks again also closed, answered, in the same commit: a commit that raises closes none; its one fault `request.writes.openQuestions.close.0`). The stale and the closed twins are accepted exchanges whose outcome is the refusal, as v1's `commit-version-changed` is, since the database's state, not the request, refuses them; the two request faults are the schema's (default 194). The two refused fixtures differ from `commit-s1-02-sam-asks-again` in their fault and their note alone (checked 2026-11-20); the stale twin has a turn, text and row of its own, and the closed twin also a question of its own, A7-03's, since no K-05 exchange closes S1-02's.

Setup goes on, saved, and nothing goes live." (MASTER-FLOW-A S1-09); the entries `open_question.opened` and two `outbox_row.created`; `answerPhoneRowsBy` set, as every turn's commit sets it (T-1419), naming no phones row; `written` names the question and its clock row at version 1 and the rows at commitSeq 2 and 3, after `outbox-s1-02-sam-1`'s 1; S1-04's own part of the turn, its question's close and the company rows it writes, is K-09's, and no K-04 seed holds an S1-04 question or a pick, so the exchange shows K-05's part alone and `oq-s1-02-sam-1` stays as `commit-s1-01-sam` wrote it), `read-outbox-row-s1-08-sam` (after it, S1-08's row read under the barbershop: `line` `{"step": "S1-08", "index": 0, "from": "row"}` where v1.3 to v1.21 answered null; `slotSource.question` null, so finalize's question is null for it; Sam with his owner role and his phone's seeded flags; the text it answers, `evt-sam-0004`, with no safety row in the chain; `businessSensitive` null, since the barbershop holds no kind of work in K-05's world; and no other question still open with Sam that has gone, since no row of S1-02's or S1-09's question went) and `read-outbox-row-s1-02-older-row` (in the same world, turn 1's `outbox-s1-02-sam-1`, written with neither column: every field as v1.6's `read-outbox-row-s1-02-no-kinds` answers it but the moment, `line` from `openQuestion`, S1-02, index 0). Names and values come from fixture set v1's demo shops (barber, yoga studio, movers and, from v1.6, therapist; from v1.18, the plumber's and electrician's as Lee Home Services; from v1.19, the pet boarding shop with its drafted pack hikes, K-24's `seed-new-work-draft`: `fixtures/v1/test-kit/shops/`) and the master's sample cast (Sam, Lee's Barbershop, Alex M., Riley, Avery, Maya, Tomás; MASTER-FLOW-A A7-03), the same rows K-04's seeds hold, never a real person (rule 9). Phone numbers are in the 555-01xx range; the email domain is example.com. Agent tokens, Linq ids, trace ids, event ids and job ids are examples. The bundle id is K-01's `bundle-v0-skeleton.json`, and its manifest value is K-04's seed stand-in, whose bytes and sha256 match.

## Producers and consumers (02-contracts K-05)

- **Producer:** database engineer: C-152 data-access calls, with C-135 double-booking guard.
- **Consumers** (each at the newest frozen minor version): action-layer engineer: C-087 one commit per turn, C-075 frontier builder, C-100 reservations, C-134 saved-time rules, C-109 effects runner and C-112 reconciliation job (at least v1.1); runtime engineer: C-050 bundle pin, C-054 clock rows, alarm and sweep, C-055 clock arming, C-124 outbox dispatcher, C-125 limit 8, C-126 channel fallback, C-127 two-part send, C-180 crash in the middle of a turn; release engineer: C-020 deploy script (`publishBundleCopy`, `setActiveBundle`: BP 5.4; NQ-PB-5); frontend engineer: C-203 fake clock "Fire now" (through K-21 and the Tool API).
- **v1.1's consumers** (each moves to v1.1 in its own task that needs it; v1 consumers keep working): action-layer engineer: C-087 (effects and waiting rows in the commit), C-109 effects runner (`claimEffects`, `recordEffectResult`, `serviceBack`), C-112 reconciliation job (`reconciliationRefund`); runtime engineer: C-044 gateway (`recordProviderEvent`), C-054 sweep (a firing's effects), C-124 outbox dispatcher (the claim, `renewClaim`, `markPart`, `dispatcherGivesUp`), C-125 limit 8 (`markRow` `waitingChat`), C-126 channel fallback (`dispatcherGivesUp` `followUps`: the next way and the owner's number; `markPart` failed `followUps`: a safety line's other ways; `writeAllSet`: the "all set"), C-174 to C-176 outage paths (`serviceBack`). **Producer:** database engineer, C-152, with C-151 migrating K-07's schema addition.
- **v1.2's consumers** (each moves to v1.2 in its own task that needs it; v1 and v1.1 consumers keep working): database engineer: C-152, T-219 (`readTurnState`), T-220 (`handleTargets`, `sendState`, `handleSealingKey`), T-667 (the setup-part facts `readTurnState` reads, over K-22 to K-35, in M4), and the tasks that build `claimOutbox`'s port, `markPart` by key, `renewClaim`'s phone read and the claim entries; action-layer engineer: C-074 Tool API turn face and C-075 frontier (`readTurnState`: T-315), C-109 effects runner (the claim entries); runtime engineer: C-044 gateway (`handleSealingKey`: T-553), C-051 guard (`handleTargets`: T-556), C-046 actor and C-050 bundle pin with the frontier from the Tool API (T-557), C-124 outbox dispatcher (the port, `renewClaim`'s `recipient`, `markPart` delivered by key: T-562) and C-125 limit 8 (`sendState`, `renewClaim`'s `recipient`: T-562); QA: T-128 (the fixture contract test `data-access.v1.2`) and T-478 (its database side). **Producer:** database engineer, C-152 (T-219, T-220); no migration is needed. What three consumers must hold to (the security reviewer's notes on round 1): **T-562** treats a phone row's renewal with no `recipient` (optional in the schema, so a v1.1 answer still passes) as unreadable and sends nothing, never as not stopped; **T-553 and the actor** (C-046, which opens the sealed texts at its turn: `runtime/src/actor/inbox.ts`) take the data key as the non-exportable key object item 29 hands over, never as bytes (T-243's key-store port gives 32 raw bytes today); **T-219 and T-478** test the `businessId` null path of `readTurnState`: a read with no business returns no row that has one, a booking, a role, a pause, an open question or a consent row of a business included, since that path runs on the runtime side, where no row-level security leaves them out. And two from the security reviewer's notes on round 2: **T-553** makes `newHandleId` and `newPersonId` new on every call, never built from the address (the fixtures' `handle-gw-*` and `person-gw-*` ids are examples), since an id built from the address would meet 23505 on every retry once a number's keys were erased a second time, a 503 for good again; **T-478** seeds a private booking, a deceased booking and a paused row, which no v1.2 fixture sets, so `sendState`'s record flags are tested (its pauses are the recipient's only: default 42).
- **v1.3's consumers** (T-1137): the action-layer engineer's C-087 one commit per turn, and C-074 the Tool API turn face, which binds the three reads in turn order (T-1141); built by the database engineer's C-152 (T-1138), with no migration. Section "v1.3", "Consumers of v1.3", lists every consumer port with what each must hold to; that list joins this section with QA's v1.3 contract test task, which adds its sides to the contract test's side list.
- **v1.4's consumers** (T-1160): the action-layer engineer's C-087 one commit per turn, whose `runCore` (T-1140) hands the seven reads to the safety service as its reader, bound in C-074 the Tool API turn face by T-1141; built by the database engineer's C-152 (T-1161), with no migration. Section "v1.4", "Consumers of v1.4", lists every consumer port with what each must hold to; that list joins this section with QA's v1.4 contract test task, which adds its sides to the contract test's side list.
- **v1.5's consumers** (T-1184): the action-layer engineer's C-074 the Tool API turn face, whose compose-input and finalize row ports map the reader by item 44 (T-1141's round 2); no database call is added, so the database engineer's C-152 reads as v1.3 says (T-1138), with no migration. Section "v1.5", "Consumers of v1.5", lists every consumer port with what each must hold to; that list joins this section with QA's v1.5 contract test task, which adds its side to the contract test's side list.

## Defaults used (T-014)

1. **Q-12:** one extension of 10 more minutes, offered before the end; a plan check up to 1 hour, a document up to 24 hours; never for the AI door, whose 15 minutes are fixed (MC C3). **MC C3** also gives the owner's OK up to 30 minutes (A5-08, B5-06): the `ownerOk` door.
2. **Q-15:** a quick reply is a reply in the same turn (the `quick_reply` send class, K-04).
3. **Q-21:** local PostgreSQL with PostGIS meanwhile; the calls need nothing beyond K-04's `btree_gist`.
4. **CQ-05:** a saved-time clock row is due in the business's time zone (no visit zone is in core).
5. **NQ-PB-5:** item 7 carries `publishBundleCopy` and `setActiveBundle`; the second is called only by the deploy script's step 3.
6. **NQ-RT-3:** the turn row is written once, as committed or parked. **NQ-RT-4:** a clock's firing is a turn whose only input is the clock row (`fireClock`).
7. **Scope:** the commit's writes run per write under their own business; reserve, release and extend under the reservation's business; the rest on the runtime side (K-04 rule 4's proposal, `rosie.business_id`).
8. **An open question is `waiting` until its first delivery and `open` after** (`armClock` moves it); K-04 names both statuses, and no source says when one becomes the other.
9. **A clock turn's one event id is the clock row's id**, since K-04 needs at least one (`turns_has_events`) and the firing has no text.
10. **The promoted next in line holds for 10 minutes**, the standard saved time (S12-01), since K-04 keeps no door on a waiting row.

## Defaults used (T-071, v1.1)

11. **`renewClaim` is the "part attempt"** that renews an outbox claim by 60 s (K-07 default 2), sets `first_try_at` once and clears `next_try_not_before` (K-07 names the moments, not the call); a lapsed claim nobody has taken again is still its holder's.
12. **An effect claim lapses 60 s after it is taken, and after a network error 60 s after the runner's planned next try** (`nextTryAt`), so a wait between tries longer than 60 s (the tries spread over 10 minutes: arch 10 line 542) does not hand the row to another runner mid-wait. K-07 default 2 gives 60 s; no source gives the rest.
13. **The call decides the outage** on the run's 5th try (K-07 default 3: "5 tries in all"), so the runner only reports each try.
14. **`markPart` raises the row's version whenever it changes a part**, the row's state changing or not (the database engineer's note on K-07).
15. **The commit finds the waiting rows it ends** (every one to the mobile, or about the booking or question) and writes one `outbox_row.state_changed` entry per row with the id `<row id>/superseded` or `<row id>/withdrawn` (K-08 v1's kind; K-07 defaults 9 and 10).
16. **A booking let go or cancelled while its effect waits in outage lets the effect go** (`letGo`): K-07 names the move for a time let go with no yes (arch 11 line 582; S12-17); `commit-lets-go-outage-effect` uses it for a cancellation, so no calendar event is written for a visit that will not happen.
17. **`recordProviderEvent` runs unscoped on the runtime side** (the database engineer's note on K-07; arch 02 line 109: the gateway stores it before it knows more).
