Then Lee Home Services: plumbing, then electrical"); a kind of work in words, read back to its owner. No column of item 79 holds an address or a number.
178. **The companies are the business's and the person's with no business, never their kinds** (item 79): S1-04 places a pick into a company already made rather than a second of the same name, and S1-08 names the companies; `company_kinds` is v1.18 item 72's once the business exists, and a sign-up's row is named by its pick (`<company id>/<kind id>`), which `readTurnState` reads.
179. **Orders** (item 79): picks by `(picked_at, position, id)`, the order named across S1-02's answers (K-24 `picked_kinds_owner_idx`); texts by `pf_key`; blocks by `block`; companies by `id`; ids byte by byte, as every K-05 read compares them.
180. **A pick's texts, draft and blocks are answered with it, each joined to its pick under the pick's rule** (item 79; the security reviewer's and the database engineer's notes on round 2, DL-958): every such row of an answered pick that the pick's own rule reaches, each statement joining a text or a draft to its pick, and a block to its draft and that draft's pick, the rule on the pick and the same `business_id` condition on each row joined to it, never a child's `business_id is null` alone. The three tables hold no `owner_person_id`, and their composite foreign keys hold each one's business to its pick's or none, but MATCH SIMPLE lets a child whose `business_id` is null stand on its one-column key under any pick (0021 lines 30 to 35), so only the join binds it to a person. So no nested row can be another business's or another person's. A business's pick whose text, draft or block still has no business (S1-04 sets the business on a pick's texts, draft and blocks in the commit that places it: 0021's header) is answered without that row: the business's scope hides it, and the person's statements take only picks with no business, so it is answered nowhere and the read fails closed. The reverse, a child with a business under a pick with none, the composite key refuses.
181. **Each answer names its call and the database's now** (`result`, `readAt`), and item 79's its request's business and person (`businessId`, `personId`), as v1.18 default 165.
182. **S1-11's blocks come from the pinned bundle, and no K-24 change is asked for** (section "What journey 1's first calls read"): K-24 stores a trade's defaults (`kinds`, `kind_flows`) and a business's copied blocks (`picked_kind_texts`), never a trade's own blocks (its README "Who owns what"; rule 7), which K-01's trade overlay holds whole with MC C5 applied; so the card's "if a needed column does not exist" does not fire.

## Defaults used (T-1464, no new version: the claim's walk)

183. **A parked turn's "nothing changed" row is passed by every claim but the safety claim, never a stop** (items 3 and 13; T-1464, DL-967). `parkTurn` writes that row `planned` with no plan (item 12) and nothing composes it (`runtime/src/turn/park.ts`: "the runtime never composes or words that line"), so a walk that stopped at it for commit order held every later row to that person for good (staging, Nov 18: a checked reply never claimed, and each new text parked again behind it). So a row that is `planned`, holds no plan and whose turn is `parked` (K-04 `turns.state`, read by the row's `turn_id` in the walk's own statement) is passed and not listed in `passedHeld`, as a `waiting_chat` row is (item 13), and is never claimed, sent or counted toward `limit`, by v1's claim, v1.1's and v1.2's, v1.13's and v1.14's without `sendClass`; the sweep looks past it too. Every other `planned` row (a committed turn's row not yet composed whose turn committed inside default 225's window, a row with no turn) and every `stale` row still stops the walk, a parked turn's row that holds a plan is walked as before, and the safety claim (item 67) is unchanged (it reads only safety rows; the parked row is a quick reply). No schema change, field or answer changes (`db/repo/outbox.ts` default 26). From v1.23 (item 84, default 221) a park's row in v1.23's form holds its plan and final parts and is `checked`, so this default does not touch it; it still passes a row a park in v1's form wrote.

## Defaults used (T-1536, no new version: the claim's walk past a given-up reply)

225. **A committed turn's row left with no plan once its reply has been given up is passed by every claim but the safety claim, never a stop** (items 3 and 13; default 183; T-1536, DL-1164, DL-1165; numbered after v1.23's defaults 216 to 224, so no two defaults share a number). A turn's reply is composed after its commit, under the turn's lease: a lapse reruns the compose, and after the third (`MAX_TRIES` × `LEASE_MS`, 3 × 1 minute: `runtime/src/actor/lease.ts` lines 97 to 99) the lease gives the reply up (`lapseOf`'s `giveUp`, lines 1221 to 1233). Nothing ever returns to a row it left with no plan: no sweep composes it, and `unplannedRows` (item 2) is read per turn. So a walk that stopped at it for commit order held every later row to that person for good (the user's phone, Nov 22: a JOIN turn's S1-02 row was refused three times by the composer's own check before T-1535, and a later turn's checked reply waited behind it). No K-07 move or K-05 call may clear such a row by hand, and a committed turn is never parked (DL-1164), so the claim passes it. **The rule.** A row that is `planned`, holds no plan and whose turn is `committed` with `committed_at` at or before the claim's `now` less the reply give-up window (K-04 `turns.state` and `turns.committed_at`, read by the row's `turn_id` in the walk's own statement, never locked) is passed as default 183's row is: by the walk of v1's claim, v1.1's and v1.2's, v1.13's and v1.14's without `sendClass`, and by the sweep, which looks past it, while it still counts as open in the sweep's earliest-open order (default 4). It is not listed in `passedHeld`, and it is never claimed, sent or counted toward `limit`. `now` is the claim's own (the request's `now`), so the bound moves with each claim. Nothing is written to the row: it stays `planned` at its version, with no plan, final parts, part or claim. **The window** is 10 minutes, more than three times the 3 minutes a live reply can hold its row, so a reply still being composed is not passed; standing until Jay answers (a question for QUESTIONS.md). A committed turn's row inside the window still stops the walk (default 183), as a row with no turn and every `stale` row do, and default 183's parked turn's row is passed as before. **A row that gets a plan later** is walked as before: `writePlan` moves it to `checked` (item 2), and the walk claims it in commit order. **The safety claim** (item 67) is unchanged: it reads only the recipient's safety rows and never this rule. No version, schema, field, answer or error changes (`db/repo/outbox.ts` default 30). **The known risk.** While PostgreSQL is down a held turn counts no tries (T-270), so a reply can outlast the window and its row be composed after it; by then the claim may have passed the row and sent a later row to the same person, so the composed row goes after it: out of order, never lost (once composed it is `checked` and claimed) and never sent twice (every other rule of the claim is unchanged).

## Defaults used (T-1187, v1.20)

The card and its lead note (DL-999) name the rule, the field's source and the two forms; these are the points they leave to the contract, each ruled here and named for the reviews.

184. **Two states, and more than one live owner is `live`** (item 80): the mapping reads only whether the business has an owner. `readBusinessOwner` answers `moreThanOneOwner` for two or more live owner roles (default 58), a record the sources do not allow (open gap 41); the business still has an owner, so a person with no role is refused there, and a recipient who holds a role there is read by it as before. Mapping that answer to a refused row would stop every row to that business's own people, which no source asks of the reader mapping.
185. **`owner` null is `none`, as the read answers it** (item 80; the card: "as v1.4's readBusinessOwner answers it"): `readBusinessOwner` answers null when the business has no live owner role and also when its owner writes from no handle (item 42, default 59), and the two cannot be told apart from its answer. So an owner with no handle reads as `none`, and the sign-up reading is still made there: the open side, kept because the card names this read and v1.20 adds no database call (open gap 94).
186. **A row with no business is `none`, with no read** (item 80): `readBusinessOwner` takes a business only (`refuse-business-owner-no-business`), no business has no owner, and nothing of a business is on such a row. The turn face answers `notBuilt` for a row with no business today (`rows.ts` default 7).
187. **The refusals in order: `noRole`, then `businessHasOwner`, then `lineHasNoReader`** (item 80). A row with no person or a step outside section 1 is refused as v1.5 refuses it, so every request v1.5 refused keeps its reason whether or not the state is stated; where the sign-up reading would otherwise be looked at, the business's owner is checked before the line, so `businessHasOwner` is answered whatever lane the line goes to, and a line that goes to no reader is not reported in its place. Each fails closed alike, so the order changes the label and nothing else.
188. **A state that was not read is never `none`** (item 80; the S1-04 design ruling's card 28: "A failed owner read refuses the row; it is never read as 'no owner'"): `notFound`, `transient` and a thrown read give no state, so no request is built and the row is refused before a reader is looked for; the schema refuses a null state and, in `rowReader.ownerExchange`, a missing one wherever the outcome reads it. `readBusinessOwner`'s own rule is the same: "The call fails closed on each: the wiring maps them to a thrown error, never to null".
189. **Read at the row's read, each time, and only where the outcome reads it** (item 80): by compose input and by finalize each, on the primary, never from the turn's party read, a cache or the caller, since the owner state can change between a turn's start, a row's compose and its finalize ("Why v1.11's party rule is not enough", reason 3). Not read for a recipient who holds a role, a row with no person or a step outside section 1, whose outcomes do not depend on it, so those rows cost no read and cannot be refused by a failed one.
190. **The request names no owner** (item 80): `businessOwner` is `none` or `live`, never the owner's person, role or handle, which the mapping does not need. Named for what it says of the row's business; `none` and `live` rather than a boolean, so a missing, null or false-by-default value is never a state (default 188).
191. **No company is read** (item 80; the card names the owner state alone): a person with no role is read by a sign-up line at a business with no live owner whether or not a company of theirs names that business, as item 44 reads a third party a sign-up step writes to (S1-77's owner, whom a helper names: T-1184's security note d). Who may run a turn under a business is item 52's (default 99); item 80 adds the owner condition at the row's read and no other.

## Defaults used (T-1508, v1.21)

The card and its Lead note 1 (DL-1030, DL-1033) name the raise, its refusals, its fixtures and the repeated lines' writers; these are the points they leave to the contract, each ruled here and named for the reviews.

192. **The raise names the version read, never the count** (item 81): `openQuestionAskAgain` is the question's `id` and `expectVersion`, and the write is `ask_count = ask_count + 1` at that version. `ask_count` changes only by an update, which 0009's version trigger makes raise the version, so the version the turn read pins the count it read, and the compare makes the count written exactly one more: no lost update, and two turns that read one count never both raise it (the second answers `versionChanged`: `commit-s1-02-sam-asks-again-stale`). A count in the request would be a second spelling of what the version already fixes, which a stale caller could get wrong.
193. **Live is `waiting` or `open`, and a raise of a closed question is `wrongState`, after `versionChanged`** (item 81; the card: "refused `wrongState` unless the question is live (waiting or open)"). The order is item 1's: `reservationLost`, then `versionChanged`, then `wrongState`, an effect let go's (v1.1 item 18) before the raise's, one row named. So a question closed after the turn read it, whose version the close raised, answers `versionChanged` and the turn reloads (limit 4); `wrongState` answers only a raise of a question the turn read closed. Its `state` is the status now, never a live one (`openQuestionWrongStateError`), as v1.1's names an outbox row's or an effect's state now.
194. **One raise a commit, and none beside a close, both refused by the schema** (item 81; the card: "At most one raise per question per commit; a question raised and closed in the same commit is refused (say which refusal)"; DL-1030: "a commit may raise one live open question's ask count by one"). JSON Schema cannot compare a raise's id with a close's, so v1.21 bounds the request instead: `askAgain` holds one raise, and with it `close` is empty. Both bounds are what K-09 v1.12, the one writer, needs and no more: its commit writes at most one row a turn, for the one open question, and opens and closes nothing (its default 192). The refusal is the schema's, so db/repo throws before any statement reaches the database (its default 1), nothing written and nothing answered: never `versionChanged` (the versions match) or `wrongState` (the question is live), which name the record's state and send the caller to reload, while a request that raises a question twice, or raises and closes it, contradicts itself and no reload cures it. A later minor version may lift either bound by adding, when a card needs two raises or a raise beside another question's close.
195. **The raise moves `ask_count` and `version` and nothing else; the send time moves with the re-sent row's send** (item 81; the card's Gives 2; 0037 lines 55 to 62; v1.2 default 41; K-09 v1.12 defaults 193 (2), 196 and 197): section "v1.21", "What a raise moves, and what it never moves". The send time is no column, so the raise cannot move it and does not; read from the rows that ask the question, it becomes the re-sent row's once a part of that row has gone, and stays the first row's until then, or for good if that row never goes. Status, options, read-back, versions read, Clock line, clock row and due time never move.
196. **One `open_question.asked_again` entry per raise, from the caller's events** (item 81; the card's Gives 3, ruled yes): section "v1.21", "The event-log entry". K-08 v1's closed list holds the kind, so v1.21 needs no K-08 version and raises no NEED.
197. **K-05 holds no cap and no cover** (item 81): it raises any live question whose version matches, whatever its count, step, options, read-back or booking. The cap (no row at `ask_count` 2 or more) and which questions are asked again (no options as sent, no read-back, no booking) are K-09 v1.12's (its defaults 193 and 194), the rules of the one caller that knows why it asks; the database keeps `ask_count` at least 1 and nothing more (0009 `open_questions_ask_count`). The read and the compare make the cap exact for that caller (default 192); a cap in the database is open gap 97.
198. **`written` keeps the order of the writes, the raise after the opens and closes** (item 81; db/repo `commit-turn.ts` default 2): state writes, open questions opened, then closed, then raised, then the rest as default 2 orders them. With no open or close beside a raise (default 194), a raising commit names the question first, after any state write.
199. **A firing raises nothing, and db/repo's `checkFire` refuses a firing that carries `askAgain`; a commit with no `askAgain` keeps v1.20's answer type** (item 81; round 2, after the database engineer's FAIL on round 1: DL-1045). `fireClock.request` keeps v1.1's `writes` (`data-access.v1.1.schema.json`), so a firing's writes carry no `askAgain` and the schema refuses one. A question is asked again in reply to a text (K-09 v1.12 default 192: an asked part of decided texts), never at a clock's firing, which applies the step's safe default (arch 02 line 127). The two calls share no writer: `fireClock` checks its request in its own `checkFire` (`db/repo/clocks.ts` lines 866 to 958, run first at line 2121) and writes its questions in its own `writeOpenQuestions` (lines 1446 to 1493), never `commit-turn.ts`'s `checkRequest` or `writeOpenQuestions`, and `checkFire` reads only `open` and `close` of `writes.openQuestions`, refusing no other key, so as built a firing that carried `askAgain` would be written with the raise silently left out. So `checkFire` throws on a firing whose `writes.openQuestions` holds `askAgain`, with any value, before any statement reaches the database, naming the field alone, as `commitTurn`'s `checkRequest` throws on a request the schema refuses (db/repo's default 1): nothing written, nothing answered. T-1509 builds that throw there (its May touch widened to `checkFire` and its tests, cases added only: DL-1045; section "Consumers of v1.21"). **The commit's answer type:** a commit request with no `askAgain` keeps v1.20's answer type, v1.1's `CommitTurnResponse` or `CommitTurnErrors` (v1.20's `commitTurn.errors` refers to v1.1's), by an overload of db/repo's `commitTurn`, so `actions/src/commit/pg/commit-turn.ts`, whose port answers v1.1's `CommitTurnAnswer` (`actions/src/commit/types.ts` lines 15, 27, 28 and 205), is untouched; only a request that carries `askAgain` may answer v1.21's errors, the open question's `wrongState` among them.
200. **`writePlan` writes the opener mark** (Lead note 1's Gives 5; DL-1033 ruling 4; closes open gap 37; K-04 `openers_sent`: "Whether Rosie's opener for a business has gone to a handle", written once). In `writePlan`'s own transaction, after its update, when the row's `business_id` and `turn_id` are set and its final parts' `fixedLines` hold an opener (`opener` or `signUpOpener`: default 204), `writePlan` inserts the mark for the row's recipient handle and business, naming the row, `on conflict do nothing`: the mark is written once (0012 line 96 grants `openers_sent` insert and select only, and it has no version: 0005 lines 43 to 52), so the first row planned to carry an opener is the one named and a row planned later never moves it. `rosie_job` on the runtime side, where `writePlan` runs (item 2), holds the insert under `openers_sent_runtime` (0012 lines 138 to 146). A row with no business needs no mark: the no-business read finds an earlier row by its final parts (item 36). A row with no turn (K-04 v1.1's job rows, such as a sign-in's code) writes none: its commit has no turn time (default 201). Nothing is written on `alreadyPlanned`, `versionChanged`, `notFound` or a refusal. The row the mark names reads `textedAboutBusiness` false and carries the opener whenever it is finalized again (default 50).
201. **The mark's `sent_at` is the turn's `committed_at`** (Gives 5; K-04 core v1.2 `openers_sent.sent_at`, `../db-schema/core/core.v1.2.tables.json` line 692: "When the outbox row carrying the opener was committed"; check-repeat's point 6), read from `turns` by the row's `turn_id` in `writePlan`'s transaction (`outbox_rows` holds no time column: 0010); never `writePlan`'s own now().
202. **No earlier row that never went counts, in either branch: a mark whose row never went is read past, to the earliest earlier row that carries an opener and did not end so, and the no-business branch takes the same filter** (Gives 5's read fallback; check-repeat's corrected ruling 1; DL-1033 ruling 4: "the earliest earlier row that carries the opener and went out"; RULES 1.11, 4.3; round 2, after the security reviewer's FAIL on round 1: DL-1045). A row ended without going when its state is `withdrawn`, `failed`, `dropped_stopped` or `superseded` (K-07's ends of a row that did not go, or will not: `superseded` from `waiting_chat`, `db/repo/outbox.ts` lines 303 and 340). **With a business**, `readFixedLineFacts` (item 36) reads the mark: none, false, as today; naming this row, false, as today; naming another row in one of those four states, the read falls back to the earliest row to the same handle and business with a lower `commit_seq` than this row's whose final parts' `fixedLines` hold an opener (default 204) and whose state is none of those four, the shape of the no-business branch: found, true, and `openerRowId` that row; none, false, and `openerRowId` null; naming another row in any other state, true, as today. **With no business** (`openers_sent` holds no mark: item 36), the read is that same query with no mark before it: the earliest row to the handle with no business with a lower `commit_seq` than this row's whose final parts' `fixedLines` hold an opener (default 204) and whose state is none of those four (`db/repo/turn-face-reads.ts` lines 839 to 852, the state filter added): found, true, and `openerRowId` that row; none, false, and `openerRowId` null. So an owner's first sign-up row that ended failed, withdrawn, dropped_stopped or superseded never counts, and the next row, the first text the owner's thread actually gets, carries the opener and the privacy-notice link (RULES 1.11: "the first text of every thread"; RULES 4.3: "Every page and first text links to a plain privacy notice"; default 204 says why the no-business branch needs the filter from v1.21). **Never a state filter without that fallback:** the mark is written once (default 200), so a read that only ignored a dead row's mark would leave every later row to that handle and business carrying the opener for good, the fault being fixed (check-repeat's point 2). In either branch, a first text that never went leaves the next row to carry the opener, and the row after it reads that next row and does not. A row still held, waiting for its chat, checked or sending counts (open gap 100). The read stays one read-only transaction. After v1.21, a mark naming another row committed after this one (a higher `commit_seq`), in any state, takes this fallback too, in place of true (default 215).
203. **`markPart` writes the opt-out line's delivery time, the phone's version raised, the phone locked last** (Lead note 1's Gives 6; DL-1033 ruling 4; RULES 4.3; arch 07 line 361: "on a number's texts until one is delivered"). In `markPart`'s transaction (item 4, in each of its forms), when a `delivered` receipt moves a part to `delivered` now (a repeat writes nothing: item 4), the part is of kind `words`, and its row's final parts' `fixedLines` hold `optOutLine`, `markPart` sets the recipient handle's phone's `optout_line_delivered_at` to the receipt's time, with `version = version + 1`, only where it is null: 0002's `enforce_version_raise` refuses an update that does not raise the version by one (check-repeat's point 1), and a failed update would fail the receipt (503), keep the part `sent` and never arm the question's clock. The phone is updated last, after the row and its parts are locked, as `renewClaim` reads it last (`db/repo/outbox.ts` default 15), and `commitTurn`, which locks in (table, row id) order, takes `outbox_rows` before `phones` too. A repeat receipt, a delivered poll or link-card part, an email row (no phones row), a row without the line and a second line-carrying delivery (the time already set) write nothing, so the first delivered time stays (`db/repo/phones.ts` `recordOptoutLineDelivered`: "The first time stays"). The raised version means a turn that read the phone before the receipt is refused `versionChanged` and reloads (limit 4): a cost, not a fault. Nothing of STOP changes: `phones.stopped` is untouched and limit 8 reads it as before. Item 46's list of what `openChatOnText` never changes keeps `optout_line_delivered_at`: its writer is `markPart`. An SMS-way chat gets no delivered mark (open gap 98).
204. **"Texted about this business" counts either opener id, only on a row that did not end without going** (Lead note 1's Gives 7; DL-1033: default 50 widened for `signUpOpener`, the sign-up opener T-1510 and T-1511 add; round 2, after the security reviewer's FAIL on round 1: DL-1045): an earlier row counts when its final parts' `fixedLines` hold `opener` or `signUpOpener` and its state is none of `withdrawn`, `failed`, `dropped_stopped` or `superseded` (default 202), in item 36's no-business branch and in default 202's fallback alike, and default 200 writes the mark for either. In the no-business branch the state filter comes with the widening: an owner's sign-up rows before there is a business carry `signUpOpener` in place of `opener` (K-01 default 19, T-1510: `../bundle/README.md`), which that branch, counting `opener` alone before v1.21, never counted, so the opener repeated there, the safe side; counting `signUpOpener` with no filter would count a first sign-up row that never went out and leave the owner's real first text without the opener and the privacy-notice link (RULES 1.11, 4.3). The filter holds for `opener` in that branch too.
205. **No backfill** (DL-1033 ruling 6): a migration that wrote marks and opt-out times for texts already sent would write consent state in a data migration, which no expand-only migration does; so rows sent before T-1513 deploys keep no mark and no time, and the next text to each handle may carry the three lines one last time, and then not again.

## Defaults used (T-1474, v1.22)

206. **Both or neither, and left out when neither, never null** (item 82; the card's Gives 1, "both or neither"; DL-999 decision (e); K-04 core v1.2 `outbox_rows_step_with_line`). `step` and `lineNo` are optional together (`dependentRequired` each way) and take no null, so a row that names no line has one spelling, the fields left out, as v1.21's `askAgain` has (its default 194), and every v1 to v1.21 row is a v1.22 row. One without the other is refused by the schema and thrown by db/repo before any statement (db/repo's default 1), never written half: a step with no line would be read as no line or, worse, a line guessed (`refuse-commit-s1-04-sam-step-without-line`).
207. **`lineNo` counts as `rowLine.index` counts** (item 82; v1.3's `rowLine`; CQ-151 (a)'s default 2; K-04 core v1.2 default 24): the line's index among the step's lines from Rosie to the row's reader, in the step's say order, 0 the first, with no upper bound, so the read hands it back unchanged as `line.index`. S1-08 has one line from Rosie, to the owner (line 0); S1-09's question is its first line from Rosie (line 0) and its read-back the second (line 1, after the answer: MASTER-FLOW-A S1-09).
208. **The row's own pair first, whatever it asks; never compared with its question's step, and the commit refuses no mismatch** (item 83; DL-999 decision (e): "readOutboxRow reads the row's own pair first"; the card's Gives 3; K-04 core v1.2 gap 2, put to this version). A row that stores a step answers `from` `row` even when it asks a question, and the question's step is then not read for the line: the row's stored pair says which line it carries, which a question's step only stood in for (default 48). `commitTurn` does not compare a row's step with the step of the question it asks: for a question opened in an earlier turn that is a read the commit does not make (item 1 reads only the rows it compares), and the database cannot (a CHECK reads one row: K-04 core v1.2 default 25). The walk names a row that asks a question by that question's step (T-1479), so the two agree as written; a mismatch is open gap 102.
209. **No check against the bundle** (item 82; K-04 core v1.2 gap 3, put to this version, and its proposed default): the commit does not check the step against the turn's bundle or the line against that step's lines. The walk writes only steps it walked in the turn's bundle (T-1479), and compose input throws on a master line the bundle does not hold (CQ-151 (b)'s default), so a wrong pair leaves the row with no plan, failing closed, never sending another step's words.
210. **An email copy names neither, and a row with no turn is never read for one** (K-04 core v1.2 gap 5, put to this version, and its proposed default). An email copy (`copyOfRowId` set) reuses its text row's plan and is never composed (arch 07 line 371; item 35's table, `ComposeRow.copyOfRowId`), so the walk writes it no step or line; the schema does not refuse one that does, since no source makes it wrong and K-04 takes it, and the read answers it as stored, which no consumer composes. A row with no turn (K-04 v1.1's job row, a sign-in's code row) is written by `sendSignInCode`, whose request names neither, and `readOutboxRow` answers it `notFound` before anything of it is read (item 65), so its columns are never selected.
211. **A firing's rows carry neither** (item 82): `fireClock.request` keeps v1.1's `writes`, as v1.21's default 199 keeps it for `askAgain`, since the card names `commitTurn`'s `outboxRowNew` alone and no clock firing that writes a row asking no question is built (the clock firing, C-054, is bound by none of the composition root's ports in `runtime/src/wiring/ports.ts`; open gap 80). A firing's row reads its line from its question, else null, as before. Open gap 101.
212. **A row that stores neither reads as v1.3 reads it; nothing is backfilled** (item 83; K-04 core v1.2 default 27 and gap 4): every row written before db/repo builds item 82, and every row of a caller not yet on v1.22, holds both null and answers its line from its question, else null (`read-outbox-row-s1-02-older-row`; the frozen `read-outbox-row-no-question`), so such a row that asks nothing is still refused at compose input and finalize, never given a guessed line.
213. **Where the fixtures are listed** (the card's Proves; DL-396): the commit fixtures under `commitTurn.rowLineExchange`, a narrowing, as v1.17 and v1.21 listed theirs, because `db/repo/commit-turn.int.test.ts` checks each `commitTurn.exchange` entry against v1.1 too (line 584), which a step can never pass; the reads under `readOutboxRow.exchange` itself, as the card asks ("db/repo/turn-face-reads.int.test.ts pins the read's fixture list"), so that file fails on them until T-1476, which rides one train with this version and T-1475 (section "v1.22", "The tests on this branch").
214. **The reader mapping reads no `from`** (items 44 and 80; the card's Gives 2): `rowReader.request.line` takes v1.22's `rowLine`, and the mapping's rules (v1.20's rules 0 to 7) read `recipient`, `section`, `lineTo` and `businessOwner`, so a line from the row is mapped exactly as a line from a question is, and a section 1 row that asks no question, such as S1-08's, is read by the sign-up line under item 44's and item 80's conditions alone. Whether that reading is right for a row with no question is T-1475's security review (its card), not a rule added here.

## Defaults used (T-1521, no new version: a mark naming a row committed later)

The security reviewer's new task on T-1513 (its PASS on bcc4095: default 202 answers "texted before" whenever the mark names a live row, whatever that row's commit order; DL-1083, DL-1084) asks for one rule default 202 leaves out. No request, answer or error changes shape, so it is this README's text alone, as default 183 (T-1464) is for K-05 and defaults 191 and 199 (T-1499, T-1491) are for K-09: no version, schema, named rule, fixture, index entry or `frozen.json` changes, and no frozen file is edited. It is numbered 215, after K-05 v1.22's defaults 206 to 214 (T-1474), so no two defaults share a number. Readers: the database engineer, who builds it in `readFixedLineFactsIn` (`db/repo/turn-face-reads.ts`, the live-mark branch: the branch with a business, which reads the mark with the state of the row it names and `marked_later`, lines 922 to 952 when T-1521 merged) and in the runtime's finalize double (`runtime/src/wiring/test-support.ts`, which follows db/repo: T-1514), with their tests (T-1521 part 2); and QA. The frozen fixture it contradicts, `fixed-line-facts-texted-before.json`, stays as it is, and the tests that compare with it, and db/repo's case of 100 `writePlan`s at once, expect what the default answers in their worlds (the default's last two parts; T-1521 part 1b, DL-1089).

215. **A mark naming a row committed after this one is read as the fallback, so the earliest row sent carries the opener and the privacy-notice link** (T-1521; the security reviewer's new task on T-1513, DL-1083; open gap 99's second case; RULES 1.11: "the first text of every thread"; RULES 4.3: "Every page and first text links to a plain privacy notice"; arch 07 line 361). **The rule.** With a business, `readFixedLineFacts` (item 36) takes default 202's fallback when the mark names another row whose `commit_seq` is higher than this row's, whatever that row's state, exactly as for a mark naming a row that never went: the earliest row to the same handle and business with a lower `commit_seq` than this row's whose final parts' `fixedLines` hold an opener (`opener` or `signUpOpener`: default 204) and whose state is none of `withdrawn`, `failed`, `dropped_stopped` or `superseded`: found, `textedAboutBusiness` true and `openerRowId` that row; none, false and `openerRowId` null. So a mark is answered true with its own row only when it names another row, committed before this one, that did not end without going. Unchanged: no mark, false and `openerRowId` null; a mark naming this row, false and this row (default 50); a mark naming another row that never went, the fallback (default 202). With no business nothing changes: that branch reads no mark and already counts only rows with a lower `commit_seq`. `commit_seq` is unique to each row (0010 line 51, `outbox_rows_commit_seq_key`), so another row is always committed strictly before or after this one; the marked row's `commit_seq` is read with its state, in the read's one read-only transaction under the business's scope (the marked row is the business's own: default 200). **Why.** Rows go out in commit order (arch 02 line 125: "The dispatcher sends each recipient's rows in commit order"), but they are planned in the order their finalizes end: a turn composes its rows in parallel (arch 07 line 397; `runtime/src/turn/compose.ts` lines 420 to 428), and two handles' turns, each under its own lease, can each write a row to one recipient at once. So two first-text rows to one handle and business can be planned out of commit order: the row committed later reads no mark, carries the opener and takes the mark, which is written once and never moves (default 200); under default 202 alone the row committed earlier, read after it, answered "texted before" and went first, by commit order, with no opener and no privacy-notice link, against RULES 1.11 and 4.3. Before T-1513 no mark was written, so the case is new with defaults 200 and 202. **What each row then reads.** The earlier row finds no earlier opener row and carries the opener and the privacy-notice link. The later row, planned first, already carries them, since it read no mark; read again, it reads the mark naming itself, false (default 50): an opener twice, the safe side (open gap 99's first case), never a first text without it. A row committed after both reads the mark, which names a row committed before it, true, and carries neither. Where the mark is written before either read (the world of `fixed-line-facts-texted-before.json` as K-04's seeds load it, whose mark names `out-alex-first` while `out-a7-03-alex` was committed before it: "The frozen fixture it contradicts", below), the row committed before the marked one reads what the fallback finds: `out-a7-03-alex` reads false and `openerRowId` null in every state of `out-alex-first`, as it reads today while that row is in a state that never went. **What does not change.** Nothing is written: `writePlan`'s mark (defaults 200 and 201) and `markPart`'s opt-out time (default 203) are as before, and the mark still names the row planned first. `optOutLineDeliveredAt` is read as before: a delivery receipt sets it, never a plan, so it follows the order texts arrive in, not the order rows are planned in. The read stays one read-only transaction. **The frozen fixture it contradicts, and how a test compares with it** (T-1521 part 2's NEED on 84783002; DL-1089). K-05 v1.3's `fixed-line-facts-texted-before.json` reads `out-a7-03-alex` and states `textedAboutBusiness` true and `openerRowId` `out-alex-first`. That is what every reader before this default answers, and what this default answers too in the world the fixture's note tells, where Alex's first reply (`turn-alex-first`, committed 2026-10-12T16:20:03Z) went before A7-03's row (`turn-alex-move-1`, committed 2026-10-23T14:05:02Z). But a test builds the fixture's world from K-04's seeds as K-04's README says (each seed's `after` chain loaded first, `commit_seq` always left out: `contracts/db-schema/core/README.md` line 87), and `seed-thread-messages.json`, which holds `out-alex-first` and the mark, has `seed-outbox-row.json`, which holds `out-a7-03-alex`, in its `after` chain. So the loaded world commits `out-a7-03-alex` before `out-alex-first` (a lower `commit_seq`), and from this default on commit order decides, not a turn's commit time: there the mark names a row committed later, and the fallback finds no earlier row to Alex's handle at the barbershop whose final parts hold an opener (`out-a7-03-alex` is the first row of that handle and business the seeds load), so the read answers false and `openerRowId` null. A new version would not settle it: the v1.3 fixture stays listed (a minor version only adds), and every test that selects fixtures by version would still meet it. So: (1) **The fixture stays as it is**, accepted: no schema, fixture, index entry or `frozen.json` changes (false with `openerRowId` null is as valid a v1.3 answer as the one it states, and `npm run contracts:check` checks the file against the schema, never against a database). No test rewrites `commit_seq`, loads the seeds out of K-04's order, adds or changes a row, or edits the fixture to reach the outcome it states. (2) **A test that loads a `readFixedLineFacts` exchange's given and compares the producer's answer with its outcome expects what this default answers in the loaded world.** Where the outcome is a `fixedLineFacts` answer with a business and, in the loaded world, the mark for the row's handle and business names another row whose `commit_seq` is higher than the row read's, the expected answer is the outcome with `textedAboutBusiness` and `openerRowId` as default 202's fallback finds them in that world, and every other field (`result`, `outboxRowId`, `businessId`, `recipientHandleId`, `readAt`, `optOutLineDeliveredAt`) as the fixture states it, at the exchange's own version, with no field added. The test decides this from the loaded world, never from the file's name, and checks it before the read as the tables' owner, whom no policy binds, as v1.6's switch is checked against a world with no kind of work (item 45): the mark names that other row, whose `commit_seq` is higher than the row read's, and for false and null, no row to the handle and business with a lower `commit_seq` than the row read's has final parts whose `fixedLines` hold `opener` or `signUpOpener`, whatever its state. No frozen fixture's world has the fallback find a row, so a test that meets one fails as broken, never guessing. Every other `readFixedLineFacts` exchange is compared with its outcome whole, as before. Of the frozen fixtures this touches `fixed-line-facts-texted-before.json` alone (`fixed-line-facts-s1-02.json` holds no mark, `fixed-line-facts-opener-row.json`'s mark names the row it reads, and `fixed-line-facts-other-business.json` answers `notFound`), whose expected answer is: `result` `fixedLineFacts`, `outboxRowId` `out-a7-03-alex`, `businessId` `biz-lees-barbershop`, `recipientHandleId` `handle-alex`, `readAt` `2026-10-23T14:05:03Z`, `textedAboutBusiness` false, `openerRowId` null, `optOutLineDeliveredAt` `2026-10-12T16:20:00Z`. Two tests compare it: QA's database side (`tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts`, its `answerOf`, beside v1.6's switch and v1.16's recipient), and db/repo's own (`db/repo/turn-face-reads.int.test.ts`: its `answerOf`, for the fixture loop, and the yoga studio's case, whose last read of the fixture's own request expects that same answer, the yoga studio's mark changing nothing of it). (3) **A later version's `readFixedLineFacts` fixture** states its outcome as this default answers in the world its given loads, in the commit order K-04's seed rules give. **Many first-text rows planned at once.** Take rows r0, r1, and so on, to one handle and business, committed in that order with no earlier row of theirs carrying an opener, each planned with an opener in its final parts, in any order, and none ended without going: the mark names the row planned first, M (default 200). Read after every plan, M reads false and itself (default 50); each row committed after M reads true and M; r0, when it is not M, reads false and null; and each row between r0 and M reads true and r0, the earliest row with an opener, which may still go. So r0 and M read false and no other row does (r0 alone when it is M): the first row sent carries the opener and the privacy-notice link, and so does the row planned first, the safe side. `db/repo/outbox-first-lines.int.test.ts`'s case of 100 `writePlan`s at once (its rows added one by one, so their `commit_seq` follows their ids) asserts that whole list, every row's facts from the mark it finds. Its assertion that only the marked row reads false held under default 202 alone; under this default it fails whenever the mark lands on any row but the first.

## Defaults used (T-1531, v1.23)

The card, its lead note and T-1529's ruling (DL-1142, DL-1144) name the reason, the team's record, the line and the crash park's line; these are the points they leave to the contract, each ruled here and named for the reviews.

216. **The park's words are K-01's passedOn line, word for word, held by the schema** (the ruling's ruling 2 and its defaults: "passedOn as the park line until Jay gives words"; arch 02 line 123; S13-29). `parkedLineParts` pins one words send whose text is the line exactly as bundles/v2's manifest and K-01 v1's `fixed-lines.json` hold it, "I’ve passed this on. Rosie’s team will reply within 1 business day.", its two apostrophes the typographic one (U+2019), with `fixedLines` `["passedOn"]`, no slot value, option, link or calendar file, and a plan with no lines. A constant rather than "the pinned bundle's line" because the ruling asks for that line word for word, and a constant keeps model words, slot values and anything of the refused part out of the row by the schema itself, which the security review checks (ruling 3); every bundle built so far holds the same words, the line is `asWritten` from the master (S13-29), and the runtime's park then needs no bundle read. A bundle whose passedOn changes, or Jay's own words for the park, take a minor version that widens the definition (open gap 103), never an edit.
217. **The reason is three labels, each a K-08 `safeId`** (the card: "a reason (a final refusal's label: from, fault, ids, labels only)"; the ruling's card 1, K-09 v1.13's `turnRefused {from, fault, ids}`). `from` and `fault` are open labels, not closed lists, because K-09 v1.13, which names them, freezes on its own card, not before this one (the card: "Depends on: nothing unmerged"), and the faults grow with each walk, commit and read lane (`actions/src/commit/walk/errors.ts` lists sixteen walk faults today); `ids` are the refusal's own labels (step ids, the flow letter, a row id), each once, possibly none. K-08 v1's `safeId` (letters, digits and `_ . : / # -`, never a run that reads as a number) is the form the runtime's `isLabel` checks (`runtime/src/trace/validate.ts`) and K-08's spans take, so no words, number or address can stand in a reason, and the runtime can pass K-09's answer across field for field. K-09 v1.13 keeps its three fields to that form, or a value there is refused here, never cut down.
218. **A reason before the third try, any reason on it, none for a crash** (the card: "parkTurn takes tries 1 to 3 and a reason"; the ruling's card 7: the crash park keeps today's meaning plus the line). Only a final refusal parks a turn before its third try, so tries 1 and 2 require the reason (`parkTurn.lineRequest`'s `if` and `then`). On the third try a final refusal names its reason and a crash names none: a turn that crashed twice and is refused as final on its third run is parked for the refusal, with its labels; one that crashes a third time is parked as v1 parks it, plus the line. Whether a refusal is final is the ruling's ruling 1, applied by the runtime (card 6); K-05 never checks a fault against a list.
219. **The row is a quick reply to the turn's own handle, about no booking, asking no question, decided on nothing** (`runtime/src/turn/park.ts`'s defaults; Q-15; RULES 4.3, 4.8). `parkedRowNew` pins `sendClass` `quick_reply` (a reply in the same turn, never held for text hours), `bookingId`, `openQuestionId` and `copyOfRowId` null, and `versionsRead` empty: limit 8 compares a row's versions at send (arch 06 line 313), and a row that went stale would have its plan cleared (item 4), leaving an unworded parked row that default 183 passes for good, so a park's row is decided on no versions and never goes stale. Its recipient is the handle the turn's texts came from (`recipientHandleId` the turn's `handleId`), on that handle's own way (`imessage` for a phone, `email` for an email handle), and its plan's and final parts' `row` and `channel` are the row's: code checks, as `writePlan`'s (db/repo's default 3), each a `TypeError` before any statement. A stopped number's row is dropped at send, as any row is (RULES 7.2).
220. **The team's record: K-37's row as db/repo writes it today, its summary built from the labels** (K-37 v1 rule 4: "plain descriptions, never a person's private details"; `db/repo/park.ts` defaults 1 to 3; the ruling's ruling 3: "all labels and ids"). Every park writes one `parked_turns` row, `open`, with `reply_due_at` 1 business day after `parked_at` in Rosie's team's zone (db/repo's default 2) and `person_told_at` null (its default 3; open gap 104). Its `error_summary` is, for a refusal, `Refused as final (<from>: <fault>) on try <tries> of 3; nothing was committed`, with `; ` and the ids joined by `, ` after the fault when there are ids (`park-turn-sam-refused-at-once`: `Refused as final (walk: stepNotInBundle; S1-04) on try 1 of 3; nothing was committed`), and for a crash db/repo's fixed `Crashed three times before the commit (try 3 of 3); nothing was committed`. Built from labels and the try alone, it can hold no value of the turn's texts or of the request's other fields. v1's park already writes this row (CQ-96); v1.23 states it in the contract, so the promise in the line always has the record behind it (the ruling's ruling 2).
221. **The row is planned and then checked in the park's own transaction, K-07's two moves** (item 59's form; K-07 v1's moves `(new) → planned` and `planned → checked`). The row is inserted as v1's park inserts it, `planned` at version 1, and its plan and final parts are written by an update that moves it to `checked` and raises its version to 2, as `writePlan` does, before the transaction ends: no reader sees it without final parts, so it never stops its recipient's later rows (item 3), and the claim walks and claims it as any checked row. `writePlan`'s opener mark (default 200) and `markPart`'s opt-out time (default 203) are not written: neither call runs, and the row carries neither line. A later `writePlan` on the row answers `alreadyPlanned`, and `unplannedRows` never lists it.
222. **One `turn.parked` entry, its `why` the reason's labels** (K-05 v1 `park-turn.json`'s form; K-08 v1's kind `turn.parked`). The request's entries are written as item 12 writes them; the park's one entry is `turn.parked` on `turns` and the turn's id, actor Rosie, zone null, nothing sealed, `newValues` `{"state": "parked", "tries": <tries>}`, and `why` `{"refused": {"from": ..., "fault": ..., "ids": [...]}}` for a refusal (the reason, the same labels) or v1's `{"outage": "third crash before the commit"}` for a crash. The `parked_turns` row and the line's row get no entry of their own, as v1's park writes none for its row; K-08 names no kind for them and no `why` key for either (open gap 106). The schema leaves an entry's `why` free (K-05 v1 `eventEntry`), so its labels are the producer's and the contract test's to check.
223. **A repeat answers the first park, whatever its body or form** (item 12; arch 10 line 508). A turn id already parked answers its first park and writes nothing: no second team record, row, entry or line. A park first written in v1's form and repeated in v1.23's answers the first, unworded row, and the line is never added after; the runtime sends the same request again (`runtime/src/turn/park.ts` `PARK_SENDS`), so this meets only a park that changed form between two sends of one turn, which no task builds.
224. **The line alone, even on a number's first text from Rosie** (open gap 105; RULES 1.11, 4.3; Q-30; arch 07 line 361). A turn refused at once, or crashed three times, on the first text a number sends gives a park row that is Rosie's first reply in that chat, which the opener, the opt-out line and the privacy-notice link would join (defaults 200 to 205). The schema pins `fixedLines` to passedOn alone, so they are not added: no source rules the park's first text, the ruling names the line alone, and widening the pinned parts later is a minor version, while narrowing them would not be. The next text Rosie sends that number reads `readFixedLineFacts` as today (no opener mark and no opt-out time, since the park wrote neither) and carries the three lines, one text late.

Settling it needs a K-04 change (a turnless outbox row, or a job's turn on the owner's handle), which only adding cannot make; it stays open. v1.13 (T-1277): K-04 v1.1 now holds an outbox row with no turn (its `job_id`), which `sendSignInCode` writes for a sign-in's code (item 59), but `fireClock` keeps v1's refusal (`refuse-fire-without-turn-writes-outbox`), so a firing with no turn still writes no outbox row and CQ-28 stays open (ADR-016 section 1).

29. **Closed by v1.22 (T-1474; DL-999 decision (e)):** K-04 core v1.2 (T-1472) adds `outbox_rows.step` and `line_no`, both or neither, migration 0039 (T-1473) adds them, `commitTurn`'s `outboxRowNew` takes `step` and `lineNo` (item 82) and `readOutboxRow` answers a row's own first (`from` `row`: item 83); db/repo builds both (T-1476) and the walk writes them (T-1479), and until those merge a row still reads as below.
