As first written: **The step and line a row carries are not stored** (K-04 v1 `outbox_rows` has neither: QUESTIONS.md CQ-151 (a)). v1.3 reads them from the question the row asks (default 48), so a row that asks no question answers `line` null and compose input and finalize refuse it. The walking skeleton's rows (S1-01 and S1-02) each ask a question, so they are not blocked; S1-04's notices, a confirmation and every walked row that asks nothing are. **Needs:** a **K-04 v1.1** core table list adding `outbox_rows.step` (text, null: a K-01 stepId) and `outbox_rows.line_no` (integer, null, with a CHECK `line_no is null or line_no >= 0`); a **migration task** for the database lane after it (two nullable columns and the CHECK, expand only, safe to ship dark); a **K-05 minor version** in which `commitTurn`'s `outboxRowNew` takes both (optional) and `readOutboxRow` reads them first (`line.from` `row`); and the commit's walk (C-087, after T-1139) writing them.
30. **What the committed turn knows about a row beyond its line is not stored** (K-04 v1): the slots the message states besides its master line's (`ComposeRow.factSlots`) and what it is about (`SituationFacts.about`: a death report, a problem, money or consent: arch 07 line 357). The caller gives none, so the composer states only the master line's slots, and the tone tag reads only the safety outcomes and the sensitive switch. Proposed: the K-04 minor version of gap 29 also adds `outbox_rows.fact_slots` and `outbox_rows.about` (text arrays, null), written by the walk and read by the same K-05 minor version. **Still open after v1.22:** K-04 core v1.2 holds neither (DL-999 decision (e); K-04 core README open gaps), so v1.22, which closes gap 29, reads neither; a later K-04 and K-05 minor version adds them when the slot filler's or the tone tag's card asks. Also not in v1.3's slot source, so each slot that reads one stays unfilled and the Values check refuses a message that needs it (arch 07 line 409): a visit's time, place, code and zone (K-30; default 34), money (K-32), links (K-29's chat links, K-30's secure links) and a relayed quote. The sensitive switch (RULES 4.6; S13-22), a plug-in switch per kind of work and flow (K-23 `company_kinds.pf_switches_a` and `pf_switches_b`), is **read by v1.6 for the row's business** (item 45: true when any of its kinds of work lists it in either flow, false when none does, null, which the caller reads as on, when it has none), and that fills a row about no booking, which shows no visit's service (before v1.6 the turn face read it as on for every row: DL-326 note b). A booking's own switch, reached from a row only through its booking's service and that service's kind of work, is still not read: a row about a booking is filled as if the switch were on (each slot in its sensitive form, the stricter), whatever the business's switch. A later minor version reads each from its frozen home when the slot filler's card (C-088) asks.
31. **A released hold that still blocks booking** (A3-26: `blocks_booking` after an at-once release) is not open, so `readOpenHolds` does not return it, and the commit's `OpenHolds` has no place for it. The gate's booking check (C-080) reads it on its own when its card asks, in a later minor version.
32. **The hold read and the commit are two transactions:** `commitTurn` runs as `rosie_job` in its own transaction (T-306; STATE note 59), so a hold committed between `readOpenHolds` and `commitTurn` binds the next turn, not this commit. The actor runs one turn at a time per handle (arch 10 line 502), so only a write outside the turn (an arrival record: K-09 v1.1) can land in that window. Proposed: `commitTurn` reads the handle's open holds again in its own transaction and refuses a routine write while one is open, in a later K-05 minor version, as `renewClaim` reads the stop flag (item 32). New question for QUESTIONS.md.
33. **The conversation** (`ComposeRow.conversation`: the reader's last 6 messages, arch 07 line 397) is not read: `messages.words` is sealed (CQ-54), and, as for the words a row answers (default 47), no Tool API path opens sealed words. The caller gives none (K-02's `recentMessages` is empty too: gap 20). Proposed: the actor hands them in, opened as it opens the turn's texts, in a later K-09 minor version; or the context bundle (C-114) reads them on the runtime side.
34. **How Rosie addresses the reader and how the reader is reached** (K-03 `audience.name`, `way`, `handlingLine`): v1.3 returns the stored name (`persons.name`; K-09's `compose-input-s1-02.json` addresses "Sam Lee" as "Sam") and the handle's kind. K-28's reach preference (texts only, calls only) is not read, so a texts-only or email-only customer's handling line (S13-19) cannot be chosen yet; the skeleton's rows go to an owner by text. New question for QUESTIONS.md: the rule that turns a stored name into the name Rosie uses (C-090), and which minor version reads K-28's reach.
35. **A row's calendar file** (`FinalizeRow.calendarFile`: new, move or cancel on a confirmation, a move or a call-off: arch 07 line 383) needs K-30's visit and the walk's message, and is not read. A row about no booking carries none.
36. **The row's other fixed lines** (`FixedLineFacts.lines`: the code text, the safety ask a tense unknown adds, checked terms, the handling line) have no rule yet (C-089's card). v1.3 gives their database inputs in item 35 (the answered texts' outcomes and tenses, the booking's private flag, the recipient's roles). Proposed default for QUESTIONS.md: until that rule is carded, the turn face finalizes only rows none of them applies to, refusing (failing closed) a row that answers a text whose outcome is not No or whose tense is unknown, a row about a private booking, and a row to the owner or staff about a booking. The skeleton's rows pass.
37. **Closed by v1.21 (T-1508; DL-1033):** `writePlan` writes the mark naming its row in its own transaction (default 200), its `sent_at` the turn's commit time (default 201), for either opener id (default 204), and `readFixedLineFacts` reads past a mark whose row never went (default 202); T-1513 builds it. As written for v1.3: **Who writes the opener mark** (`openers_sent`): no task on the texting path writes it yet (`db/repo/threads.ts` `markOpenerSent` exists). Until one does, every row to a handle about a business reads `textedAboutBusiness` false and carries the opener and the privacy-notice link: the safe side (RULES 1.11, 4.3), at the cost of repeating them. Proposed: the finalize call that inserts the opener writes the mark naming its row in the same transaction as `writePlan` (C-089 with C-093), so that row still carries it if finalized again (default 50).
38. **A fixed-line row of another send class** (the guard's STOP, START and HELP replies; the outage lines: K-09 `sendFixedLine`) is not marked in K-04 v1, so `readOutboxRow` cannot refuse it by name. Until a K-04 minor version stores the line's id (`outbox_rows.fixed_line_id`, text, null) and a migration adds it, its writer gives it its plan and final parts (`writePlan`) before anything composes it, so compose input answers `alreadyPlanned`; the read refuses only the safety class (default 49).
39. **A question's clock row is found by its question** (`clock_rows.open_question_id`, kind `question`), as `armClock` finds it (item 5), and K-04 v1 lists no index on `clock_rows.open_question_id`. **Needs:** a K-04 minor version listing `clock_rows_question_idx` on `(open_question_id) where open_question_id is not null`, and a migration task that builds it concurrently (expand only), before clock rows grow past what a scan reads inside a hop. v1.10's `readQuestionFacts` finds a question's never-delivered row the same way (item 49), inside the receipt's 1 s, so the index serves both.
40. **A text's arrival time** (`SafetyReader.arrivedAt`: its K-06 v1 `receivedAt`) has no K-04 home: `messages` keeps the sender's `sent_at`, and `turns.event_ids` orders texts within one turn only (T-318's handoff 1). v1.4 gives it no call. Until T-1155 freezes a K-04 home, its migration and its K-05 read, the reader answers null (T-1141), and a release that needs the arrival order answers `stillDanger` (`release.ts` default 13): the hold stays, failing closed.
41. **Two live owner roles at one business:** K-04 v1 does not refuse them (`person_roles_one_live_role` is per person), while the sources give one account owner (S2-02). `readBusinessOwner` refuses them (`moreThanOneOwner`, default 58), so a safety line to the owner is not built and the record fails closed. **Needs:** a **K-04 minor version** listing a unique index on `person_roles (business_id) where role = 'owner' and ended_at is null`, and a **migration task** that builds it concurrently (expand only), once the hand-over (S11-100) is built to end the old owner's role in the same transaction that starts the new one. New question for QUESTIONS.md.
42. **A text whose record another scope holds:** the record is not seen (default 57), so the service writes it again and the commit's insert meets `safety_outcomes_event_key` (`constraint`): nothing is written, and the turn, tried again, is parked (arch 11 line 576), where Rosie's team sees it. K-09's arrival record and its batch turn name the same business (`j3-safety-arrival-emergency.json`, `j3-safety-arrival-batch-turn.json`), so that path does not meet it; a text recorded with no business and recorded again by a turn under a business would. Proposed: the turns that record a text keep the business of its first record, in a later K-09 minor version, when a card meets it. New question for QUESTIONS.md.
43. **The owner's sign-in mobile** (FS row B12; K-22 v1 `accounts.mobile`, proven by the owner's own text) is not read: among several phone handles `readBusinessOwner` takes the lowest id (default 59), each of them the owner's own. A later minor version reads K-22's `accounts.mobile` to pick the sign-in mobile, when a card asks.
44. **A row to someone with no role, outside section 1, has no reader** (item 44: "every other row with no role is still refused", DL-364): a new customer's replies in section 3 before any commit writes their customer role, a booking's question to someone who holds no role at the business (`row-reader-a5-05-tomas-no-role`), and section 2's lines to a team member before their staff role is written. Each is refused (`noReader`), failing closed, as under v1.3. **Needs:** each journey's lane writes the role a row is read by in the commit that writes the row, or asks a later K-05 minor version for a reading of its own, as v1.5 gives section 1 (journey 2's and journey 3's lanes). New question for QUESTIONS.md.
45. **Text hours for a section 1 row to a person with no role:** `sendState` reads the audience as default 31 gives it, `customer` for a recipient with no role (9am, the stricter), while compose input and finalize read the row as its line's audience (the owner's 8am: RULES 4.3). Turn 1's row is a quick reply, which goes at any hour (RULES 4.3; Q-15), so the skeleton does not meet it. Proposed: keep the stricter until journey 1's lane asks; a later minor version may give `sendState` item 44's reading. New question for QUESTIONS.md.
46. **Closed by v1.20 (T-1187), by the second of its two needs:** item 80 refuses the sign-up reading at a business that has a live owner role, read through v1.4's `readBusinessOwner` at the row's read; the first need, a design record on the business a sign-up turn runs under, was not taken (section "v1.20", "Why v1.11's party rule is not enough"). What it leaves is open gaps 94 to 96. As written for v1.5: **The business a sign-up row is read under:** item 44 reads a section 1 row to a person with no role as its line's audience at the row's business, the turn's own (K-09 v1.5 default 130: the skeleton runs under test owner 1's seeded business, which no one owns yet; K-09 v1.5 open gap 119: before any business head exists, journey 1 decides). Were a sign-up turn run under a business someone else owns, limit 7 would give the line's audience that business's fields on the row (item 35's `slotSource`: its head and the question the row asks). The sign-up door is offered only to a handle with no target (CQ-178), and S1-02's line carries no slot fact, so turn 1 shows nothing of the business but what its fixed lines carry. **Needs:** journey 1's lane (gap 119) runs a sign-up turn under the business it creates, or under none, never under one with a live owner role; or a later minor version refuses the reading at a business that has one (v1.4 `readBusinessOwner`). New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the security reviewer.
47. **A sign-up row before S1-04 reads the switch as `null`** (item 45; DL-391): a business that holds no kinds of work yet has nothing to read the switch from, so the caller reads `null` as on and fills the row in its sensitive form. The walking skeleton's turn 1 is such a row: D-017's seed (T-1148's `db/seeds/skeleton.ts`) writes the barbershop's head, Sam, his handle and their link, and no kind of work (S1-04 has not run, and the demo shops' seeds, T-200's and T-213's, use ids of their own), so turn 1's S1-02 row reads `null` (`read-outbox-row-s1-02-no-kinds`) and is filled in its sensitive form (gentle, the display name alone), while K-09 v1.6's `compose-input-s1-02-v1.6.json` and T-1175's recording 4 have it routine. It reads `false`, as they have it, only once the business holds kinds of work with no switch on (`read-outbox-row-s1-02-not-sensitive`). **Needs** one of, by the lead's ruling: the skeleton's seed gives the barbershop K-23's barber kind of work (the database engineer: one `companies` row and one `company_kinds` row in `db/seeds/skeleton.ts`, as K-23's `seed-company-two-kinds.json` holds them; the guard reads no kinds of work, so JOIN stays a first text); or K-09's S1-02 fixtures and recording 4 take the sensitive form (a K-09 minor version and a new recording); or the security reviewer rules that a section 1 row before S1-04 reads the switch as off, which this version does not do (default 67). New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the security reviewer.
48. **A number's chat thread outside S1-01:** `sendState`'s chat (item 28) and the dispatcher's chat target (`runtime/src/dispatch`, `RecipientDirectory.chatFor`) need the handle's chat thread with Linq's chat id (K-04 `threads`, kind chat, `linq_chat_id`), which only S1-01's commit writes (K-09 v1.5 default 135). A customer's first text writes none yet, so a reply to it would end `noChat` at the dispatcher, its chat open or not. v1.7 writes the phone row's flags, never the chat id. **Needs:** the journey lanes give a first text's commit the thread write, as S1-01's has it (a K-09 minor version). New question for QUESTIONS.md. From v1.10 the same gap reaches a reply's receipt: `readChatHandle` finds no thread for such a chat (item 51; `read-chat-handle-unknown`), so the gateway answers 503 until Linq stops retrying, and the reply's delivery is never marked nor its question's clock armed, as for a question never delivered (arch 02 line 127: "Never delivered: no clock runs").
49. **A chat whose service changes** (a person who moves from an iPhone to an Android and keeps the number): `chat_way` keeps the opening text's service (default 72). Replies follow their own text's service and other rows Linq's capability check (arch 07 line 365), but a reader of `chat_way` alone (T-307's `textChannel`, `actions/src/domain/messages-consent`) picks the old way. **Needs:** a rule for when the stored way follows Linq's reports, in a later minor version. New question for QUESTIONS.md.
50. **Texts while PostgreSQL is down:** like the key (item 29), the call needs PostgreSQL at receipt. While PostgreSQL does not answer, the gateway answers 503 and Linq retries for 30 minutes (arch 02 line 109), and the actor's queue (arch 11 line 566) holds the texts received before. Were texts ever stored while PostgreSQL is down (a key served from a copy), the chat open must go with each text, on the actor's write-on-return queue ahead of that text's safety and stop records (`runtime/src/actor/copies`), never dropped. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the runtime engineer.
51. **Two first texts of one number recorded in parallel:** the one whose call takes the row first sets `chat_open_at`, though the other may have been received a second earlier. Kept: the time is when the chat opened, to the second, and the actor orders the texts by their own `receivedAt` (K-06 default 2).
52. **A fresh yes's booking, new or old** (RULES 7.5 and S13-32 line 7242: "a fresh yes on a new booking"; K-04 `phones.stopped`: "a new booking's texts after its fresh yes from this phone"; T-1099's security note a): v1.8 names the booking a yes covers, not when the booking was made, which no frozen part stores, so limit 8 lifts a stop for an own yes on a booking made before the stop as for a new one. Nothing goes on it while the stop stands (default 83). **Needs**, if a fresh yes is ever to send before START (CQ-74 decided the other way): a **K-04 minor version** adding `bookings.created_at` (timestamptz, null for rows made before it, written by the commit that inserts the head), a **migration task** (one nullable column, expand only, safe to ship dark), and a **K-05 minor version** in which `textsConsent` says whether its booking was made after the phone's `stop_at`; or C-102's writer refuses an own yes for a stopped mobile on a booking made before the stop, as T-307's `yes()` already refuses every other yes for it (`stoppedNumber`). New question for QUESTIONS.md, with CQ-74.
53. **A contact's own yes recorded outside a turn** (default 79): K-29 v1's row shows who sent a yes only through the turn that wrote it, or, for a START, by its having no business. Every frozen writer records a yes by text in a turn (K-09: a staff member's ready text rides as a core action; a read-back's yes is bound to its turn) or it is a START, so none meets this today, and a seed's own yes with no turn reads false, the safe side. **Needs**, if a writer ever records a contact's own yes outside a turn (a queue written on return, an import): a **K-29 minor version** adding `consents.from_handle_id` (text, null: the handle the word came from, null for a page, an AI or a job), a **migration task** (one nullable column and its foreign key to `handles`, expand only), and the K-05 minor version that reads it first. New question for QUESTIONS.md.
54. **The order receipts were recorded in is not stored** (default 85): K-04 v1 keeps each part try's `delivered_at`, not which receipt said `firstDelivery`, so a repeat (item 48) names the row's first delivery by the receipts' own times. Where a receipt was recorded after a later-timed one (the poll sent after its 10 s wait while the words' receipt was late: K-07 rule 4; a late receipt of an earlier try after a 4006: K-07 rule 2), the later-timed delivery said `firstDelivery`, its repeat says `firstOfRow` false, and if the crash lost its arming, that arming stays lost: the question takes the never-delivered path, as before v1.9 (STATE note 37). It needs a crash in the milliseconds between `markPart`'s commit and `armClock`, after receipts recorded out of their own order. **Needs**, if the arming must survive that too: a **K-04 minor version** adding `outbox_rows.first_delivered_key` (text, null: the part key whose delivery the row recorded first, written once by the mark that says `firstDelivery`), a **migration task** (one nullable column, expand only, safe to ship dark), and a **K-05 minor version** in which a repeat reads it before the times. New question for QUESTIONS.md, with CQ-201.
55. **The handle's party for a turn is not named** (`runtime/src/turn/types.ts` `TurnDirectory.partyOf`: the handle's K-04 id, the business the turn's calls run under, the person, and the zone its texts' dates resolve in; T-557's handoff: "stateReads.turn ... has no join card yet"; the D-017 path audit's R3; T-1240's card, its first read). Three of its fields have one reading in the stored rows: the handle by its address (`handles_address_key`; none is not known, so no turn runs), the person when the handle has exactly one (`person_handles`; none or several: null, K-09 v1 `caller.personId`'s "when known"), and the zone, the business's `time_zone`. **The business has two readings, and no source picks one.** K-09 v1 says only "The business the call runs under ... Null only where no business exists yet" (`caller.businessId`), and nothing says which of a handle's businesses a turn runs under, or which business a handle whose people hold no role has:
    - **Reading A, live roles only:** the business at which the handle's people hold live roles (`person_roles`, `ended_at` null) when there is exactly one; none gives null, the runtime side, as a sign-up before S1-09 writes a business. Under it test owner 1's JOIN, whose person holds no role (T-1148's seed), runs with no business, so the frontier's sign-up door has no target (K-02 v1 `target.businessId` is required; K-02 v1.1 default 9: "the turn's business as its target"), and ADR-013's default 130 and K-09 v1.5's turn 1 fixtures, which run it under `biz-lees-barbershop`, no longer describe the staging turn: the seed or ADR-013 would change with it.
    - **Reading B, default 130's seeded business, read from K-23:** as A, but a handle whose people hold no live role takes the business of the K-23 company its one person owns (`companies.owner_person_id`, `business_id` not null; T-1148's seed writes `company-lees-barbershop` for Sam), so JOIN runs under `biz-lees-barbershop`, as default 130 and the turn 1 fixtures say. It gives a turn a business at which its sender holds no role: an owner whose role ended at a hand-over (S11-100) while the company row still names them would run turns there, which open gap 46's condition ("never under one with a live owner role") would then have to refuse; and one login may own two companies (K-23 README: "a second company a second business on the same login"), which no source orders.

    Neither reading orders several businesses: K-04's `seed-person-roles.json` gives Sam three live owner roles and Alex two customer roles, and arch 03 line 159 labels other bookings' choices by business without naming the turn's own (K-09 v1.5 open gap 119: journey 1 decides). Nor does either give the zone of a turn with no business, which the port never takes null (UTC, as K-08 gives a row with no business, is the nearest default). A third source, staging's alone, is K-21 v1's test roster, which names tp-01's business (QUESTIONS.md CQ-206). **Needs:** the lead's ruling between A and B, or the roster on staging, with the security reviewer for B; a rule for several businesses and for the zone with none; then a K-05 minor version names the read, with no new column under either reading (its rows: `handles`, `person_handles`, `person_roles`, `businesses`, and under B `companies`, all read as `rosie_job` on the runtime side, the guard's side: K-04 v1 rule 4). Until then the turn's directory stays closed on staging and no turn runs there. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the lead. **Closed by v1.11 (T-1249; CQ-220; DL-499):** item 52 names the read and rules its business: the one scope the handle's people's live roles and companies name, live roles and companies together (default 98); a company's business only while no other person holds its live owner role and none of the handle's people left it (default 99); `businessUndecided`, never a business picked and never no business in its place, for several scopes or a company the rows contradict (defaults 100 and 102); the zone null with no business, read as UTC (default 101). Reading A alone would leave test owner 1, and every owner journey 1 signs up, with no business (gap 58); reading B alone would run a turn for an ex-owner after S11-100. What is left is gaps 56 to 61.

56. **A handle that acts for several businesses runs no turn** (`severalBusinesses`: default 98). K-04's seed holds such people (Sam's three owner roles, Alex's two customer roles), and so will every customer of two businesses and every owner of two. The sources pick the business from the message (S13-27 rule 29: "Read the role from what the message is about. When two roles fit, ask which one"; arch 02 line 115; arch 03 line 159: "Questions about other bookings add only their options, labeled by business, date and time"), which no read before the turn holds, while K-09 v1 runs every Tool API call of a turn under one business (`caller.businessId`; arch 10 line 546). **Needs:** a design for a turn across its handle's businesses (the journey lanes; K-09 and K-02 minor versions): the frontier reads each business's targets under that business's scope and labels them by business, decide picks by content and asks one lettered "which?" on a tie (S13-12), and the commit writes per write (item 1); or a turn that asks "which business?" first, as S11-15 does. Until then such a handle's texts run no turn, and gap 57's card decides what its person hears. No staging test phone meets it: tp-01 is test owner 1, and tp-02 to tp-05 hold one customer role each at the barber demo shop (T-202's `db/seeds/staging-core.ts`). New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the lead and the CTO.
57. **What an undecided handle's texts get** (default 102). Until a runtime card takes `businessUndecided`, the binding gives the turn no party, and both a run of the turn and its park end at the missing party (`runtime/src/turn/turn.ts` and `runtime/src/turn/park.ts`: `unknownHandle`), so the texts wait and nothing is written: a STOP from such a number is not recorded (RULES 7.2: "It stops texts from the number it answers, at once"), and the person never hears that nothing changed (arch 11 line 576). That fails closed for every business, but not for the stop. **Needs:** a runtime card (C-046 the actor's turn, C-051 the guard, C-180 the park) that, on `businessUndecided`, runs the guard's code path for a STOP, START or HELP with business null (K-09 v1 `caller.businessId`: "or for a call that covers every business (a STOP: RULES 7.2)") and parks every other run at once for Rosie's team, its one row and entry with business null, from the error's handle and person; before any handle that meets gap 56 or default 99 texts Rosie. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the runtime engineer, with the security reviewer.
58. **No step writes the owner's role** (default 98). K-09's table of journeys 1 and 2 (README, `saveSetupStep`) writes `companies` (S1-04) and `businesses` (S1-09), and its one `person_roles` write is S2-02's staff, so an owner who signs up holds no live owner role: the party read finds the business by the company, while v1.4's `readBusinessOwner` reads the owner from the live owner role (default 58) and answers none (`business-owner-none`), so a safety line to the owner finds no one, and nothing that reads roles sees the owner. **Needs:** journey 1's lane writes the owner's live owner role in the commit that writes the business head (S1-09) or takes it live (S1-35), or the architect rules the company as the owner's record for every reader; the party read then names the business by the role, and the company stays its reading only between S1-04's company and that commit. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the lead (journey 1).
59. **A hand-over or a removal leaves the company naming the old owner** (default 99). S11-100's writer (journey 11, not built) ends the old owner's access and starts the new owner's role (gap 41), and nothing frozen says it moves `companies.owner_person_id`; a removal on papers (S13-31: "Or by Rosie's team on papers, like a death notice, a court order or a signed sale"; "Removing an owner is a hand-over (S11-100)"; RULES 9.5) of an owner who never held a role (gap 58) ends nothing the rows show. The party read fails the first two closed (`anotherOwner`, `roleEnded`), but a removed owner with no role, whose business has no other owner, is still given the business by the company. **Needs:** journey 11's lane moves the company to the new owner in the hand-over's commit (a company is "one tax number, one Stripe, one bill", and the new owner "links their own Stripe": S11-100), and a removal records the end of the owner's access in a row this read reads (with gap 58, the owner's role, ended), so no company keeps naming them. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the lead (journey 11), with the security reviewer.
60. **An erased person's link** (gap 28) reaches this read: until C-156's erasure removes an erased person's `person_handles` link (gap 28's proposal), the erased person's live roles and companies still name their business for the number, with `personId` null once `handleSealingKey` has given the number a fresh person (default 97), as they still show in the guard's targets. **Needs:** C-156 follows gap 28's proposal, or ends the erased person's roles, before any key is erased. Kept with gap 28's question.
61. **The zone of a turn with no business is UTC** (default 101). S13-03 and RULES 4.3 name the person's own zone, which no frozen table stores, so a newcomer's or a sign-up's texts read their dates in UTC, and a "tomorrow" sent near midnight UTC names another day than the sender's (read back before it changes anything: RULES 4.2). **Needs:** a person's zone in a frozen part (a K-04 or K-22 minor version), from the person's own answer, if sign-up turns before S1-09 must read dates in the sender's zone. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the lead (journey 1).
62. **Where the roster's testers who hold no team row are kept** (default 103; the card's first read, reported open as DL-498's lead note asks). K-21 v1 default 2 makes the roster's testers Rosie's team on staging, read from K-36's rows, while T-1250's card (sign-in's record, in review) rules "no team-page row for a human tester (build prompt 9.4)" and takes "the tester's standing from T-1248's source"; no frozen part lists testers any other way (K-21 open gap 12), and a list in a new place is a decision this card does not hold. The readings that fit: (a) K-21 default 2 as frozen: a human tester holds a staging team row whose one reach is a roster test phone or the test console, never their own phone or inbox, so build prompt 9.4 holds and item 53 is complete as frozen; (b) the testers' K-04 person ids, opaque ids only, in staging's roster config (default 106's module), handed to a later minor version's `readTesterStanding` request; (c) one staging-only K-04 `release_flags` row (scope `global`) whose `value` lists them, seeded beside the roster, as K-21 default 16 keeps a staging-only override there, with no column; (d) a K-22 or K-36 minor version that marks a tester, with its migration. Until ruled, a human tester with no team row answers `notTester`, failing closed; nothing waits on it before sign-in's server side serves K-21's sign-in calls (T-1250; CQ-214 (b)), and the check tester (default 105) is not affected. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the lead, with the security reviewer and the CTO (CQ-214's owner). **Closed by v1.15 (T-1295; the lead's ruling DL-535, reading (d)):** K-22 v1.3's `staging_testers` lists staging's human testers apart from Rosie's team, one row per listing that the testers' seed writes at run time (T-1281), with no business and no team row, read by the runtime side and never by the Tool API; items 68 and 69 read a live listing beside the live team row (defaults 136 to 139), and K-21 v1 default 2's line now says so. Reading (a) contradicts ADR-016 section 5, (b) cannot hold run-time seeded person ids in a code module, and (c) puts people in a flags row (DL-535). What is left is gap 77 and K-22 v1.3's two open gaps.
63. **Rosie's number on staging** (default 109): no staging config value holds it (STATE note 18: "a ROSIE_NUMBER variable (not a secret) is a new release-engineer task"), so the page shows no chat link until one does. **Needs:** that release-engineer task; the binding then builds K-09's form. New question for QUESTIONS.md, with STATE note 18.
64. **The check tester stands as a team member** (default 105): on staging it may also use what K-21 gives Rosie's team there (the team console's reads and writes), and staging's pages reach it as emails to the double. Staging holds test data only (CQ-218), its token is a key kept by name, and the seed is staging's alone. A standing narrower than the team's needs open gap 62's (b) to (d). It needs an owner role whose person has no `person_handles` row: an owner whose handle link was removed (gaps 28 and 60), or a hand-over that starts the new owner's role before their phone is linked. **Needs:** a later minor version in which a read says whether a live owner role exists apart from its handle (`readOutboxRow`'s answer, read in its own transaction under the row's business, which would also save the second read; or a field on `readBusinessOwner`'s answer), with its db/repo task, and the mapping then takes the state from it. What stands between is item 52 (only a person on record for the business runs a turn under it) and item 44's own conditions. **Needs:** journey 1's lane writes the owner's live owner role (gap 58: with the business head at S1-09, or at S1-35) before a section 1 line that carries slot facts is sent to a recipient who is not the turn's sender (S1-77's line to the owner a helper names); from then the signer is read by their role and any other person with no role is refused. 96. **The rule is not live until the port is built** (item 80): the turn face maps by v1.5 until T-1497 merges, so nothing refuses the sign-up reading at a business with a live owner yet. **Needs:** T-1497 merged and deployed before a section 1 line that carries slot facts is sent to a reader with no role (T-635's read-back at S1-09 is the first: the S1-04 design ruling's card 28). The rows the bundle with S1-08 and S1-09 sends release no slot fact (that ruling's decision (g)). Kept with the lead.
97. **No cap on `ask_count` in the database** (default 197). K-09 v1.12's cap at 2 (its default 194) is held by its read of the count and K-05's compare (default 192); a caller that raised without reading the count, or a later caller with another rule, could raise past 2, and nothing in K-04 refuses it (0009 checks `ask_count >= 1` alone). For the security reviewer, with the database engineer: should K-04 add a CHECK, or K-05 refuse a raise past a bound? Either binds every later re-ask rule (K-09 v1.12 open gap 175; arch 09 line 490 allows a further ask "after answering a question back"). Default in use: none; the caller's cap, compared and set. New question for QUESTIONS.md.
98. **An SMS-way chat's opt-out line keeps repeating** (default 203). On SMS the delivery receipt is Linq's sent receipt, which the receipt door refuses with `smsSentReceiptNoDoor` (`runtime/src/wiring/root.ts` lines 1189 to 1192; DL-997), so `markPart` never marks such a part `delivered` and `optout_line_delivered_at` stays null for a number whose chat goes by SMS (0004 allows the chat way `sms`). **Needs:** a door for SMS sent receipts (the runtime engineer's), after which default 203 applies unchanged. Default in use: the line goes on every text there, the safe side (RULES 4.3). Not fixed here. New question for QUESTIONS.md.
99. **The opener when finalizes race or come out of order** (defaults 200 and 202). A row's fixed-line facts and its `writePlan` are two transactions (`runtime/src/wiring/staging/tool-api-rows.ts` lines 118 to 120), so two rows to one handle and business finalized at once (rows of one turn, since the turn lease runs a handle's turns one at a time) can both read no mark and both carry the opener, the mark naming the one planned first; and a later row planned before an earlier one is the one the mark names, so the earlier row, read after it, reads "texted before" and, sent first in commit order, goes with no opener. The first errs to the safe side (an opener twice); the second can leave a handle's first text without it (RULES 1.11), and needs a finalize-order inversion. **Needs,** if T-1513's or T-1514's proofs meet it: a ruling whether `writePlan` refuses a second opener row, or the read weighs commit order against the mark. Default in use: as written. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the architect with the runtime engineer. **The second case is ruled by default 215** (T-1521, after the security reviewer's new task on T-1513: DL-1083): the read weighs commit order against the mark, so a mark naming a row committed after this one is read as default 202's fallback, and the earlier row, sent first, carries the opener and the privacy-notice link, while the later row, planned first, carries them too (the first case's safe side). The first case stays as written.
100. **A held or sending row counts before it goes** (defaults 200, 202 and 204; the security reviewer's round 1 note a, carded in round 2: DL-1045). Default 202 leaves out only a row that ended without going (`withdrawn`, `failed`, `dropped_stopped`, `superseded`), and the mark names a row at `writePlan`, before it is sent (default 200), so an earlier opener row still `held`, `waiting_chat`, `checked` or `sending` counts as "texted before", in the fallback and in the no-business branch alike. Two ways that leaves a business's first text to a handle without the opener: the claim walk passes a row held for text hours or waiting for its chat and claims the rows behind it (`db/repo/outbox.ts` lines 52 to 55), so a later row, a quick reply among them, finalized without the opener, can go before the earlier row that carries it; and an earlier row counted while held or sending can still end without going after a later row read it, which then goes first without the opener. Either breaks arch 07 line 361's per-business rule ("on Rosie's first text to a handle about one business (DD), the opener ... and the privacy-notice link"). RULES 1.11 and 4.3 hold, since a thread's first text is a reply (the security reviewer's note a). **Needs:** a ruling whether "texted before" counts only an earlier row a part of which has gone (`sent` or `delivered`, as v1.2 default 41 reads a question's send time), at the cost of an opener twice when two rows are finalized before either goes (open gap 99's first case), or whether the later row waits for the earlier one. Default in use: as written (an opener row counts from its plan until it ends without going). New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the architect with the runtime engineer.
101. **A clock firing's rows carry no step or line** (default 211). `fireClock.request` keeps v1.1's `writes`, so a firing that writes an outbox row asking no question (a safe default's notice, when one is built) writes it with neither column, and its row reads `line` null and is refused at compose input and finalize, as every such row was before v1.22. The clock firing (C-054) is bound by none of the composition root's ports (`runtime/src/wiring/ports.ts`; open gap 80), so no firing writes such a row today. **Needs:** a later minor version gives `fireClock.request`'s writes v1.22's `outboxRowNew`, in the task of the first firing that writes a row asking no question, with its db/repo task (`db/repo/clocks.ts`, which writes a firing's rows itself). New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the lead with the runtime engineer.
102. **A row whose own step differs from the step of the question it asks** (default 208; K-04 core v1.2 gap 2): the read answers the row's pair and the commit refuses nothing, so a walk fault that named a question row by another step would compose that step's line beside a question asked at its own step, and the reply would be read against the question while the words came from the other step. The walk names a question row by its question's step (T-1479), and a question opened in the same commit could be compared in the request alone. **Needs:** a ruling whether `commitTurn` (or db/repo's `checkRequest`) refuses a row whose `openQuestionId` names a question opened in the same request at another step, and whether a row asking an earlier turn's question is compared at all (a read the commit does not make today). Default in use: not compared; the row's own pair answers. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the architect with the action-layer engineer (T-1479).
103. **Jay's words for the park** (the ruling's first need; default 216). No fixed line says that nothing changed (arch 11 line 576: "the person hears once that nothing changed and that Rosie's team will reply within 1 business day"), so the park sends passedOn, which promises the reply and says nothing of a change. **Needs:** Jay's words (a new K-01 fixed line, or passedOn kept), then a minor version that lets `parkedLineParts` take them, with the runtime's park (cards 6 and 7). Default in use: passedOn, word for word. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for Jay through the lead.
104. **When the person counts as told** (K-37 v1 `parked_turns.person_told_at`: "Set once; null until the line has gone"; db/repo's default 3; default 220). The park now writes the line, but no call sets `person_told_at` when its row goes: `markPart` (item 15) knows nothing of `parked_turns`. Until one does, the team console reads the row's own state in `outbox_rows` (by the turn's id) beside `person_told_at` null. **Needs:** a ruling whether `markPart`, on the first accepted or delivered part of a parked turn's row, sets `person_told_at` once in the same transaction, as default 203 sets a phone's opt-out time, with its minor version and db/repo task. Default in use: null. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the architect with the database engineer.
105. **The first-text lines on a park's row** (default 224). **Needs:** a ruling whether a park's row that is a number's first text from Rosie also carries the opener (or `signUpOpener`), the opt-out line and the privacy-notice link (RULES 1.11, 4.3; Q-30), read through `readFixedLineFacts` as finalize reads them, which would widen `parkedLineParts` to those fixed lines beside passedOn in a minor version and have the runtime's park read the facts. Default in use: the line alone, the three lines going with the next text. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the architect with the security reviewer.
106. **K-07's and K-08's names for the park's writes** (defaults 221 and 222). K-07 v1's move table names `commitTurn` for `(new) → planned` and `writePlan` for `planned → checked`, while v1's `parkTurn` and v1.13's `sendSignInCode` already make the first move and v1.13's and v1.23's the second; K-08 v1's `why` has keys for a step, a choice, a limit, a safe default, an outage path and a provider event, none for a refusal (`refused`) or v1's crash (`outage`), and no kind names a `parked_turns` row. **Needs:** a K-07 minor version naming the other calls that make the two moves, and a K-08 minor version with a `why` key for a park and, if the team's record is to have its own entry, its kind. Default in use: the moves and the entry as defaults 221 and 222 write them. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the architect.

Why: the security reviewer's new task on T-1513 (DL-1083, DL-1084): a turn composes its rows in parallel (arch 07 line 397), so the row committed later can be planned first and take the mark, and under default 202 alone the earlier row, sent first by commit order (arch 02 line 125), went with no opener and no privacy-notice link, against RULES 1.11 and 4.3. Item 36 and defaults 50 and 202 point to it; open gap 99's second case is ruled, its first stays as written.
